  \item $\mathcal{X}$ satisfies the Axiom of Infinity-Choice ($\mathrm{AC}_\infty$) if every effective epimorphism $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ (of arbitrary homotopy type $X$) onto a $0$-truncated codomain $Y$ admits a section.

\end{itemize}

\end{definition}

Because every $n$-truncated morphism is $(n+1)$-truncated, full infinity-choice $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$ is strictly the \emph{strongest} principle in the ladder while set-level choice $\mathrm{AC}_0$ is the \emph{weakest}, forming a strictly descending deductive chain with shrinking satisfaction classes:
\[
  \mathrm{AC}_\infty \implies \cdots \implies \mathrm{AC}_{n+1} \implies \mathrm{AC}_n \implies \cdots \implies \mathrm{AC}_0,
\]
\[
  \mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC}_\infty) \subsetneq \cdots \subsetneq \mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC}_{n+1}) \subsetneq \mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC}_n) \subsetneq \cdots \subsetneq \mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC}_0).
\]
Here $\mathrm{AC}_0$ is ordinary 1-topos choice for $0$-truncated objects (sets) in the underlying 1-topos $\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$, whereas $\mathrm{AC}_1$ requires splitting groupoid-fibration epimorphisms over sets.

What is the categorical residuum bridging ordinary set-level choice $\mathrm{AC}_0$ to full infinity-choice $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$? If $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ is an effective epimorphism onto a $0$-truncated object $Y$, $X$ decomposes into its Postnikov tower $\cdots \to \tau_{\le n+1}X \xrightarrow{p_{n+1}} \tau_{\le n}X \to \cdots \to \tau_{\le 0}X \twoheadrightarrow Y$.

\begin{theorem}[The Global Infinity-Choice Residuum: $\mathrm{AC}_0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}_\infty$]\label{thm:ac0-acinf}%
\uses{def:higher-choice,thm:bdf}%

In an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$, the categorical residuum bridging $0$-truncated choice $\mathrm{AC}_0$ to full infinity-choice $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$ is the geometric condition that $0$-truncated objects (sets) cover $\mathcal{X}$:

\[
  \mathrm{AC}_0 + \bigl(0\text{-truncated objects cover } \mathcal{X}\bigr) \iff \mathrm{AC}_\infty
\]

\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Suppose first that $\mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_\infty$. Then $\mathrm{AC}_0$ holds immediately as the special case of $0$-truncated morphisms. Moreover, for any object $X \in \mathcal{X}$, the $0$-truncation unit $\eta_X : X \twoheadrightarrow \tau_{\le 0} X$ is an effective epimorphism onto the $0$-truncated object $\tau_{\le 0} X \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$. By $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$, $\eta_X$ admits a section $s : \tau_{\le 0} X \to X$ satisfying $\eta_X \circ s \simeq \mathrm{id}_{\tau_{\le 0} X}$. Because $\tau_{\le 0}(s) \simeq \mathrm{id}_{\tau_{\le 0} X}$ is an effective epimorphism in $\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$, the map $s : \tau_{\le 0} X \twoheadrightarrow X$ is itself an effective epimorphism from a $0$-truncated object covering $X$ (Lurie~\cite{Lurie09}, Proposition~7.2.1.14).

Conversely, suppose $\mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_0$ and every object $X \in \mathcal{X}$ admits an effective epimorphism $q : S \twoheadrightarrow X$ with $S \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$. Let $e : X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ be an arbitrary effective epimorphism onto a $0$-truncated object $Y \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$. Since effective epimorphisms are closed under composition, $e \circ q : S \twoheadrightarrow Y$ is an effective epimorphism between $0$-truncated objects, hence admits a section $\sigma : Y \to S$ by $\mathrm{AC}_0$. Then $s := q \circ \sigma : Y \to X$ satisfies $e \circ s = (e \circ q) \circ \sigma \simeq \mathrm{id}_Y$, establishing $\mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_\infty$.
\end{proof}

Even more finely, we can isolate the stepwise residuum $(\mathrm{AC}_n \Rightarrow_{(\infty,1)\text{-}\mathbf{Topos}} \mathrm{AC}_{n+1})$ between adjacent rungs of the Postnikov ladder. In the relative Postnikov tower of $X \twoheadrightarrow Y$, lifting a section $s_n: Y \to \tau_{\le n}(X/Y)$ across stage $p_{n+1}: \tau_{\le n+1}(X/Y) \to \tau_{\le n}(X/Y)$ is governed for the non-abelian base rung $n = 0$ by Giraud's non-abelian gerbe cohomology~\cite{Giraud71} $H^2_{\mathrm{gerbe}}(\mathcal{X}/Y, \Pi_1)$ (for general group bands $\Pi_1 = s_0^*\bigl(\pi_1(X/Y)\bigr)$, where $B^2\Pi_1$ need not exist) and for the abelian rungs $n \ge 1$ by the Eilenberg--MacLane cohomology class in $H^{n+2}(\mathcal{X}/Y, \Pi_{n+1})$ classified by $K(\Pi_{n+1}, n+2) = B^{n+2}\Pi_{n+1}$ (for abelian group bands $\Pi_{n+1} = s_n^*\bigl(\pi_{n+1}(X/Y)\bigr)$):

\begin{conjecture}[The Postnikov Stepwise Residuum Conjecture: $\mathrm{AC}_n \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}_{n+1}$]\label{conj:postnikov-step}

For every integer $n \ge 0$ in an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$, the stepwise categorical residuum $\mathcal{R}_n(\mathcal{X})$ satisfying

\[
  \mathrm{AC}_n + \mathcal{R}_n(\mathcal{X}) \iff \mathrm{AC}_{n+1}
\]

is the condition that for every $0$-truncated object $Y \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$, the $(n+1)$-gerbe obstruction vanishes on a $0$-truncated projective cover of $Y$: specifically, the vanishing of Giraud's non-abelian gerbe cohomology $H^2_{\mathrm{gerbe}}(\mathcal{X}/Y, \Pi_1)$ for every group band $\Pi_1$ when $n = 0$, and the vanishing of Eilenberg--MacLane cohomology $H^{n+2}(\mathcal{X}/Y, \Pi_{n+1})$ for every abelian group band $\Pi_{n+1}$ when $n \ge 1$.

\end{conjecture}

\section{Summary of the Choice-Geometry Residuum Atlas}

\begin{center}\emph{[Summary ledger table omitted in blueprint edition; see Royal Octavo monograph PDF.]}\end{center}

\part{Iterative Fixed Points, $\delta$-Rings, and Infinite Towers}

\chapter{Non-AC Theorem Lattices: Continuum Analysis, Pointfree Spatiality, and Homological Exactness}

\label{ch:non-ac-lattices}

\section{The Internal Continuum and Real Analysis Lattice}

While the Axiom of Choice ($\mathrm{AC}$) provides the archetypal testing ground for Categorical Reverse Mathematics ($\mathrm{CRM}$), the residuum equivalence $\mathcal{D}_0 + \mathrm{Res}(\kappa) \iff \mathcal{D}_1$ applies across diverse mathematical structures whenever a classical equivalence bifurcates inside general categories or topoi. A primary example of this bifurcation occurs in the internal construction of the real line inside an elementary topos $\mathcal{E}$ equipped with a Natural Numbers Object ($\mathrm{NNO}$)~\cite{Johnstone82,Johnstone02}.

In classical mathematics over $\mathbf{Set}$ (with Countable Choice $\mathrm{CC}$ and the Law of the Excluded Middle $\mathrm{LEM}$), all standard completions of the rational numbers $\mathbb{Q}$ yield isomorphic fields. In an arbitrary elementary topos $\mathcal{E}$ with an $\mathrm{NNO}$, however, the continuum splits into a canonical chain of comparison morphisms:

\[
  \mathbb{Q}_{\mathcal{E}} \rightarrowtail \mathbb{R}_C \rightarrowtail \mathbb{R}_D \longrightarrow \mathbb{R}_M
\]

Here $\mathbb{R}_C$ denotes the \emph{Cauchy reals} (quotients of modulated Cauchy sequences of rationals), $\mathbb{R}_D$ denotes the \emph{Dedekind reals} (pairs of open, located, rounded lower and upper cuts $(L, U) \subseteq \mathbb{Q} \times \mathbb{Q}$), and $\mathbb{R}_M$ denotes the \emph{MacNeille reals} (the Dedekind-complete lattice order completion of $\mathbb{Q}$, which exists in any Boolean topos even when $\mathbb{R}_D$ is degenerate). Crucially, unlike $\mathbb{Q}_{\mathcal{E}} \rightarrowtail \mathbb{R}_C \rightarrowtail \mathbb{R}_D$, the canonical comparison map $\mathbb{R}_D \longrightarrow \mathbb{R}_M$ is a \emph{monomorphism} ($\mathbb{R}_D \rightarrowtail \mathbb{R}_M$, and indeed an isomorphism under bounded completeness) if and only if $\mathcal{E}$ satisfies the Weak Law of Excluded Middle ($\mathrm{WLEM}$: $\neg\phi \vee \neg\neg\phi = \top$, i.e., $\Omega_{\mathcal{E}}$ is a De Morgan / Stone lattice; see Theorem~\ref{thm:omega-dictionary})~\cite{Johnstone02}.

Calibrating classical real analysis via the categorical residuum $(A \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{Topos}} B)$ isolates three fundamental geometric bridges:

\begin{enumerate}

  \item \textbf{The Cauchy-to-Dedekind Continuum Collapse ($\mathbb{R}_C \Rightarrow \mathbb{R}_D$).} In any topos $\mathcal{E}$ satisfying Countable Choice ($\mathrm{CC}$), one can extract a modulated rational Cauchy sequence from the locatedness of any Dedekind cut, forcing $\mathbb{R}_C \simeq \mathbb{R}_D$. However, $\mathrm{CC}$ is a choice principle rather than a pure geometric property of a site. If we forbid $\mathrm{CC}$ and ask for the purely topological residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ over a Grothendieck sheaf topos $\mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}(\mathbb{C}, J)$ with an $\mathrm{NNO}$ such that the canonical inclusion $\mathbb{R}_C \rightarrowtail \mathbb{R}_D$ is an isomorphism, $\mathrm{CRM}$ isolates $\omega$-completeness (open-cover refinement) of the site:

\[
  \top + \bigl(\text{Countable open-cover refinement of } (\mathbb{C}, J)\bigr) \iff \mathbb{R}_C \simeq \mathbb{R}_D
\]

Specifically, when every countable family of open coverings in the underlying locale admits a common refinement (as in sheaves over Baire or $\sigma$-compact zero-dimensional spaces), locally selected rational approximations glue into global Cauchy sequences without invoking $\mathrm{CC}$.

  \item \textbf{Approximate versus Exact Intermediate Value Theorem ($\varepsilon\text{-}\mathrm{IVT} \Rightarrow \mathrm{IVT}$).} Over Bishop-style constructive mathematics ($\mathrm{BISH}$)~\cite{Bishop67} and in any elementary topos with an $\mathrm{NNO}$, the $\varepsilon$-Approximate Intermediate Value Theorem ($\varepsilon\text{-}\mathrm{IVT}$) holds unconditionally: for every uniformly continuous map $f: [0,1]_{\mathbb{R}_D} \to \mathbb{R}_D$ with $f(0) < 0 < f(1)$ and every $n \in \mathbb{N}$, bisection yields $x \in [0,1]$ with $|f(x)| < 2^{-n}$. Yet the \emph{Exact Intermediate Value Theorem} ($\mathrm{IVT}$: $\exists x \in [0,1],\, f(x) = 0$) fails in general sheaf topoi $\mathbf{Sh}(X)$, where continuously varying functions can cross zero on nowhere-dense boundaries without admitting a continuous root section. Computing the categorical residuum yields:

\[
  \varepsilon\text{-}\mathrm{IVT} + \mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}) \iff \mathrm{IVT}
\]

where $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ calibrates the exactness of Dedekind cuts against the \emph{zero-dimensionality or local connectedness} of the ambient site (or, internally, the Weak Law of Excluded Middle $\mathrm{WLEM}$ combined with countable cut-completeness).

  \item \textbf{Sequential Compactness to the Extreme Value Theorem.} In classical reverse mathematics over $\mathrm{RCA}_0$, the Extreme Value Theorem and Uniform Continuity of continuous functions on $[0,1]$ reverse to Weak K\"onig's Lemma ($\mathrm{WKL}_0$)~\cite{Simpson09}. In $\mathrm{CRM}$, bridging sequential compactness ($A$) to the Extreme Value Theorem and Uniform Continuity on $[0,1]_{\mathcal{E}}$ ($B$) isolates the \emph{internal compactness of the Cantor locale $2^{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathcal{E}}$} inside $\mathcal{E}$, equivalent to the Fan Theorem over the internal binary tree~\cite{Johnstone82}. (By contrast, the Baire locale $\mathbb{N}^{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathcal{E}}$ is \emph{never} compact in any non-trivial topos.)

\end{enumerate}

\section{The Pointfree versus Spatial Duality Lattice}

A central result of topos theory is that many classical theorems of topology and functional analysis that require $\mathrm{AC}$ or the Boolean Prime Ideal Theorem ($\mathrm{BPI}$) for point-set topological spaces become \emph{completely constructive and choice-free} when formulated for pointfree locales (frames) inside an arbitrary topos $\mathcal{E}$~\cite{Johnstone82}.

A \emph{frame} $L$ in a topos $\mathcal{E}$ is a complete lattice satisfying the infinite distributive law $a \wedge \bigvee_{i \in I} b_i = \bigvee_{i \in I} (a \wedge b_i)$; the opposite category $\mathbf{Loc}(\mathcal{E}) = \mathbf{Frm}(\mathcal{E})^{\mathrm{op}}$ is the category of \emph{internal locales}. A point of a locale $L$ is a frame homomorphism $p^*: L \to \Omega_{\mathcal{E}}$, and $L$ is \emph{spatial} (has enough points) if distinct elements of $L$ are separated by points.

When we compare a pointfree localic theorem ($A$) to its classical spatial counterpart ($B$), $\mathrm{CRM}$ factors out set-theoretic choice and isolates \emph{internal spatiality} as the exact geometric residuum:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Localic versus Spatial Tychonoff Theorem:} Peter Johnstone~\cite{Johnstone81,Johnstone82} proved that the arbitrary product of compact locales $\prod_{i \in I} L_i$ is compact in \emph{any} elementary topos $\mathcal{E}$ without using $\mathrm{AC}$ or $\mathrm{BPI}$. Taking $A$ to be Johnstone's localic Tychonoff theorem and $B$ to be the classical point-set Tychonoff theorem for topological spaces in $\mathcal{E}$, the residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ is precisely the \emph{spatiality of compact product locales} (which, by Deligne's theorem~\cite{Deligne72}, is equivalent to internal $\mathrm{BPI}$ / logical compactness of $\Omega$).

  \item \textbf{Localic versus Spatial Hahn--Banach Theorem:} Christopher Mulvey and Joan Wick Pelletier~\cite{MulveyPelletier91} proved that for any normed space $E$ inside a Grothendieck topos $\mathcal{E}$, the \emph{localic Hahn--Banach theorem} ($A$) holds constructively: the locale of norm-preserving linear extensions of a functional on a subspace $M \subseteq E$ is always compact and non-trivial. Elevating $A$ to the classical point-set Hahn--Banach theorem ($B$: the existence of an actual global linear functional $f: E \to \mathbb{R}_D$) requires the residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ that compact regular locales in $\mathcal{E}$ possess global points.

\end{itemize}

Crucially, by the \emph{Base-Fiber Conservation Principle} (Chapter~\ref{ch:foundations}, Section~\ref{sec:base-fiber}, Principle~\ref{prin:conservation}), if we evaluate this spatiality residuum in a relative Grothendieck topos $\gamma: \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ and \emph{remove} $\mathrm{BPI}$ from the metamathematical base topos $\mathcal{S}$, finitary coherence (every cover has a finite subcover) is no longer strong enough to guarantee points. The lost ultrafilter strength in $\mathcal{S}$ reappears in the fiber residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S})$, which must strengthen from finitary coherence to \emph{supercompactness}---the topological condition that every covering refines to a single generating element, producing points without base ultrafilters.

\section{The Intermediate Logic and Subobject Classifier Lattice}

In Constructive Reverse Mathematics over $\mathrm{BISH}$~\cite{Bishop67,Ishihara06}, numerous theorems of analysis and commutative algebra are calibrated by semi-classical logical principles lying strictly between Intuitionistic Propositional Calculus ($\mathrm{IPC}$) and the full Law of the Excluded Middle ($\mathrm{LEM}$). Categorical Reverse Mathematics translates this logical hierarchy directly into the order-algebraic geometry of the subobject classifier $\Omega$ and the topology of underlying sites~\cite{Johnstone82,Johnstone02}.

\begin{theorem}[The $\Omega$-Geometry Dictionary for Intermediate Logics]\label{thm:omega-dictionary}%
\uses{def:comparison-triple,def:residuum}%

Let $\mathcal{E}$ be an elementary topos with subobject classifier $\Omega$, and in the spatial case let $\mathcal{E} = \mathbf{Sh}(X)$ be sheaves on a topological space $X$. Then the residuums of semi-classical logical principles correspond to the following geometric invariants:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{De Morgan's Law ($\mathrm{WLEM}$:} $\neg\phi \vee \neg\neg\phi = \top$): Holds internally in $\mathcal{E}$ if and only if $\Omega_{\mathcal{E}}$ is an internal Stone (De Morgan) lattice (a Heyting algebra satisfying $\neg\phi \vee \neg\neg\phi = \top$, not to be confused with a topological Stone space); in $\mathbf{Sh}(X)$, this holds if and only if $X$ is \emph{extremally disconnected} (the closure of every open set is open)~\cite{Johnstone82}.

  \item \textbf{G\"odel--Dummett Linearity ($(\phi \to \psi) \vee (\psi \to \phi) = \top$):} Holds internally in $\mathcal{E}$ if and only if the Heyting algebra $\Omega$ is linearly ordered; in presheaf topoi $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{op}}}$ over a poset $\mathcal{P}$, this holds if and only if $\mathcal{P}$ is a \emph{forest of trees} (every upward cone is linearly ordered).

  \item \textbf{Full Booleanness ($\mathrm{LEM}$:} $\phi \vee \neg\phi = \top$): Holds internally in $\mathcal{E}$ if and only if $\Omega \simeq 1+1$ is an internal Boolean algebra; in $\mathbf{Sh}(X)$ for a $T_0$ space $X$, this holds if and only if $X$ is discrete (or in localic topoi $\mathbf{Sh}(B)$, when the frame $B$ is a complete Boolean algebra).

\end{itemize}

\end{theorem}

This dictionary precisely calibrates the internal order-algebraic properties of the Dedekind reals $\mathbb{R}_D$ in a sheaf topos $\mathcal{E} = \mathbf{Sh}(X)$ (Johnstone, \emph{Sketches of an Elephant}, Section~D4.7~\cite{Johnstone02}). In any sheaf topos $\mathbf{Sh}(X)$, $\mathbb{R}_D$ constructively forms a commutative local ring ($x$ invertible or $1-x$ invertible) and an archimedean $f$-ring. However, two stronger classical properties of the continuum bifurcate along the $\Omega_{\mathcal{E}}$-geometry lattice:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Weak Order Linearity and the MacNeille Collapse ($\mathbb{R}_D \simeq \mathbb{R}_M$):} Taking $A$ as the baseline theory of $\mathbb{R}_D$ and $B$ as \emph{weak order linearity} $\forall x \in \mathbb{R}_D\,(x \le 0 \vee x \ge 0)$ together with the order-completion isomorphism $\mathbb{R}_D \simeq \mathbb{R}_M$ with the MacNeille reals, the exact categorical residuum $(A \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{Sh}(X)} B)$ is the De Morgan (Stone) lattice structure of $\Omega_{\mathcal{E}}$ ($\mathrm{WLEM}: \neg\phi \vee \neg\neg\phi = \top$, i.e., $X$ is extremally disconnected)~\cite{Johnstone02}.

  \item \textbf{Geometric Field Dichotomy:} By contrast, the stronger \emph{geometric field dichotomy} $\forall x \in \mathbb{R}_D\,(x = 0 \vee x \text{ invertible})$ requires that zero-sets of continuous real-valued functions be open. This fails even on extremally disconnected spaces such as $\beta\mathbb{N}$, and requires open zero-sets ($X$ is a $P$-space, or Boolean $\Omega_{\mathcal{E}} \simeq 1+1$ on compact Hausdorff bases).

\end{itemize}

\section{The Homological and Categorical Exactness Lattice}

Because the formal definition of the categorical residuum $\mathrm{Res}(\kappa)$ on $\mathrm{Dom}(\mathrm{Res})$ (Definitions~\ref{def:comparison-triple}--\ref{def:residuum}) applies across arbitrary $2$-categories of structured doctrines---even when $\mathcal{D}_0$ consists of non-Cartesian or non-topos categories---$\mathrm{CRM}$ provides a rigorous foundation for calibrating the diagram lemmas of \emph{homological algebra}.

In classical homological algebra, diagram lemmas such as the Five Lemma, the $3 \times 3$ Lemma, and the Snake Lemma are proved inside \emph{abelian categories} ($\mathbf{Ab}$, $R\text{-}\mathbf{Mod}$), which enjoy biproducts, kernels, cokernels, and exactness of all monomorphisms and epimorphisms. However, non-abelian algebra ($\mathbf{Grp}$, $\mathbf{LieAlg}$, $\mathbf{Rng}$, crossed modules) operates in a genuine two-dimensional bifurcation lattice of exactness doctrines rather than a single linear chain (Figure~\ref{fig:homological-lattice}):

\begin{center}\emph{[Figure omitted in blueprint edition; see Royal Octavo monograph PDF.]}\label{fig:homological-lattice}\end{center}

Here downward arrows denote strict doctrine specialization ($\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{top}} \supsetneq \mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{bot}}$). Applying the $\mathrm{CRM}$ residuum framework to diagram chasing and infinite limits reveals the exact structural cost separating each tier in Figure~\ref{fig:homological-lattice}:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Orthogonality of Barr-Exactness and Bourn Protomodularity:} A category $\mathcal{A}$ is \emph{regular} if it has finite limits, coequalizers of kernel pairs, and pullback-stable regular epimorphisms. From $\mathbf{RegCat}$, exactness bifurcates along two genuinely incomparable axes:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Barr-exactness} demands that every internal equivalence relation is effective (a kernel pair). Every elementary topos ($\mathbf{Set}$, $\mathbf{Sh}(\mathbb{C}, J)$), pointed sets $\mathbf{Set}_*$, and commutative monoids $\mathbf{CMon}$ is Barr-exact, yet fails Bourn protomodularity (in $\mathbf{Set}_*$, the inclusion $\{*, a\} \hookrightarrow \{*, a, b\}$ pulls back to an isomorphism along the split epimorphism $\{*, a, b\} \twoheadrightarrow \{*, a\}$ sending $b \mapsto *$, violating the Split Short Five Lemma).
    \item \emph{Bourn protomodularity}~\cite{Bourn91} asserts for pointed regular categories that the Split Short Five Lemma holds for split extensions (equivalently, pullback functors along split epimorphisms reflect isomorphisms). Group-like categories ($\mathbf{Grp}$, $\mathbf{Loop}$ with inverses), torsion-free abelian groups $\mathbf{TFAB}$, and Hausdorff topological groups $\mathbf{HausGrp}$ are all pointed, regular, and Bourn protomodular, so the Short Five Lemma holds without abelianness. Yet neither $\mathbf{TFAB}$ nor $\mathbf{HausGrp}$ is Barr-exact: in $\mathbf{TFAB}$, the congruence $x \equiv y \pmod 2$ on $\mathbb{Z}$ is an internal equivalence relation whose coequalizer is $\mathbb{Z} \to 0$ (whose kernel pair is all of $\mathbb{Z} \times \mathbb{Z}$), while in $\mathbf{HausGrp}$ any non-closed normal subgroup defines a non-effective equivalence relation.
  \end{enumerate}

  \item \textbf{Semi-Abelian Intersection and the Snake Lemma Residuum:} By definition, a category is \emph{semi-abelian} if and only if it is pointed, Barr-exact, Bourn protomodular, and has finite coproducts:
  \[
    \begin{aligned}
      \mathbf{SemiAbelian} \;=\; \mathbf{BarrExact} &\cap \mathbf{Protomodular} \\
      &\cap \mathbf{Pointed} \cap \mathbf{FinCoproducts}.
    \end{aligned}
  \]
  While the Short Five Lemma ($A$) holds in any pointed protomodular category, the full \emph{Snake Lemma} ($B$---constructing the connecting homomorphism $\delta: \mathrm{Ker}(c) \to \mathrm{Coker}(a)$ and proving exactness of the six-term sequence) fails in general protomodular categories. Computing the homological residuum isolates \emph{Barr-exactness and normality/subobject modularity} ($\mathbf{SemiAbelian}$) for naturality of $\delta$, and full \emph{additivity} ($\mathbf{Ab}$-enrichment, where every monomorphism is normal) to ascend to $\mathbf{Abelian}$.

  \item \textbf{Divergence of Infinite Abelian Exactness ($\mathbf{AB5}$ vs.\ $\mathbf{AB4}^*$):} Within $\mathbf{Abelian}$, Grothendieck's two higher exactness axioms bifurcate along dual infinitary directions:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item \emph{Axiom $\mathbf{AB5}$} asserts that filtered colimits are exact (commute with finite limits). Together with a generator, $\mathbf{AB5}$ guarantees enough injectives for injective resolutions and right derived functors $R^q F$.
    \item \emph{Axiom $\mathbf{AB4}^*$} asserts that small (in particular countable) products of epimorphisms remain epimorphic (exactness of small products), which is the dual exactness condition governing inverse limits and the vanishing of derived inverse limits $\varprojlim^1$.
  \end{enumerate}
  In abelian sheaf categories $\mathbf{Sh}(X, \mathbf{Ab})$, $\mathbf{AB5}$ holds unconditionally while $\mathbf{AB4}^*$ fails on non-discrete spaces $X$, precipitating non-vanishing $\varprojlim^1$ obstructions (examined quantitatively in Chapter~\ref{ch:base-fiber}, Sections~4.4--\ref{sec:exotic-r4-e0}).

\end{itemize}

\section{Predicativity, Recursion, and Well-Foundedness (The Categorical ``Big Five'')}\label{sec:predicativity-wtypes}

Whereas the lower tiers of the classical Friedman--Simpson ``Big Five'' ($\mathrm{RCA}_0$ and $\mathrm{WKL}_0$) correspond to internal arithmetic and Cantor-locale compactness~\cite{Simpson09}, the upper tiers ($\mathrm{ACA}_0$, $\mathrm{ATR}_0$, and $\Pi^1_1\text{-}\mathrm{CA}_0$) calibrate arithmetical comprehension, transfinite bar recursion along well-orderings, and impredicative inductive definitions.

In Categorical Reverse Mathematics, this proof-theoretic climb maps directly onto the Axis~1 structural hierarchy of universes (Chapter~\ref{ch:foundations}, Sections~1.5 and~\ref{sec:base-fiber}), ascending from predicative \emph{Arithmetic Universes} to impredicative \emph{Elementary Topoi}:

\[
  \mathbf{ArithUniv} \subsetneq \Pi\text{-}\mathbf{Pretopos} \subsetneq \Pi W\text{-}\mathbf{Pretopos} \subsetneq \mathbf{ElemTopos}
\]

As formulated in Chapter~\ref{ch:foundations} (Section~1.5), each adjacent tier in this hierarchy is bridged by a universal adjoint or initial-algebra residuum:

\begin{enumerate}

  \item \textbf{Primitive Recursion to Arithmetical Comprehension ($\mathrm{RCA}_0 \to \mathrm{ACA}_0$).} An \emph{Arithmetic Universe} (in the sense of Andr\'e Joyal and Maria Emilia Maietti~\cite{Maietti05}) is a predicative list-arithmetic pretopos possessing a parameterized Natural Numbers Object ($\mathrm{NNO}$), disjoint stable sums, and effective quotients, but lacking function spaces $Y^X$ and a subobject classifier $\Omega$. Bridging finitary recursive arithmetic ($\mathrm{RCA}_0$) to full arithmetical quantification ($\mathrm{ACA}_0$) is governed by \emph{local Cartesian closure} ($\Pi$-types / dependent products $\Pi_{x:A} B(x)$ right adjoint to pullback $f^*$), elevating an Arithmetic Universe to a locally Cartesian closed $\Pi$-pretopos.

  \item \textbf{Arithmetical Comprehension to Arithmetical Transfinite Recursion ($\mathrm{ACA}_0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{ATR}_0$).} Theorems at the level of $\mathrm{ATR}_0$ (and closely related well-quasi-ordering theorems such as Kruskal's Tree Theorem or Goodstein's Theorem) require iterating comprehension along arbitrary well-founded trees. In a $\Pi$-pretopos $\mathcal{E}$, every polynomial container $f: B \to A$ defines a polynomial endofunctor $P_f(X) = \Sigma_{a \in A} X^{B_a}$. The exact categorical residuum bridging primitive/arithmetical recursion ($A = \mathrm{ACA}_0$) to transfinite bar recursion ($B = \mathrm{ATR}_0$) is the existence of \emph{initial algebras for polynomial endofunctors (Martin-L\"of W-types)}~\cite{Maietti05}:

\[
  \begin{aligned}
    \mathrm{ACA}_0 + \bigl(\text{Initial Algebras / } W\text{-Types for } &\text{Polynomial Endofunctors } P_f\bigr) \\
    &\iff \mathrm{ATR}_0
  \end{aligned}
\]

Adding W-types promotes a $\Pi$-pretopos to a predicative topos ($\Pi W$-pretopos), providing universal well-founded trees without assuming impredicative power sets.

  \item \textbf{Predicative Tree Induction to Impredicative Comprehension ($\mathrm{ATR}_0 \Rightarrow \Pi^1_1\text{-}\mathrm{CA}_0$).} Finally, theorems at the pinnacle of the ``Big Five''---such as the Cantor--Bendixson theorem or the existence of hyperarithmetical fixed points ($\Pi^1_1\text{-}\mathrm{CA}_0$)---exceed predicative W-types because they form greatest fixed points over arbitrary impredicative collections of subsets. The categorical residuum bridging $\mathrm{ATR}_0$ ($A$) to $\Pi^1_1\text{-}\mathrm{CA}_0$ ($B$) is the impredicative subobject classifier $\Omega$ (and power objects $\mathcal{P}(X) = \Omega^X$), which completes the climb from a $\Pi W$-pretopos to a full Lawvere--Tierney \emph{Elementary Topos}.

\end{enumerate}

\section{The Synthetic Differential and Cohesive Geometry Lattice}\label{sec:cohesive-lattice}

An important feature of Categorical Reverse Mathematics over classical Friedman--Simpson reverse mathematics is that $\mathrm{CRM}$ can calibrate mathematical theories that are formally inconsistent with classical logic ($\mathrm{LEM}$) and the Axiom of Choice ($\mathrm{AC}$). The premier example is \emph{Synthetic Differential Geometry (SDG)}, developed by F.~William Lawvere, Anders Kock~\cite{Kock06}, and Ieke Moerdijk--Gonzalo Reyes~\cite{MoerdijkReyes91} inside smooth Grothendieck topoi (such as the Cahiers topos or Dubuc's topos of $C^\infty$-rings).

Inside a smooth topos $\mathcal{E}$ equipped with a commutative line object $R$, one defines the object of first-order nilpotent infinitesimals $D = \{x \in R \mid x^2 = 0\}$. The foundational \emph{Kock--Lawvere Axiom} ($A$) asserts that the canonical evaluation map~\cite{Kock06}:

\[
  \alpha: R \times R \xrightarrow{\sim} R^D, \quad (a, b) \mapsto \bigl(d \mapsto a + b \cdot d\bigr)
\]

is an isomorphism in $\mathcal{E}$, meaning every curve on the infinitesimal tangent disk $D$ is uniquely affine. Because $D$ contains non-zero indistinguishables ($\neg\forall d \in D,\, d = 0$ yet $\forall d \in D,\, \neg\neg(d = 0)$), the Kock--Lawvere axiom immediately refutes $\mathrm{LEM}$ and hence refutes $\mathrm{AC}$ by Diaconescu's theorem~\cite{Diaconescu75}.

Within this non-Boolean smooth universe $\mathcal{E}$, $\mathrm{CRM}$ calibrates the deductive hierarchy of differential geometry and cohomology via two exact geometric residuums:

\begin{enumerate}

  \item \textbf{First-Order Calculus to Jet Bundles and Curvature (The Microlinearity Residuum).} While the Kock--Lawvere axiom ($A$) suffices for elementary single-variable derivatives on $R$, it does not guarantee that general smooth manifolds $M \in \mathcal{E}$ possess well-behaved tangent bundles $M^D$, Lie brackets of vector fields, affine connections, or Riemann curvature tensors ($B$). In a general smooth topos, higher infinitesimal objects---such as $D(2) = \{(d_1, d_2) \in D \times D \mid d_1 d_2 = 0\}$---form finite colimits of Weil algebras in the opposite category of infinitesimal spaces. Computing the categorical residuum $(A \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{SmoothTopos}} B)$ isolates \emph{microlinearity}~\cite{Kock06,MoerdijkReyes91}: the exactness condition that the functor $M^{(-)}$ sends canonical finite colimits of infinitesimal varieties (such as $D \amalg_{\{0\}} D \to D(2)$) to finite limits (pullbacks $M^{D(2)} \xrightarrow{\sim} M^D \times_M M^D$):

\[
  \begin{aligned}
    &\text{Kock--Lawvere Axiom } (A) + \text{Microlinearity} \\
    &\quad\Longleftrightarrow \text{Connections, Lie Brackets, and Curvature } (B)
  \end{aligned}
\]

  \item \textbf{Local Infinitesimal Calculus to Global Cohomology (Cohesion vs.\ Tinyness Across Smooth Regimes).} Even when a smooth topos $\mathcal{E}$ satisfies the Kock--Lawvere axiom and microlinearity (supporting local differential forms and infinitesimal simplices $D^n$), global theorems such as Stokes' Theorem and the de Rham isomorphism between synthetic singular cohomology and differential-form cohomology ($B$) can fail. Following Moerdijk and Reyes~\cite{MoerdijkReyes91}, solving $A + \mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}) \iff B$ bifurcates into two distinct geometric residuum regimes depending on the presentation site of $\mathcal{E}$:

  \begin{itemize}

    \item \textbf{Manifold and Analytic Smooth Topoi (Integration, Archimedean Glueing, and Cohesion):} In well-adapted Grothendieck smooth topoi equipped with open-cover topologies---such as Dubuc's topos $\mathcal{G}$ and the Cahiers topos---the infinitesimal disk $D$ is \emph{not} tiny, since a right adjoint $(-)^D \dashv (-)_D$ would force $(-)^D$ to preserve open covers. In this regime, global Stokes' and de Rham theorems require the \emph{Integration Axiom}, \emph{Archimedean open-cover glueing}, and \emph{Lawvere's cohesive adjoint quadruple}~\cite{Lawvere07,Schreiber13}, where over the base topos $\mathcal{S}$, the canonical geometric morphism $\gamma: \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ (with direct image $\Gamma = \gamma_*$) extends to a \emph{cohesive quadruple of adjoint functors}:

\[
  \Pi \dashv \mathrm{Disc} \dashv \Gamma \dashv \mathrm{Codisc}
\]

where the left adjoint $\Pi$ (the fundamental $\infty$-groupoid / path-component functor) preserves finite products, bridging local infinitesimal contractibility to global homotopy and de Rham cohomology.

    \item \textbf{Algebraic and Simplicial Smooth Presheaf Topoi (Tinyness of $D$):} In algebraic or finitely presented $C^\infty$-ring presheaf topoi $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{op}}}$, synthetic cochain duality is bridged instead by the \emph{tinyness (internal atomicity) of the infinitesimal disk $D$}~\cite{Kock06,MoerdijkReyes91}: the tangent-bundle exponentiation functor $(-)^D: \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{E}$ possesses a right adjoint $(-)_D$ ($(-)^D \dashv (-)_D$), making $D$ an internally projective atom so that infinitesimal cochains dualize directly into global differential forms.

  \end{itemize}

\end{enumerate}

\section{Joyal $\delta$-Rings, $2$-Torsion Lift Torsors ($R_2$ vs.\ \texttt{DualInt}), and Relative Slice Perfection}\label{sec:delta-rings}

Axis~IV of $\mathrm{Dom}(\mathrm{Res})$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:foundations}, Definition~\ref{def:comparison-triple} and Table~\ref{tab:five-axis-master}) demonstrates how Categorical Reverse Mathematics operates natively inside commutative algebra without requiring any ambient topos of sets. Comparing Andr\'e Joyal's~\cite{Joyal85} universal $\delta$-ring functor to naive Fermat quotient division $\frac{\phi(x) - x^p}{p}$ exposes a sharp $p$-torsion bifurcation between lift torsors ($R_2$), non-perfect torsion-free obstructions (\texttt{DualInt}), and relative colimit perfection~\cite{BhattScholze22,Goldthorpe23}.

\subsection{Joyal's $\delta$-Ring Axioms and Polynomial Cofactors $C_p(x, y)$}

Fix a prime $p$. Following Joyal~\cite{Joyal85} and Bhatt--Scholze~\cite{BhattScholze22} (\S2), a \emph{$p$-typical $\delta$-ring} is a commutative ring $A$ equipped with a set map $\delta : A \to A$ satisfying $\delta(0) = 0$, $\delta(1) = 0$, and the integral polynomial identities
\[
  \begin{aligned}
    \delta(x + y) &\;=\; \delta(x) + \delta(y) + C_p(x, y), \\
    \delta(xy) &\;=\; x^p\delta(y) + y^p\delta(x) + p\,\delta(x)\delta(y),
  \end{aligned}
\]
where $C_p(x, y) := \frac{x^p + y^p - (x+y)^p}{p} = -\sum_{k=1}^{p-1} \frac{1}{p}\binom{p}{k} x^k y^{p-k} \in \mathbb{Z}[x, y]$ is the universal binomial cofactor polynomial. Because $C_p(x, y)$ has integral coefficients, these identities make no use of division by $p$, yet unconditionally force the associated map $\phi(x) := x^p + p\,\delta(x)$ to be a ring endomorphism $A \to A$ (the \emph{Frobenius lift}) satisfying $\phi(x) \equiv x^p \pmod{pA}$ and $\delta \circ \phi = \phi \circ \delta$.

At $p = 2$, $C_2(x, y) = -xy$, simplifying the additive axiom to $\delta(x + y) = \delta(x) + \delta(y) - xy$ (\texttt{DeltaRing2} in \texttt{HomotopyCRM/ DeltaRing Perfection.lean}). Over $\mathbb{Z}$---which is initial in $\mathbf{CRing}$---the unique ring endomorphism is $\phi = \mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}}$ (since $x \mapsto x^2$ fails additivity), forcing the unique $2$-typical $\delta$-structure $\delta_{\mathbb{Z}}(a) = \frac{a - a^2}{2}$ (\texttt{instDeltaRing2Int}, \texttt{instUniqueDeltaRing2Int}), which is already perfect ($\phi = \mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}}$, \texttt{isPerfectDeltaRing\_int}).

\subsection{The $2$-Torsion Lift Torsor $R_2$ vs.\ Torsion-Free \texttt{DualInt}}

When a commutative ring possesses $p$-torsion, the comparison $\kappa : \delta(x) \mapsto \frac{\phi(x) - x^p}{p}$ breaks down, precipitating two complementary phenomena:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Non-Trivial Lift Torsors on $R_2 = \mathbb{Z}[\eta]/(2\eta, \eta^2)$:} As an underlying additive abelian group, $R_2$ decomposes as $\mathbb{Z} \oplus \mathbb{F}_2$ (every element writes uniquely as $a + b\eta$ with $a \in \mathbb{Z}$ and $b \in \mathbb{F}_2$). Crucially, $R_2$ is \emph{not} isomorphic as a commutative ring to the reduced product $\mathbb{Z} \times \mathbb{F}_2$, because $R_2$ is connected and non-reduced ($\eta \neq 0$, $\eta^2 = 0$). Because $2\eta = 0$, naive division by $2$ loses all $\eta$-information. Fixing the \textbf{augmentation Frobenius endomorphism} $\phi(a + b\eta) := a$ (so $\phi(\eta) = 0$; never $\phi = \mathrm{id}_{R_2}$), Joyal's identities determine $\delta(a + b\eta) = \frac{a - a^2}{2} + (ab + c\cdot b)\eta$ modulo the free parameter $c = \delta(\eta) \in \mathrm{Ann}_{R_2}(2) = \{0, \eta\} \cong \mathbb{F}_2$. Thus there exist \emph{precisely two} distinct $\delta$-ring structures
  \[
    \begin{aligned}
      \delta_0(a + b\eta) &\;=\; \frac{a - a^2}{2} + ab\,\eta &&(\delta_0(\eta) = 0), \\
      \delta_1(a + b\eta) &\;=\; \frac{a - a^2}{2} + (a+1)b\,\eta &&(\delta_1(\eta) = \eta)
    \end{aligned}
  \]
  lifting the exact same augmentation Frobenius $\phi$, exhibiting $\mathrm{Res}(\kappa)(R_2)$ as a non-trivial $\mathbb{F}_2$-torsor of liftings.

  \item \textbf{Torsion-Free Non-Perfection in \texttt{DualInt} $= \mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2)$:} In \texttt{HomotopyCRM/ DeltaRing Perfection.lean} (1,398 lines, 21 kernel axiom checks under the 4-Layer Definition Check Gate), the kernel-verified witness is the torsion-free ring of dual integers \texttt{DualInt} $= \mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2)$ on underlying carrier $\mathbb{Z} \times \mathbb{Z}$. Because $2$ is a non-zero-divisor in \texttt{DualInt}, the augmentation Frobenius $\phi(a + b\varepsilon) = a$ (\texttt{DualInt.frobeniusLift\_mk}) uniquely forces $\delta(a, b) = \bigl(\frac{a - a^2}{2}, -ab\bigr)$ (\texttt{DualInt.dualDelta}, \texttt{DualInt.instDeltaRing2}). Although its $\delta$-lift is unique, \texttt{DualInt} remains strictly non-perfect (\texttt{DualInt.not\_isPerfectDeltaRing}): $\phi(\varepsilon) = 0$ (\texttt{Checks.dualInt\_frobeniusLift\_eps}) blocks both injectivity and surjectivity of $\phi$.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Goldthorpe Theorems~A and~B, Shift Chirality, and Relative Slice Perfection}

Inside any category $\mathcal{C}$ equipped with an endofunctor $F : \mathcal{C} \to \mathcal{C}$ and a coalgebra $c = (V, \mathrm{str} : V \to F(V))$, Goldthorpe's~\cite{Goldthorpe23} (Proposition~2.3.13) slice endofunctor \texttt{sliceEndofunctor F c} on the coslice $\mathcal{C}_{c.V/}$ (\texttt{Under c.V}) sends $(X, \eta : V \to X)$ to $(F(X), \mathrm{str} \gg F(\eta))$. By Lambek's Lemma (\texttt{lambek\_isIso}), any initial algebra $\mathrm{initAlg}$ of \texttt{sliceEndofunctor F c} has an invertible structure morphism $\nu_\infty : F(A_{\mathrm{init}}) \xrightarrow{\sim} A_{\mathrm{init}}$ (\texttt{sliceLambek\_right\_isIso}), making $A_{\mathrm{init}}$ initial among fixed-point $F$-coalgebras under $c$ (\textbf{Theorem~A}: \texttt{theoremA\_homEquiv}, \texttt{theoremA\_isInitial\_fixedPointCoalgebraUnder}).

Specializing to $F = \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}$ and a natural endomorphism $\varphi : \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}} \Rightarrow \mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}$ (specifically $\varphi = \texttt{DeltaRing2Cat. frobeniusNatEndo}$ on $\mathbf{DeltaRing}_2$, \texttt{DeltaRing2Cat}) on $c = \langle A, \varphi_A \rangle$, let $A_{\mathrm{perf}} := \varinjlim \bigl(A \xrightarrow{\varphi_A} A \xrightarrow{\varphi_A} \cdots\bigr)$ denote any sequential colimit (\texttt{endomorphismChain}). Shifting cocone legs forward by $+1$ (\texttt{ι\_ comp\_ shiftIso\_ hom}) and precomposing backward by $\varphi_A$ (\texttt{ι\_ comp\_ shiftIso\_ inv}) construct mutually inverse shift automorphisms $s : A_{\mathrm{perf}} \xrightarrow{\sim} A_{\mathrm{perf}}$ (\texttt{endomorphismColimitShiftIso}). This certifies \textbf{Theorem~B} with an intrinsic \textbf{$+1$/$-1$ shift chirality}:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Initial Slice-Algebra ($+1$ Shift):} Pairing $(A_{\mathrm{perf}}, \iota_0)$ with the forward shift $s$ (\texttt{perfection SliceAlg}) yields the initial algebra of \texttt{slice Endofunctor} $(\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}, \langle A, \varphi_A \rangle)$ (\texttt{theoremB\_ perfection SliceAlg\_ isInitial}), sending any slice algebra $(B, f, \psi)$ via the iterated cocone legs $n \mapsto \psi^n \circ f$ (\texttt{comp\_ endomorphism Pow\_ eq\_ iterate}).
  \item \textbf{Induced Frobenius ($-1$ Shift):} Naturality forces the induced Frobenius endomorphism $\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}}$ to coincide with the \emph{inverse} shift $s^{-1}$ (\texttt{theoremB\_ natEndo\_app\_ eq\_shift\_inv}), making $\varphi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}}$ an isomorphism (\texttt{theoremB\_ isIso\_ natEndo\_app}).
\end{itemize}
On \texttt{DualInt}, idempotence $\phi(\phi(x)) = \phi(x)$ (\texttt{Checks. dualInt\_ frobenius Lift\_ idem}) kills $\varepsilon$ in a single step, collapsing the sequential perfection colimit to $(\mathbb{Z}, \texttt{DualInt. fst DeltaHom})$ in both $\mathbf{CRing}$ and $\mathbf{DeltaRing}_2$ (\texttt{DualInt. perfection CommRing Cocone\_ isColimit}, \texttt{DualInt. perfection Delta Cocone\_ isColimit}, \texttt{DualInt. perfection SliceAlg DualInt\_ isInitial}).

\begin{remark}[Mathematical Red-Team Guardrail~2: Relative Perfection vs.\ General Prisms]\label{rem:guardrail-2}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.lambek_isIso, HomotopyCRM.sliceLambek_right_isIso, HomotopyCRM.theoremA_homEquiv, HomotopyCRM.theoremA_isInitial_fixedPointCoalgebraUnder, HomotopyCRM.endomorphismColimitShiftIso, HomotopyCRM.theoremB_natEndo_app_eq_shift_inv, HomotopyCRM.theoremB_isIso_natEndo_app, HomotopyCRM.theoremB_perfectionSliceAlg_isInitial, HomotopyCRM.instDeltaRing2Int, HomotopyCRM.isPerfectDeltaRing_int, HomotopyCRM.instUniqueDeltaRing2Int, HomotopyCRM.DualInt.instDeltaRing2, HomotopyCRM.DualInt.not_isPerfectDeltaRing, HomotopyCRM.DualInt.perfectionDeltaCocone_isColimit, HomotopyCRM.DualInt.perfectionSliceAlgDualInt_isInitial}%
\leanok%
\uses{def:comparison-triple}%
The sequential colimit construction $A_{\mathrm{perf}} = \varinjlim_\varphi A$ of Theorem~B models \textbf{strictly} the \emph{relative slice perfection} (the left-adjoint perfect reflector $A \to A_{\mathrm{perf}}$ on which $\varphi$ becomes invertible). A Lambek initial-algebra model \textbf{must never} be imposed on general Bhatt--Scholze prisms $(A, I)$ or on the prismatic site $(R)_{\Prism}$~\cite[\S\S 2--3, \S 12]{BhattScholze22}. In a general prism, non-invertibility of $\phi$ is essential to define the \textbf{Nygaard filtration} $\mathcal{N}^{\ge i} A := \phi^{-1}(I^i)$ (\texttt{nygaardLevel} and \texttt{nygaard\_antitone} in \texttt{DeltaRingPerfection.lean}); forcing $\phi$ to become an isomorphism collapses $\mathcal{N}^{\ge i} A$ to $I^i$. Indeed, Layer~3 of \texttt{DeltaRingPerfection.lean} (\texttt{Checks.dualInt\_nygaard\_separation}) proves that for $I = (\varepsilon)$ in \texttt{DualInt}, $\varepsilon \in \mathcal{N}^{\ge 1}\mathrm{DualInt}$ whereas $1 \notin \mathcal{N}^{\ge 1}\mathrm{DualInt}$---a non-trivial filtration completely obliterated upon passing to $\mathrm{DualInt}_{\mathrm{perf}} \cong \mathbb{Z}$ (where $\phi = \mathrm{id}_{\mathbb{Z}}$).
\end{remark}

\section{Summary of Non-AC Categorical Residuums}
