\chapter{Base-Topos Variation, Geometric Morphisms, and the Base-Fiber Conservation Principle}

\label{ch:base-fiber}

\section{The Two-Axis Relativization of Categorical Reverse Mathematics}\label{sec:two-axis-relativization}

Having calibrated the internal non-$\mathrm{AC}$ theorem lattices of analysis, topology, algebra, homological exactness, and characteristic-$p$ $\delta$-rings within a fixed ambient universe $\mathcal{E}$ in Chapter~\ref{ch:non-ac-lattices} (Sections~3.1--\ref{sec:delta-rings}), we now examine the multi-axis dynamism of Categorical Reverse Mathematics. Classical Friedman--Simpson reverse mathematics over $\mathrm{RCA}_0$ and Ishihara's constructive reverse mathematics over $\mathrm{BISH}$ both fix a single static metamathematical background. In $\mathrm{CRM}$, by contrast, every deductive reversal $A + \mathcal{R} \iff B$ is governed by a doctrinal comparison triple
\[
  \mathfrak{T} = \bigl(\mathcal{D}_0 \supseteq \mathcal{D}_1,\; \kappa : \Phi \Longrightarrow \Psi\bigr) \in \mathrm{Dom}(\mathrm{Res})
\]
in the sense of Chapter~\ref{ch:foundations} (Definitions~\ref{def:comparison-triple}--\ref{def:residuum}, Sections~\ref{sec:finset-infinity}--\ref{sec:base-fiber}, and Table~\ref{tab:five-axis-master}), where $\mathrm{Res}(\kappa)$ measures the universal obstruction to the canonical comparison $\kappa$ restricting to an equivalence outside the stronger subdoctrine $\mathcal{D}_1$. Because $\mathrm{Dom}(\mathrm{Res})$ ranges over structured categorical doctrines equipped with comparisons across ambient and base universes, we can deform any residuum along two orthogonal axes:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Axis~1 (Structural Deformation of the Ambient Universe $\mathcal{E}$):} We weaken or strengthen the Lawvere--Tierney categorical axioms of $\mathcal{E}$ itself---dropping impredicativity ($\Omega$), local Cartesian closure ($Y^X$ and $\Pi_f$), arithmetic infinity ($\mathrm{NNO}$), or $0$-truncation (into $(\infty,1)$-topoi)---to observe how $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ fractures across doctrinal boundaries.

  \item \textbf{Axis~2 (Metamathematical Variation of the Base Universe $\mathcal{S}$):} Given an adjunction $F \dashv U : \mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{E}} \rightleftarrows \mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{S}}$---prototypically a bounded geometric morphism $\gamma : \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ ($\gamma^* \dashv \gamma_*$, classically $\mathcal{S} = \mathbf{Set}$ with $\mathrm{AC}$ and $\mathrm{LEM}$), or a ring base change $R \to S$---we vary the logical and choice axioms assumed in $\mathcal{S}$ and measure the compensatory shift in the relative fiber residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S}) = \mathrm{Res}(\kappa_{\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S}})$.

\end{itemize}

Together, these two axes transform Reverse Mathematics from a static taxonomy of formal subsystems into a quantitative theory of \emph{logical-geometric covariance} across mathematical universes.

\section{Axis 1 Deformations: Weakening the Lawvere--Tierney Axioms of $\mathcal{E}$}\label{sec:axis1-deformations}

An elementary topos $\mathcal{E}$ with a Natural Numbers Object ($\mathrm{NNO}$) is governed by four structural pillars: finite limits and colimits, exponential function objects $Y^X$ (Cartesian closure in every slice $\mathcal{E}/X$), an impredicative subobject classifier $\Omega$, and an infinitary $\mathrm{NNO}$ together with $0$-truncation of hom-sets. Removing each pillar systematically exposes which components of $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ originate from impredicativity, higher-order function spaces, arithmetic infinity, or strict set-truncation.

\subsection{Dropping the Subobject Classifier $\Omega$ (Elementary Topos $\to$ Pretopos and Arithmetic Universe)}

In an elementary topos $\mathcal{E}$, the subobject classifier $1 \xrightarrow{\top} \Omega$ and exponential objects $Y^X$ combine to form impredicative power objects $\mathcal{P}(X) = \Omega^X$. Dropping $\Omega$ places us in the predicative doctrine of locally Cartesian closed pretopoi ($\Pi$-pretopoi) and \emph{Maietti--Joyal Arithmetic Universes}~\cite{Maietti05}.

Without $\Omega$, any residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ previously formulated as an internal order-algebraic property of $\Omega$---such as Booleanness ($\Omega \simeq 1+1$) or De Morgan's Law ($\Omega$ is a Stone lattice)---must be \emph{externalized} into a uniform schematic condition across the fibration of subobject lattices $\mathrm{Sub}_{\mathcal{E}}(X)$ for all $X \in \mathcal{E}$. Note carefully which functor encodes internal choice: in any locally Cartesian closed pretopos, pullback $f^* : \mathcal{E}/Y \to \mathcal{E}/X$ already possesses a right adjoint $\Pi_f$ and therefore preserves colimits and effective epimorphisms unconditionally; internal choice instead constrains the right adjoint $\Pi_f$~\cite{Diaconescu75,Johnstone02}.

\begin{proposition}[Predicative Stability of Diaconescu's Residuum Factor]\label{prop:predicative-diaconescu}%
\uses{thm:diaconescu}%

In any locally Cartesian closed pretopos $\mathcal{E}$ lacking a subobject classifier $\Omega$, the Internal Axiom of Choice ($\mathrm{IAC}$, asserting that the dependent product functors $\Pi_f : \mathcal{E}/X \to \mathcal{E}/Y$, right adjoint to pullback $f^*$, preserve effective epimorphisms) implies that every subobject lattice $\mathrm{Sub}_{\mathcal{E}}(X)$ is a complemented Boolean algebra.

\end{proposition}

\textbf{Significance for $\mathrm{CRM}$.} Proposition~\ref{prop:predicative-diaconescu} proves that the obligatory Booleanness factor in $(A \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC})$ is \emph{predicative}. The collapse of intuitionistic logic under choice does not depend on impredicative comprehension $\Omega^X$, as Diaconescu's pushout construction uses only finite colimits ($X +_U X$), pullbacks, and dependent products ($\Pi$-types). Conversely, as established in Chapter~\ref{ch:non-ac-lattices} (Section~3.5), impredicative comprehension $\Omega$ is isolated as the exact Axis~1 residuum bridging predicative tree recursion ($\mathrm{ATR}_0$ in $\Pi W$-pretopoi) to $\Pi^1_1$-comprehension ($\Pi^1_1\text{-}\mathrm{CA}_0$ in elementary topoi)~\cite{Maietti05}.

\subsection{Dropping Exponentials $Y^X$ (Topos $\to$ Coherent and Regular Categories)}

Dropping local Cartesian closure ($Y^X$ and dependent products $\Pi_f$) descends from pretopoi to \emph{coherent} and \emph{regular categories}~\cite{Johnstone02}. Two structural deformations occur in this regime:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Loss of Internal Choice ($\mathrm{IAC}$):} Because $\mathrm{IAC}$ is defined by preservation of epimorphisms under $\Pi_f$ (or exponentiation $(-)^Z : X^Z \to Y^Z$), $\mathrm{IAC}$ cannot even be formulated internally without function objects. Consequently, bridging local splitting of covers to internal choice in a coherent category $\mathcal{E}$ forces \emph{local Cartesian closure} directly into the residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$.

  \item \textbf{Bifurcation of Epimorphisms:} In an elementary topos, every epimorphism is effective (the coequalizer of its kernel pair). In a general regular or coherent category $\mathcal{A}$ that is not balanced, cancellation epimorphisms are strictly weaker than \emph{regular (effective) epimorphisms}. External choice therefore splits into $\mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{reg}}$ (every regular epimorphism splits) versus full $\mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{epi}}$, with $(\mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{reg}} \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{RegCat}} \mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{epi}})$ isolating \emph{balancedness} (or Barr-exactness/pretopos structure).

\end{itemize}

\subsection{Dropping the Natural Numbers Object $\mathrm{NNO}$ ($\mathbf{FinSet}$ vs.\ Base--Fiber Arithmetic)}

Removing the Natural Numbers Object ($\mathrm{NNO}$) moves $\mathrm{CRM}$ from infinitary universes to \emph{finitary topoi}, such as $\mathbf{FinSet}$ or finite group-action topoi $\mathbf{FinSet}^G$ for a finite group $G$. As calibrated in Chapter~\ref{ch:foundations} (Section~\ref{sec:finset-infinity}), $\mathbf{FinSet} \in \mathbf{ElemTop}$ satisfies both $\mathrm{LEM}$ and full external $\mathrm{AC}$ everywhere, so \emph{every choice residuum vanishes identically}, yet the failure of $\mathrm{NNO}$ ($\neg\mathsf{Inf}$) isolates three pure Axis~III infinity residua inside $\mathrm{Pro}(\mathbf{FinSet})$: the profinite lasso initial-algebra phantom $\widehat{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathrm{lasso}} = \varprojlim_{(p,q)} L_{p,q} \notin \mathbf{FinSet}$ (whose periodic $p=0$ quotient is $\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}$), the Dedekind--Pigeonhole gap $\mathrm{Mono}(X,X)\setminus\mathrm{Iso}(X,X)$, and the biproduct-tail comparison $\bigoplus_{i \in I} A \to \prod_{i \in I} A$ (isomorphic for all $I \in \mathbf{FinSet}$, but precipitating $\prod_{\mathbb{N}} A / \bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}} A \neq 0$ once $\mathrm{NNO}$ enters).

Adding non-trivial symmetries inside a finitary base produces orthogonal mixtures: in $\mathbf{FinSet}^G$ for $G \neq \{1\}$, the topos remains Boolean ($\Omega \simeq 1+1$) and Kuratowski-finite, yet external $\mathrm{AC}$ fails because $1$ is not projective (nontrivial $G$-sets without fixed points admit no global sections). Conversely, in Schanuel's nominal topos $\mathbf{Nom}$, an $\mathrm{NNO}$ exists ($\Delta(\mathbb{N})$) so Axis~III infinity holds, yet on the Kuratowski-infinite atom object $\mathbb{A}$ finite support forces $\mathrm{Mono}_{\mathbf{Nom}}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A}) = \mathrm{Iso}_{\mathbf{Nom}}(\mathbb{A},\mathbb{A})$, cleanly crossing Axis~I ($\neg\mathrm{AC}_\omega$) with Axis~III ($\mathrm{Inf}$).

\subsection{Dropping $0$-Truncation ($1$-Topos $\to$ $(\infty, 1)$-Topos $\mathcal{X}$)}\label{sec:axis1-truncation}

Finally, dropping $0$-truncation of mapping spaces elevates $\mathrm{CRM}$ from $1$-topoi to an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$ equipped with homotopy pullbacks and an univalent object classifier $\mathcal{U}$~\cite{Lurie09}. As shown in Chapter~\ref{ch:choice-atlas} (Section~2.5; Definition~\ref{def:higher-choice}, Theorem~\ref{thm:ac0-acinf}, and Conjecture~\ref{conj:postnikov-step}), removing $0$-truncation fractures any single $1$-categorical choice residuum into a descending deductive chain of truncated choice principles
\[
  \mathrm{AC}_\infty \implies \cdots \implies \mathrm{AC}_n \implies \cdots \implies \mathrm{AC}_1 \implies \mathrm{AC}_0,
\]
with strictly shrinking classes of models $\mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC}_\infty) \subsetneq \cdots \subsetneq \mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC}_n) \subsetneq \cdots \subsetneq \mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC}_0)$. The residuum bridging set-level choice $\mathrm{AC}_0$ to $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$ is precisely the geometric condition that $0$-truncated objects generate $\mathcal{X}$ under effective epimorphisms, while each adjacent step $(\mathrm{AC}_n \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}_{n+1})$ is governed by the vanishing of Giraud non-abelian gerbe cohomology $H^2_{\mathrm{gerbe}}$ ($n=0$) and degree-$(n+2)$ Eilenberg--MacLane obstructions $H^{n+2}$ ($n \ge 1$).

\section{Axis 2 Relativization and Deforming Classical Residuums Without Base Choice or Ultrafilters}

We now turn to Axis~2: varying the metamathematical axioms of the base topos $\mathcal{S}$ over which a Grothendieck or relative topos $\mathcal{E}$ is constructed~\cite{Johnstone02,MacLaneMoerdijk92}.

\subsection{Bounded Geometric Morphisms and Absolute vs.\ Relative Residuums}

Let $\mathcal{S}$ be an elementary base topos, and let $\gamma: \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ be a bounded geometric morphism consisting of a direct image functor $\gamma_*: \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ and a left exact inverse image functor $\gamma^*: \mathcal{S} \to \mathcal{E}$ with $\gamma^* \dashv \gamma_*$, so that $\mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbb{C}, J)$ for an internal site $(\mathbb{C}, J)$ in $\mathcal{S}$. Because $\gamma^*$ preserves the terminal object ($\gamma^*(1_{\mathcal{S}}) \simeq 1_{\mathcal{E}}$), the relative global sections functor $\Gamma_{\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S}}: \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ is canonically isomorphic to the direct image functor $\gamma_* = \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(1_{\mathcal{E}}, -)$.

\begin{definition}[Absolute vs.\ Relative Categorical Residuums]\label{def:abs-rel-residuum}

Let $A$ and $B$ be structural or internal principles evaluated across relative topoi $\gamma: \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$.

\begin{itemize}

  \item A categorical residuum $\mathcal{R} = (A \Rightarrow B)$ is \emph{absolute (base-invariant)} if the equivalence $\mathcal{E} \models A \wedge \mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}) \iff \mathcal{E} \models B$ holds internally for every topos $\mathcal{E}$ over $\mathcal{S}$ regardless of which logical or choice axioms hold in the base topos $\mathcal{S}$.

  \item A categorical residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S}) = (A \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{Topos}/\mathcal{S}} B)$ is \emph{relative (base-sensitive)} if its characterization depends on the metamathematical axioms (such as $\mathrm{AC}$, $\mathrm{DC}$, $\mathrm{BPI}$, or $\mathrm{LEM}$) assumed in the base topos $\mathcal{S}$.

\end{itemize}

\end{definition}

\textbf{Why Internal Residuums are Absolute and External Residuums are Base-Sensitive.} Purely internal implications formulated inside the Mitchell--Bénabou language of $\mathcal{E}$ using finite limits, colimits, and exponentials are \emph{absolute}. Thus Diaconescu's theorem ($\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{IAC} \implies \Omega_{\mathcal{E}} \simeq 1+1$) holds over \emph{any} base topos $\mathcal{S}$, even if $\mathcal{S}$ is intuitionistic and non-Boolean; indeed, $\mathbf{Sh}_{\neg\neg}(\mathcal{S}) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{S}$ is always Boolean. Likewise, internalizing Theorem~\ref{thm:coshep-svc} by calling $Q \in \mathcal{E}$ \emph{internally projective} when $(-)^Q : \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{E}$ preserves epimorphisms---yielding internal enough projectives ($\mathrm{ICOSHEP}$) and internally retract-bounded Small Violations of Choice ($\mathrm{ISVC}_{\mathrm{ret}}$, positing an internally projective cover $P \twoheadrightarrow S$ such that every $X \in \mathcal{E}$ is a retract $r : U \twoheadrightarrow X$ of $U \rightarrowtail C \times P$ for an internally projective $C$)---gives the base-invariant equivalence $\mathrm{ICOSHEP} + (\mathrm{ISVC}_{\mathrm{ret}} \wedge \Omega_{\mathcal{E}} \simeq 1+1) \iff \mathrm{IAC}$ over \emph{any} base $\mathcal{S}$. Indeed, $(-)^{C \times P} \cong ((-)^P)^C$ preserves epimorphisms; if $U \rightarrowtail Q$ in a Boolean topos ($Q \cong U \amalg \neg U$) and $e : A \twoheadrightarrow B$ is epic, setting $e_1 := e \amalg \mathrm{id}_1 : A \amalg 1 \twoheadrightarrow B \amalg 1$ makes $e_1^Q \cong e_1^U \times e_1^{\neg U}$ epic and $\mathrm{pr}_1 : (B \amalg 1)^U \times (B \amalg 1)^{\neg U} \twoheadrightarrow (B \amalg 1)^U$ split epic via $1 \xrightarrow{\mathrm{in}_2} B \amalg 1$, whence $e_1^U$ is epic and its pullback along $\mathrm{in}_1^U : B^U \rightarrowtail (B \amalg 1)^U$ recovers $e^U : A^U \twoheadrightarrow B^U$ as epic by extensivity; and if $r \circ i = \mathrm{id}_X$, then $B^i \circ B^r = \mathrm{id}_{B^X}$ makes $B^i \circ e^U = e^X \circ A^i$ epic, forcing $e^X$ epic (whereas bare subquotient $\mathrm{SVC}$ still fails internally by the quotient fallacy~\cite{Blass79}). By contrast, any external statement $B$ asserting global sections, external projectivity, or spatial points---such as external $\mathrm{COSHEP} \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:coshep-svc}, which additionally requires external projectivity of $1_{\mathcal{E}}$, separated in $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}$ where $\mathrm{IAC}$ holds yet $\Gamma(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}) = \emptyset$)---invokes the direct image functor $\Gamma_{\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S}} = \gamma_* : \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$; because the right adjoint $\gamma_*$ fails to preserve epimorphisms in general, external residuums couple $\mathcal{E}$ directly to the splitting and compactness axioms holding in $\mathcal{S}$.

\subsection{Case 1: Deforming the Barr--Diaconescu--Freyd Residuum Without Base $\mathrm{AC}$}

Over a classical base $\mathcal{S} = \mathbf{Set}$ satisfying $\mathrm{AC}$, Theorem~\ref{thm:bdf} (the Barr--Diaconescu--Freyd theorem~\cite{Barr74,Diaconescu75,Freyd72}) states that a Grothendieck topos $\mathcal{E}$ satisfies external $\mathrm{AC}$ if and only if $\mathcal{E}$ is Boolean and localic over $\mathbf{Set}$ ($\mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}_{\mathbf{Set}}(B)$ for a complete Boolean algebra $B$):
\[
  \top + \bigl(\text{Booleanness } \wedge \text{ Localicity over } \mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{AC}}\bigr) \iff \mathrm{AC}.
\]
When we drop $\mathrm{AC}$ in $\mathcal{S}$ ($\mathcal{S} \not\models \mathrm{AC}$), two fundamental constraints govern $\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{AC}$:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Connected-Base Reflection Obstruction:} Suppose $\gamma : \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ is \emph{connected} ($\gamma^*$ is fully faithful, so $\gamma_* \gamma^* \simeq \mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{S}}$) and $\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{AC}$. Given any epimorphism $e : X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ in $\mathcal{S}$, the pullback $\gamma^* e$ is an epimorphism in $\mathcal{E}$ (since the left adjoint $\gamma^*$ preserves all colimits and hence epimorphisms) and therefore admits a section $\sigma : \gamma^* Y \to \gamma^* X$ in $\mathcal{E}$. Fullness of $\gamma^*$ reflects $\sigma$ back to a section $s : Y \to X$ of $e$ in $\mathcal{S}$, forcing $\mathcal{S} \models \mathrm{AC}$. Consequently, over a base $\mathcal{S} \not\models \mathrm{AC}$, no connected $\mathcal{S}$-topos---not even $\mathrm{id} : \mathcal{S} \to \mathcal{S}$ on a Boolean Cohen--Halpern--Läuchli model---can satisfy $\mathrm{AC}$; $\gamma$ must factor through a non-trivial \emph{choice slice} $\mathcal{S}/U \models \mathrm{AC}$ (or an $\omega$-choice slice when $\mathcal{S} \models \mathrm{DC}$).
  \item \textbf{Atomic Permutation Collapse (Non-Projectivity of $1$):} Inside any connected two-valued atomic permutation topos $\mathbf{Cont}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{G})$ ($\mathrm{Sub}(1) = \{0,1\}$) for a topological group $\mathcal{G}$, the terminal object $1$ is \emph{not} externally projective whenever a proper open subgroup $U \subsetneq \mathcal{G}$ exists, because the quotient $\mathcal{G}/U \twoheadrightarrow 1$ is an epimorphism whose global sections vanish ($\Gamma(\mathcal{G}/U) = (\mathcal{G}/U)^{\mathcal{G}} = \emptyset$). Thus external projectivity of $1$, localicity, $\mathrm{COSHEP}$, $\mathrm{IAC}$, and $\mathrm{AC}$ in $\mathbf{Cont}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{G})$ all collapse simultaneously to the trivial filter of open subgroups $\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{G}) = \{\mathcal{G}\}$ ($\mathbf{Cont}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{G}) \simeq \mathcal{S}$)~\cite{Johnstone02}.
\end{enumerate}

To recover $\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{AC}$ over $\mathcal{S} \not\models \mathrm{AC}$, $\gamma$ must therefore be non-connected over a choice slice and the fiber residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S})$ must absorb the missing base strength:

\begin{theorem}[Deformation of the Barr--Diaconescu--Freyd Residuum Over an Arbitrary Base $\mathcal{S}$]\label{thm:bdf-deformed}%
\uses{thm:bdf,prin:conservation,def:abs-rel-residuum}%

Let $\gamma: \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ be a Grothendieck topos over a base topos $\mathcal{S}$ in which $\mathrm{AC}$ is not assumed.

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Arbitrary Base ($\mathcal{S} \models \top$):} $\mathcal{E}$ satisfies external $\mathrm{AC}$ if and only if $\gamma$ factors through a non-trivial choice slice $\mathcal{S}/U \models \mathrm{AC}$ over which $\mathcal{E}$ is Boolean and localic, and its underlying complete Boolean algebra $B$ is an \emph{internally projective atomic Boolean locale} over a projective generating family in $\mathcal{S}/U$ (so every cover refines to disjoint projective atoms whose global sections split in $\mathcal{S}/U$). In particular, if $\gamma$ is connected (for example $\mathbf{Cont}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{G})$ over $\mathcal{S} \not\models \mathrm{AC}$), $\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{AC}$ is impossible, and in any two-valued atomic permutation topos $\mathbf{Cont}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{G})$ any proper open subgroup $U \subsetneq \mathcal{G}$ forces $\Gamma(\mathcal{G}/U) = \emptyset$ and breaks external projectivity of $1$, localicity, $\mathrm{COSHEP}$, $\mathrm{IAC}$, and $\mathrm{AC}$.

  \item \textbf{Sequential Base ($\mathcal{S} \models \mathrm{DC}$):} Over $\mathcal{S} \models \mathrm{ZF} + \mathrm{DC}$, a Boolean localic $\mathcal{S}$-topos $\mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}_{\mathcal{S}}(B)$ satisfies external $\mathrm{AC}$ if and only if $B$ is an internally projective countably-generated Boolean locale \emph{over an $\omega$-choice slice $\mathcal{S}/U$}, reducing both fiber coverings and slice epimorphisms to countable towers split by base $\mathrm{DC}$.

\end{itemize}

\end{theorem}

\subsection{Case 2: Deforming the Deligne--$\mathrm{BPI}$ Residuum Without Base Ultrafilters}

Over $\mathcal{S} = \mathbf{Set}$, Deligne's theorem~\cite{Deligne72,Johnstone02} states that every coherent Grothendieck topos $\mathcal{E}$ has \emph{enough points} if and only if the Boolean Prime Ideal Theorem ($\mathrm{BPI}$ / Ultrafilter Principle) holds in $\mathcal{S}$. As shown in Chapter~\ref{ch:non-ac-lattices} (Section~3.2), over $\mathcal{S} \models \mathrm{BPI}$ the residuum elevating pointfree localic theorems (such as Johnstone's localic Tychonoff theorem~\cite{Johnstone81} or Mulvey--Pelletier's localic Hahn--Banach theorem~\cite{MulveyPelletier91}) to classical spatial theorems is \emph{finitary coherence} of the underlying locale.

When $\mathcal{S} \not\models \mathrm{BPI}$, finitary compactness no longer yields stalk points $p: \mathcal{S} \to \mathcal{E}$. To guarantee enough points constructively without base ultrafilters, the fiber residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S})$ must strengthen from finitary subcovers to \emph{single-element refinements}:

\begin{theorem}[The Coherence-to-Supercompactness Shift Without Base $\mathrm{BPI}$]\label{thm:deligne-deformed}%
\uses{prin:conservation,def:abs-rel-residuum}%

Let $\gamma: \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ be a localic Grothendieck topos over a base topos $\mathcal{S}$ in which $\mathrm{BPI}$ fails. To bridge pointfree localic compactness theorems ($A$) to spatial point-set theorems ($B$), the topological residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S})$ must strengthen from \emph{finitary coherence} (every covering refines to a finite subcovering) to \emph{supercompactness} (every covering of a generating open refines to a single element, yielding completely prime filters and stalk points constructively without invoking $\mathrm{BPI}$ in $\mathcal{S}$).

\end{theorem}

\section{The Base-Fiber Conservation Principle and the Quantitative Leray Sequence}

The deformations of Theorems~\ref{thm:bdf-deformed} and~\ref{thm:deligne-deformed} realize Principle~\ref{prin:conservation} from Chapter~\ref{ch:foundations} (Section~\ref{sec:base-fiber}) as an exact order-theoretic and cohomological conservation law. Crucially, because the inverse image functor $\gamma^* : \mathcal{S} \to \mathcal{E}$ is left adjoint, it preserves all colimits and epimorphisms unconditionally; non-preservation of epimorphisms and cohomological obstruction strength live instead in the direct image (pushforward) functor $\gamma_* : \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ and its right derived functors $R^q\gamma_*$ ($q \ge 1$).

\begin{principle}[Order-Theoretic Base-Fiber Conservation Principle]\label{thm:conservation-law}

Let $\gamma: \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ be a relative Grothendieck topos over a base topos $\mathcal{S}$, and let $A \implies B$ be an external deductive reversal evaluated in $\mathcal{E}$. The assignment mapping each metamathematical axiom system $\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{S})$ assumed in the base topos $\mathcal{S}$ to the relative fiber residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S}) = (A \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{Topos}/\mathcal{S}} B)$ defines an order-reversing correspondence: weakening the base axiom system from $\mathcal{M}_1(\mathcal{S})$ to $\mathcal{M}_2(\mathcal{S})$ ($\mathbf{Mod}(\mathcal{M}_1) \subsetneq \mathbf{Mod}(\mathcal{M}_2)$) induces a compensatory strengthening of the fiber residuum, transferring the missing metamathematical strength from failures of the pushforward functor $\gamma_*$ and its derived functors $R^q\gamma_*$ to force a strictly stronger topological, atomic, or cohomological rigidity condition:
\[
  \mathbf{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_2(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S})) \subsetneq \mathbf{Mod}(\mathcal{R}_1(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S})).
\]

\end{principle}

Passing from order theory to sheaf cohomology makes this conservation law completely quantitative: the total cohomological obstruction in $\mathcal{E}$ splits cleanly into base and fiber components coupled by a transgression differential.

\begin{theorem}[Quantitative Base--Fiber Conservation via the Leray Sequence]\label{thm:quantitative-conservation}%
\uses{prin:conservation,thm:conservation-law}%

Let $\gamma : \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ be a bounded geometric morphism and $A \in \mathbf{Ab}(\mathcal{E})$ an internal abelian sheaf. The Grothendieck--Leray spectral sequence $E_2^{p,q} = H^p(\mathcal{S}; R^q\gamma_* A) \Longrightarrow H^{p+q}(\mathcal{E}; A)$ induces the exact five-term conservation sequence (where $\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(R^1\gamma_* A) = H^0(\mathcal{S}; R^1\gamma_* A)$):
\[
  \begin{aligned}
    0 \longrightarrow H^1_{\mathrm{base}}(\mathcal{S}; \gamma_* A) &\longrightarrow H^1(\mathcal{E}; A) \longrightarrow \Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(R^1\gamma_* A) \\
    &\xrightarrow{\;d_2\;} H^2_{\mathrm{base}}(\mathcal{S}; \gamma_* A) \longrightarrow H^2(\mathcal{E}; A).
  \end{aligned}
\]
Consequently, for $A$-torsor splitting obstructions $\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{total}}(\mathcal{E}; A) := H^1(\mathcal{E}; A)$, base obstructions $\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{base}}(\mathcal{S}; \gamma_* A) := H^1_{\mathrm{base}}(\mathcal{S}; \gamma_* A)$, and fiber obstructions $\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{fiber}}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S}; A) := \Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(R^1\gamma_* A)$, exactness yields the partition
\[
  \begin{aligned}
    0 &\longrightarrow \mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{base}}(\mathcal{S}; \gamma_* A) \longrightarrow \mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{total}}(\mathcal{E}; A) \\
    &\longrightarrow \ker\bigl(d_2 : \mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{fiber}}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S}; A) \to H^2_{\mathrm{base}}(\mathcal{S}; \gamma_* A)\bigr) \longrightarrow 0.
  \end{aligned}
\]
Weakening the base topos $\mathcal{S}$ enlarges $H^1_{\mathrm{base}}(\mathcal{S}; \gamma_* A)$ and $H^2_{\mathrm{base}}(\mathcal{S}; \gamma_* A)$; thus $H^1(\mathcal{E}; A) = 0$ can be preserved only if the fiber residuum $\Gamma_{\mathcal{S}}(R^1\gamma_* A)$ vanishes or is killed via $R^1\gamma_* = 0$ (such as forcing $B$ in $\mathbf{Sh}_{\mathcal{S}}(B)$ to be projective atomic or supercompact).

\end{theorem}

\subsection{Iterated Geometric Morphisms and Tower Conservation}

Because bounded geometric morphisms compose, Base-Fiber Conservation extends directly to \emph{towers of relative topoi}:
\[
  \mathcal{E}_n \xrightarrow{\gamma_n} \mathcal{E}_{n-1} \xrightarrow{\gamma_{n-1}} \cdots \xrightarrow{\gamma_2} \mathcal{E}_1 \xrightarrow{\gamma_1} \mathcal{S}.
\]
Along any composite geometric morphism $\gamma = \gamma_1 \circ \cdots \circ \gamma_n : \mathcal{E}_n \to \mathcal{S}$, splitting an epimorphism or constructing a spatial point in $\mathcal{E}_n$ is governed step-by-step by the iterated Leray sequences of $\gamma_k : \mathcal{E}_k \to \mathcal{E}_{k-1}$: a theorem $B$ holds in $\mathcal{E}_n$ either by assuming strong choice in $\mathcal{S}$ together with mild localic Booleanness in each fiber, or over a purely constructive base $\mathcal{S}$ by enforcing supercompactness, atomicity, and cohomological vanishing $H^1(\mathcal{E}_k/\mathcal{E}_{k-1}; G_k) = 0$ at every level.

\section{The $E_0$ Tail Residuum, Noncommutative Smooth Moduli, and the Unclassifiability of Exotic $\mathbb{R}^4$'s}\label{sec:exotic-r4-e0}

The Base-Fiber Conservation Principle is not confined to classical topos extensions of $\mathrm{ZF}$: in topological and geometric analysis, forbidding discontinuous Choice ($\neg\mathrm{AC}$) over Polish spaces immediately reveals hyperfinite residua governing the classification of smooth $4$-manifolds. Recall from the opening pages of our Preface that Alain Connes's irrational-slope torus foliation $\mathbb{T}^2 / \mathcal{F}_\theta$ collapses in $\mathbf{Set}$ while remaining a sharp noncommutative quotient stack; in the profinite setting, its exact hyperfinite twin is the Cantor tail quotient $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0} = 2^{\mathbb{N}} \sslash E_0$.

\subsection{Compactness vs.\ Infinite Ends ($=_{\mathbb{N}}$ vs.\ $E_0$)}

Comparing closed smooth manifolds to open $4$-manifolds isolates a dramatic phase transition along Axis~III ($\mathrm{Inf}$) and Axis~I ($\neg\mathrm{AC}$):
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Compact Smooth Manifolds ($\neg\mathrm{Inf}$ on charts $\implies \, =_{\mathbb{N}}$):} Bergfalk and Smythe~\cite[Theorem~A]{BergfalkSmythe25} prove that diffeomorphism of \emph{compact} smooth $n$-manifolds ($n \ge 0$) is Borel equivalent to equality $=_{\mathbb{N}}$ on $\mathbb{N}$---represented internally by the discrete $0$-sheaf $\Delta(\mathbb{N})$ with vanishing residuum.
  \item \textbf{Radial Exotic $\mathbb{R}^4$'s vs.\ Full $\mathcal{R}^4$ ($\mathrm{Inf} + \neg\mathrm{AC} \implies E_0$):} In stark contrast, on Gompf and Panagiotopoulos's~\cite{GompfPanagiotopoulos26} explicit radial family $\{M_x\}_{x \in 2^{\mathbb{N}}}$ parameterized along a compact exhaustion $K_0 \Subset K_1 \Subset \cdots \subset \mathbb{R}^4$, oriented diffeomorphism satisfies $M_x \cong_{\mathrm{Diff}} M_y \iff x \,E_0\, y$. On the full moduli space $\mathcal{R}^4$ of all smoothings of $\mathbb{R}^4$, eventual tail equality $E_0$ Borel-reduces into oriented diffeomorphism ($E_0 \le_B \mathrm{Diff}(\mathcal{R}^4)$), embedding $2^{\mathbb{N}}/E_0$ as a Borel sub-moduli problem (climbing further above $=^+$ on general open $4$-manifolds, while remaining bounded above by continuous $S_\infty = \mathrm{Sym}(\mathbb{N})$ orbit equivalence). By the Harrington--Kechris--Louveau $E_0$-dichotomy~\cite{HKL90,Kechris95}, no Borel Polish or real-valued invariant can classify smooth structures on $\mathbb{R}^4$.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{The Double-Solvent Theorem for $4$-Dimensional Smoothings}

Identifying $2^{\mathbb{N}} \cong \prod_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2$ recovers the exact Axis~III biproduct-tail quotient of Chapter~\ref{ch:foundations} (Section~\ref{sec:finset-infinity}) at $A = \mathbb{F}_2$:
\[
  0 \longrightarrow \bigoplus_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2 \longrightarrow \prod_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2 \xrightarrow{\;q\;} \mathcal{Q}_{E_0} \longrightarrow 0.
\]
Smooth ends of $\mathbb{R}^4$ sit suspended inside Clausen and Scholze's light condensed topos $\mathbf{Sh}(\mathrm{Prof}_{\aleph_0})$ (sheaves on countably presented profinite sets) between two opposite foundational collapses:

\begin{theorem}[The Double-Solvent Theorem for $4$-Dimensional Smoothings]\label{thm:double-solvent-r4}
\lean{HomotopyCRM.exists_zfc_linear_section_e0Quotient, HomotopyCRM.nonempty_linearEquiv_cantor_prod_e0Quotient, HomotopyCRM.dense_e0Coset, HomotopyCRM.continuous_e0Invariant_const, HomotopyCRM.not_exists_continuous_t1_e0Separator, HomotopyCRM.chapter4_borel_tail_residuum_summary}%
\leanok%
\uses{def:comparison-triple,def:residuum,prin:conservation}%

In the light condensed topos $\mathbf{Sh}(\mathrm{Prof}_{\aleph_0})$, the $E_0$ smooth-end modulus $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0} = 2^{\mathbb{N}} \sslash E_0 = \bigl[\prod_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2 \sslash \bigoplus_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2\bigr]_{\mathrm{Cond}}$ mediates between topological and set-theoretic collapse:
\[
  \mathbf{Top}(\mathbb{R}^4) \simeq * \;\longleftarrow\; \Bigl[\prod_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2 \;\sslash\; \bigoplus_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2\Bigr]_{\mathrm{Cond}} \;\xrightarrow{\;\mathrm{AC}\;}\; \mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{ZFC}}(\pi_0) \cong \mathbb{R},
\]
according to three complementary regimes:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Left Solvent (Freedman $\nu F_{\mathrm{CH}}$ Collapse):} Passing from finite smooth handlebody trees ($\mu F$) to topological Casson handles absorbs infinitely many stages simultaneously, collapsing $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}$ to $*$ in $\mathbf{Top}(\mathbb{R}^4)$.
  \item \textbf{Right Solvent ($\mathrm{ZFC}$ Vitali Collapse):} Invoking full external $\mathrm{AC}$ (Zorn's Lemma) splits $q$ via a discontinuous $\mathbb{F}_2$-linear Hamel section $s_{\mathrm{AC}}$, producing an artificial cardinal classifier $|\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}| = |\mathbb{R}|$ that destroys smooth-invariant definability.
  \item \textbf{Light Condensed / Baire Topos Residuum:} Over $\mathbf{Sh}(\mathrm{Prof}_{\aleph_0})$, compact interior isotopies absorb $\bigoplus_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2$. Density of $\bigoplus_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2$ forces separated Polish reflection collapse $\pi_0^{\mathrm{Pol}}(\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}) \simeq 1$ (Baire/sheaf blindness of Polish real invariants), while Base--Fiber Conservation precipitates the non-splitting condensed torsor class
  \[
    [q] \neq 0 \;\in\; \mathrm{Ext}^1_{\mathrm{CondAb}}\Bigl(\mathcal{Q}_{E_0},\, \bigoplus_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2\Bigr),
  \]
  paired with Panagiotopoulos's non-vanishing definable derived limit $\varprojlim^1_{\mathrm{def}}$ along compact exhaustions $K_0 \Subset K_1 \Subset \cdots \subset \mathbb{R}^4$.
\end{enumerate}

\end{theorem}

The algebraic splitting, Cantor-space density collapse, and continuous $T_1$ blindness of Theorem~\ref{thm:double-solvent-r4} are machine-checked in Lean~4 (\texttt{HomotopyCRM/ BorelTailResiduum.lean}, 682 lines, 19 \texttt{\#print axioms} checks under the 4-Layer Definition Check Gate): the module formalizes the short exact sequence $0 \to (\mathbb{N} \to_0 \mathrm{ZMod}\;2) \to (\mathbb{N} \to \mathrm{ZMod}\;2) \xrightarrow{q} \mathcal{Q}_{E_0} \to 0$ (\texttt{exact\_ finFlipIncl\_ e0QuotientMk}), certifies the discontinuous $\mathsf{ZFC}$ linear right-inverse section via \texttt{Submodule.exists\_ isCompl} (\texttt{exists\_ zfc\_ linear\_ section\_ e0Quotient} and \texttt{nonempty\_ linearEquiv\_ cantor\_ prod\_ e0Quotient}), proves coordinatewise coset density in the product Cantor topology without Hamel bases (\texttt{dense\_ e0Coset}), and deduces that every continuous $E_0$-invariant map into a $T_1$ space is constant (\texttt{continuous\_ e0Invariant\_ const} and \texttt{not\_ exists\_ continuous\_ t1\_ e0Separator}).

\subsection{Noncommutative $C^*$-Moduli ($\mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty)$), Glimm's Dichotomy, and the $E_0^{\mathrm{even}}$ Parity Grading}\label{subsec:uhf-cstar-e0}

The Polish blindness $\pi_0^{\mathrm{Pol}}(\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}) \simeq 1$ admits an exact noncommutative geometric reformulation~\cite{Connes94}. Viewing Cantor space $2^{\mathbb{N}} \cong \prod_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2$ under the action of compact interior isotopies (finite coordinate flips $\bigoplus_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2$), orbit density collapses the commutative fixed-point subalgebra of continuous invariants to scalars:
\[
  C(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{\bigoplus_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2} \;=\; \mathbb{C}\cdot 1,
\]
reflecting $\pi_0^{\mathrm{Pol}}(\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}) \simeq 1$. In Connes's noncommutative geometry, rather than discarding the equivalences, one forms the étale groupoid $\mathcal{G}_{E_0} := 2^{\mathbb{N}} \rtimes \bigoplus_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2$ and evaluates its groupoid convolution $C^*$-algebra in two elementary steps:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item At each collar stage $n \in \mathbb{N}$, diagonal functions $C(\mathbb{F}_2)$ together with the Pauli-$X$ flip unitary $U_n = \bigl(\begin{smallmatrix} 0 & 1 \\ 1 & 0 \end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$ satisfy the covariant crossed-product relations generating $C(\mathbb{F}_2) \rtimes \mathbb{F}_2 \cong M_2(\mathbb{C})$.
  \item Forming the infinite inductive tensor product across all collar stages $n \in \mathbb{N}$ identifies the groupoid $C^*$-algebra of $\mathcal{G}_{E_0}$ with Glimm's \textbf{uniformly hyperfinite CAR algebra} $\mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty)$:
  \[
    \begin{aligned}
      C^*(\mathcal{G}_{E_0}) \;=\; C(2^{\mathbb{N}}) \rtimes \bigoplus_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2
      &\;\cong\; \bigotimes_{n=0}^{\infty} M_2(\mathbb{C}) \\
      &\;=\; \varinjlim_N M_{2^N}(\mathbb{C}) \;=\; \mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty).
    \end{aligned}
  \]
\end{enumerate}

Unlike the collapsed commutative invariant ring $\mathbb{C}\cdot 1$, the noncommutative modulus $\mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty)$ is a simple $C^*$-algebra with trivial center $\mathbb{C}\cdot 1$ that retains complete ordered $K$-theoretic memory of dyadic smooth-handle subdivisions:
\[
  \begin{aligned}
    \bigl(K_0(\mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty)),\; K_0(\mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty))^+,\; [1]\bigr) &\;\cong\; \bigl(\mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}],\; \mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}]_{\ge 0},\; 1\bigr), \\
    K_1(\mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty)) &\;=\; 0.
  \end{aligned}
\]
Moreover, under GNS completion with respect to its unique tracial state, both Rieffel's irrational rotation algebra $A_\theta$ (representing Connes's torus foliation $\mathbb{T}^2/\mathcal{F}_\theta$ from the Preface) and $\mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty)$ complete to the Murray--von~Neumann \textbf{hyperfinite $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor} $\mathcal{R}$~\cite{Connes94}---turning the Preface analogy between $\mathbb{T}^2/\mathcal{F}_\theta$ and $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}$ into an exact von~Neumann isomorphism on tracial closures.

This operator-algebraic translation illuminates the historical origin of $E_0$: Glimm~\cite{Glimm61} proved that a separable $C^*$-algebra $A$ is non-Type-I (equivalently, its unitary dual $\widehat{A}$ fails to be a standard Borel space) if and only if $\mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty)$ embeds as a subquotient; Effros and Harrington--Kechris--Louveau~\cite{HKL90} translated Glimm's theorem directly into the $E_0$-dichotomy of descriptive set theory. Thus $E_0 \le_B \mathrm{Diff}(\mathcal{R}^4)$ expresses in precise geometric form that $4$-dimensional smoothings cross Glimm's boundary from commutative Type-I moduli into genuinely noncommutative geometry.

\begin{remark}[Pauli-$Z$ Parity Grading ($E_0^{\mathrm{even}}$) and Anchor Witness~I ($\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}}$)]\label{rem:pauli-z-parity}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.dense_evenE0Coset, HomotopyCRM.not_exists_continuous_flipAlternating, HomotopyCRM.exists_zfc_linear_flipAlternating, HomotopyCRM.evenToE0Quotient_surjective, HomotopyCRM.exists_zfc_linear_section_evenToE0Quotient}%
\leanok%
\uses{thm:double-solvent-r4}%
Restricting finite coordinate flips to the kernel $\mathrm{Even}_0(\mathbb{N}) := \ker\bigl(\Sigma : \bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2 \to \mathbb{F}_2\bigr)$ of even-cardinality supports defines the subrelation $E_0^{\mathrm{even}} \subset E_0$ and the $\mathbb{F}_2$-torsor exact sequence
\[
  0 \longrightarrow \mathbb{F}_2 \longrightarrow 2^{\mathbb{N}}/E_0^{\mathrm{even}} \xrightarrow{\;\pi\;} \mathcal{Q}_{E_0} \longrightarrow 0.
\]
In light-condensed or Baire universes ($\mathsf{ZF}+\mathsf{DC}_\omega+\mathsf{BP}$), neither $q : 2^{\mathbb{N}} \twoheadrightarrow \mathcal{Q}_{E_0}$ nor the double cover $\pi$ splits; by contrast, the Boolean Prime Ideal Theorem ($\mathsf{BPI}$) extends the finite-support parity sum $\Sigma$ to a discontinuous homomorphism splitting $\pi$ while remaining strictly independent of full $E_0$-transversals (Geschke--Lubarsky--Rahn; Serafin~\cite{Serafin23}). In \texttt{HomotopyCRM/ BorelTailResiduum.lean}, we machine-check both sides of this parity dichotomy: every $E_0^{\mathrm{even}}$-coset is already dense in Cantor space (\texttt{dense\_ evenE0Coset}), so \emph{no continuous} single-flip-alternating functional $\chi : 2^{\mathbb{N}} \to \mathbb{F}_2$ can exist (\texttt{not\_ exists\_ continuous\_ flipAlternating}), whereas Zorn's Lemma ($\mathsf{ZFC}$ / \texttt{Classical.choice}) produces discontinuous $\mathbb{F}_2$-linear sections splitting both $\pi$ and parity evaluation (\texttt{exists\_ zfc\_ linear\_ flipAlternating}, \texttt{exists\_ zfc\_ linear\_ section\_ evenToE0Quotient}, \texttt{chapter4\_ borel\_ tail\_ residuum\_ summary}). On the $C^*$-algebra $\mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty)$, the diagonal Pauli-$Z$ conjugation automorphism
\[
  \alpha_{-1} \;:=\; \bigotimes_{n=0}^{\infty} \mathrm{Ad}\,\begin{pmatrix} 1 & 0 \\ 0 & -1 \end{pmatrix}
\]
acts on finite-flip unitaries $U_S = \prod_{n \in S} U_n$ by $\alpha_{-1}(U_S) = (-1)^{|S|} U_S$: its $+1$ fixed-point subalgebra is precisely $C^*(\mathcal{G}_{E_0^{\mathrm{even}}})$, while its $-1$ eigenspace forms the odd-flip imprimitivity bimodule---yielding the exact operator-algebraic counterpart of Anchor Witness~I, the nominal odd-finset parity torsor $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}} \in H^1(\mathbf{Nom}; \mathrm{EvenFinset})$ of Chapter~\ref{ch:realizability-sites}.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The Five-Rung CRM--Borel Complexity Dictionary}

Embedding Borel equivalence relations as condensed and nominal quotient stacks calibrates Borel reducibility directly against the Categorical Reverse Mathematics residuum hierarchy, summarized in Table~\ref{tab:crm-borel-dictionary} and illustrated around the $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}$ hub in Figure~\ref{fig:exotic-r4-hub}. Remarkably, replacing additive direct sums by finite-prefix sequences inside $\mathcal{Q} \subset \prod_{k \ge 1} \mathcal{A}_k$ reproduces this same tail residuum at the summit of directed higher category theory: the step-product Postnikov phantom $X_\bullet \in RL(\mathcal{Q}) \setminus \mathcal{Q}$ of Chapter~\ref{ch:higher-crm} (Theorem~\ref{thm:step-product-counterexample}) is the directed $(\infty,\infty)$-categorical twin of $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}$, while Horizon~VII in Chapter~\ref{ch:master-atlas} isolates the open problems linking $\mathrm{Ext}^1_{\mathrm{CondAb}}$, $\varprojlim^1_{\mathrm{def}}$, and pro-oligomorphic blindness.

\section{Summary of Base-Fiber Residuum Deformations and Topological Moduli}

\begin{center}\emph{[Summary ledger table omitted in blueprint edition; see Royal Octavo monograph PDF.]}\end{center}

\part{Applications to $(\infty,\infty)$-Categories, Fra\"iss\'e Topoi, and Formal Verification}

\chapter{Higher Categorical Reverse Mathematics in $(\infty, 1)$-Topoi, Univalent Foundations, and Postnikov Obstruction Towers}

\label{ch:higher-crm}

\section{From 1-Topoi to $(\infty, 1)$-Topoi and Univalent Universes}\label{sec:higher-topoi-intro}

In the first four chapters of this book, our ambient mathematical universes were primarily elementary and Grothendieck 1-topoi $\mathcal{E}$. In a 1-topos, hom-sets $\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(X, Y)$ are discrete $0$-truncated sets, equality of morphisms is a strict proposition, and the subobject classifier $\Omega$ classifies $(-1)$-truncated monomorphisms. As established along Axis 1 of our relativization framework (Chapter~\ref{ch:base-fiber}, Sections~\ref{sec:two-axis-relativization} and~\ref{sec:axis1-truncation}), removing the requirement of $0$-truncation elevates Categorical Reverse Mathematics ($\mathrm{CRM}$) from 1-topoi to an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$ (in the sense of Joyal, Lurie~\cite{Lurie09}, Rezk, and To\"en--Vezzosi).

An $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$ is a locally presentable (or elementary) $(\infty, 1)$-category equipped with homotopy limits and colimits, universal colimits (pullback-stable effective epimorphisms), descent (van Kampen colimits), and a hierarchy of \emph{object classifiers} $\mathcal{U}$. Its internal language is \emph{Homotopy Type Theory (HoTT)} and Voevodsky's \emph{Univalent Foundations}, in which objects $X \in \mathcal{X}$ are interpreted not as static sets, but as homotopy types ($\infty$-groupoids) stratified by their \emph{truncation level}:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{$(-2)$-Truncated Objects:} Contractible types $X \simeq 1$, possessing a unique point up to coherent higher homotopy.

  \item \textbf{$(-1)$-Truncated Objects:} Mere propositions, whose equivalence classes form the internal subobject classifier $\Omega = \mathcal{U}_{\le -1}$.

  \item \textbf{$0$-Truncated Objects:} Internal discrete sets, whose identity types are $(-1)$-truncated propositions and which form the underlying 1-topos $\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$.

  \item \textbf{$n$-Truncated Objects ($n \ge 1$):} Homotopy $n$-types ($n$-groupoids) whose homotopy groups $\pi_k(X, x)$ vanish for all $k > n$.

\end{itemize}

For each integer $n \ge -2$, the inclusion of $n$-truncated objects $\tau_{\le n}\mathcal{X} \hookrightarrow \mathcal{X}$ admits a left adjoint truncation functor $\tau_{\le n}: \mathcal{X} \to \tau_{\le n}\mathcal{X}$. Similarly, a morphism $f: X \to Y$ in $\mathcal{X}$ is $n$-truncated if its homotopy fibers $\mathrm{fib}_y(f) = X \times_Y \{y\}$ are $n$-truncated objects in the slice $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}/Y$.

Passing from $\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$ to the full $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$ transforms the Fundamental Equivalence of Categorical Reverse Mathematics, $A + \mathcal{R}(\mathcal{X}) \iff B$. In a 1-topos, an epimorphism $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ is simply a map with non-empty $(-1)$-truncated support, and splitting $e$ is a single 1-categorical problem. In an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$, an \emph{effective epimorphism} $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ is a morphism whose $(-1)$-truncation $\tau_{\le -1}(e): \tau_{\le -1}(X \to Y) \xrightarrow{\sim} Y$ is an equivalence in $\mathcal{X}/Y$. Because the homotopy fibers of $e$ can possess infinitely many non-vanishing higher homotopy groups $\pi_k(\mathrm{fib}_y(e))$, any 1-categorical reversal fractures into an infinite tower of higher homotopical and cohomological residuums.

\section{The Higher Choice Hierarchy ($\mathrm{AC}_{n, m}$) and the Global Infinity-Choice Residuum}\label{sec:higher-choice}

To map the Choice-Geometry Residuum Atlas inside an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$, we must first observe how truncation levels interact with the domain and codomain of an effective epimorphism $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$.

\begin{definition}[Bigraded Higher Choice]\label{def:bigraded-choice}

Let $\mathcal{X}$ be an $(\infty, 1)$-topos, and let $-1 \le n, m \le \infty$. We say that $\mathcal{X}$ satisfies the Axiom of $(n, m)$-Choice, denoted $\mathrm{AC}_{n, m}$, if every $n$-truncated effective epimorphism $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ whose codomain $Y$ is an $m$-truncated object admits a section $s: Y \to X$ ($e \circ s \simeq \mathrm{id}_Y$).

\end{definition}

Why did our definition of the higher choice spine $\mathrm{AC}_n$ in Chapter~\ref{ch:choice-atlas} (Definition~\ref{def:higher-choice}) restrict the codomain $Y$ to be $0$-truncated ($\mathrm{AC}_n = \mathrm{AC}_{n, 0}$)? The answer lies in a fundamental homotopical obstruction that occurs as soon as the codomain $Y$ is allowed to have non-trivial fundamental groups ($m \ge 1$):

\begin{proposition}[Degeneracy of Un-Truncated Choice]\label{prop:untruncated-degeneracy}

If an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$ satisfies $\mathrm{AC}_{0, 1}$ (every $0$-truncated effective epimorphism onto a $1$-truncated codomain splits), then every connected $1$-groupoid in $\mathcal{X}$ is contractible, collapsing all $1$-truncated homotopy types into $0$-truncated sets.

\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let $G$ be any non-trivial $0$-truncated group in $\mathcal{X}$ (for example, $G = \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}$), and let $BG = K(G, 1)$ be its delooping $1$-groupoid. Because $BG$ is $0$-connected ($\tau_{\le 0}(BG) \simeq 1$), the basepoint inclusion $e: 1 \twoheadrightarrow BG$ is a $0$-truncated effective epimorphism (its homotopy fiber is the discrete set $G$) onto a $1$-truncated codomain $BG$. If $\mathrm{AC}_{0, 1}$ held in $\mathcal{X}$, $e$ would admit a section $s: BG \to 1$ with $e \circ s \simeq \mathrm{id}_{BG}$, forcing $BG \simeq 1$ and $G \simeq \Omega(BG) \simeq 1$.
\end{proof}

Proposition~\ref{prop:untruncated-degeneracy} proves that demanding sections over un-truncated codomains ($m \ge 1$) is not a choice principle---it is an anti-homotopical truncation axiom that destroys higher groupoids. Consequently, genuine Higher Categorical Reverse Mathematics calibrates the spine where the codomain $Y$ is a $0$-truncated set ($m = 0$) while the fiber truncation level $n$ climbs from $0$ to $\infty$:

\[
  \mathrm{AC}_\infty \implies \cdots \implies \mathrm{AC}_{n+1} \implies \mathrm{AC}_n \implies \cdots \implies \mathrm{AC}_1 \implies \mathrm{AC}_0,
\]

with strictly shrinking classes of models $\mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC}_\infty) \subsetneq \cdots \subsetneq \mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC}_n) \subsetneq \cdots \subsetneq \mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC}_0)$, where $\mathrm{AC}_n := \mathrm{AC}_{n, 0}$ and $\mathrm{AC}_\infty := \mathrm{AC}_{\infty, 0}$.

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Set-Level Choice ($\mathrm{AC}_0$):} Every $0$-truncated effective epimorphism $X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ onto a $0$-truncated set $Y$ splits; this is equivalent to the classical External Axiom of Choice ($\mathrm{AC}$) in the underlying 1-topos $\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$.

  \item \textbf{Stack/Groupoid Choice ($\mathrm{AC}_1$):} Every $1$-truncated effective epimorphism $X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ (a stack of 1-groupoids with non-empty connected components) over a $0$-truncated set $Y$ admits a global section.

  \item \textbf{Infinity-Choice ($\mathrm{AC}_\infty$):} Every effective epimorphism $X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ from an arbitrary $\infty$-groupoid $X$ onto a $0$-truncated set $Y$ admits a section (equivalently, every $0$-truncated object $Y \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$ is projective in the full $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$).

\end{itemize}

When we ask for the global categorical residuum $(\mathrm{AC}_0 \Rightarrow_{(\infty,1)\text{-}\mathbf{Topos}} \mathrm{AC}_\infty)$ that elevates set-level choice in $\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$ to full infinity-choice across $\mathcal{X}$, we obtain the higher geometric counterpart of localicity:

\begin{theorem}[Global Infinity-Choice Residuum]\label{thm:global-inf-choice}%
\uses{def:bigraded-choice,prop:untruncated-degeneracy,thm:bdf}%

In any $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$, the categorical residuum bridging $0$-truncated choice ($\mathrm{AC}_0$) to full infinity-choice ($\mathrm{AC}_\infty$) is the geometric condition that $0$-truncated objects (sets) cover $\mathcal{X}$:

\[
  \mathrm{AC}_0 + \bigl(\forall X \in \mathcal{X},\, \exists S \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X} \text{ with } S \twoheadrightarrow X\bigr) \iff \mathrm{AC}_\infty
\]

\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Suppose $\mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_\infty$. Then $\mathrm{AC}_0$ holds as a special case. Moreover, for any object $X \in \mathcal{X}$, the $0$-truncation unit $\eta_X: X \twoheadrightarrow \tau_{\le 0}X$ is an effective epimorphism onto the $0$-truncated object $\tau_{\le 0}X \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$. By $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$, $\eta_X$ admits a section $s: \tau_{\le 0}X \to X$ with $\eta_X \circ s \simeq \mathrm{id}_{\tau_{\le 0}X}$. Because $\tau_{\le 0}(s) = \mathrm{id}_{\tau_{\le 0}X}$ is an effective epimorphism, $s: \tau_{\le 0}X \twoheadrightarrow X$ is an effective epimorphism from a $0$-truncated object covering $X$ (Lurie~\cite{Lurie09}, \emph{Higher Topos Theory}, Prop.~7.2.1.14). Conversely, suppose $\mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_0$ and every object $X \in \mathcal{X}$ is covered by a $0$-truncated set $q: S \twoheadrightarrow X$ with $S \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$. Let $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ be an effective epimorphism onto a $0$-truncated object $Y \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$. Composing $e$ with a $0$-truncated cover $q: S \twoheadrightarrow X$ yields an effective epimorphism $e \circ q: S \twoheadrightarrow Y$ between $0$-truncated objects, which is a $0$-truncated effective epimorphism in $\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$. By $\mathrm{AC}_0$, $e \circ q$ admits a section $\sigma: Y \to S$, so $s = q \circ \sigma: Y \to X$ is a section of $e$ ($e \circ s \simeq \mathrm{id}_Y$), proving $\mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_\infty$.
\end{proof}

\section{Postnikov Towers, $k$-Invariants, and the Stepwise Residuum ($\mathrm{AC}_n \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}_{n+1}$)}\label{sec:postnikov-residuum}

Theorem~\ref{thm:global-inf-choice} characterizes the global transition from $\mathrm{AC}_0$ to $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$, but $\mathrm{CRM}$ also seeks the fine-grained \emph{stepwise residuum} $\mathcal{R}_n(\mathcal{X}) = (\mathrm{AC}_n \Rightarrow_{(\infty,1)\text{-}\mathbf{Topos}} \mathrm{AC}_{n+1})$ between adjacent rungs of the Postnikov ladder.

Let $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ be an $(n+1)$-truncated effective epimorphism onto a $0$-truncated codomain $Y \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$. In the slice $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}/Y$, the relative Postnikov truncation functors factor $e$ into a finite tower of fibrations over $Y$:

\[
  X \simeq \tau_{\le n+1}(X/Y) \xrightarrow{p_{n+1}} \tau_{\le n}(X/Y) \xrightarrow{p_n} \cdots \xrightarrow{p_1} \tau_{\le 0}(X/Y) \xrightarrow{e_0} Y
\]

Assume that $\mathcal{X}$ satisfies $\mathrm{AC}_n$. Because $\tau_{\le n}(X/Y) \twoheadrightarrow Y$ is an $n$-truncated effective epimorphism over $Y$, $\mathrm{AC}_n$ guarantees the existence of a partial section $s_n: Y \to \tau_{\le n}(X/Y)$.

To elevate $\mathrm{AC}_n$ to $\mathrm{AC}_{n+1}$, we must determine the exact obstruction to lifting $s_n: Y \to \tau_{\le n}(X/Y)$ across the $(n+1)$-st Postnikov stage $p_{n+1}: \tau_{\le n+1}(X/Y) \twoheadrightarrow \tau_{\le n}(X/Y)$. Pulling back $p_{n+1}$ along the section $s_n: Y \to \tau_{\le n}(X/Y)$ produces a pullback square in $\mathcal{X}/Y$:

\[
  E_{n+1} := Y \times_{\tau_{\le n}(X/Y)} \tau_{\le n+1}(X/Y) \xrightarrow{\pi_{n+1}} Y
\]

By higher topos cohort theory (Lurie~\cite{Lurie09}, \emph{Higher Topos Theory}, Section~7.2), the pullback fibration $\pi_{n+1}: E_{n+1} \twoheadrightarrow Y$ is an $(n+1)$-gerbe over $Y$ banded by the internal homotopy group object $\Pi_{n+1} = s_n^*\bigl(\pi_{n+1}(X/Y)\bigr)$. Here we must explicitly separate the non-abelian base rung $n = 0$ from the higher abelian rungs $n \ge 1$:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{For $n = 0$ (Non-Abelian Bands):} The band $\Pi_1 = s_0^*\bigl(\pi_1(X/Y)\bigr)$ is a general (possibly non-abelian) group object in $\tau_{\le 0}(\mathcal{X}/Y)$, for which a second delooping $B^2\Pi_1$ need not exist; the pullback $1$-gerbe $E_1 \twoheadrightarrow Y$ is instead classified by Giraud's non-abelian gerbe cohomology $H^2_{\mathrm{gerbe}}(\mathcal{X}/Y, \Pi_1)$~\cite{Giraud71}.
  \item \textbf{For $n \ge 1$ (Abelian Bands):} The band $\Pi_{n+1} = s_n^*\bigl(\pi_{n+1}(X/Y)\bigr)$ is an abelian group object in $\tau_{\le 0}(\mathcal{X}/Y)$, so the Eilenberg--MacLane delooping $K(\Pi_{n+1}, n+2) = B^{n+2}\Pi_{n+1}$ exists and classifies the $(n+1)$-gerbe obstruction via a unique Postnikov $k$-invariant:
  \[
    \kappa_{n+1}(e, s_n) \in \pi_0\,\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}/Y}\bigl(Y, K(\Pi_{n+1}, n+2)\bigr) = H^{n+2}(\mathcal{X}/Y, \Pi_{n+1})
  \]
\end{itemize}

In either regime, the section $s_n$ lifts to an $(n+1)$-stage section $s_{n+1}: Y \to \tau_{\le n+1}(X/Y)$ if and only if the pullback gerbe $E_{n+1} \twoheadrightarrow Y$ is trivial, which occurs if and only if its corresponding gerbe or Eilenberg--MacLane cohomology class vanishes. This establishes the precise mechanism behind the fourth core conjecture of our program:

\begin{conjecture}[Postnikov Stepwise Residuum]\label{conj:postnikov-stepwise}

For every integer $n \ge 0$ in an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$, the stepwise categorical residuum $\mathcal{R}_n(\mathcal{X}) = (\mathrm{AC}_n \Rightarrow_{(\infty,1)\text{-}\mathbf{Topos}} \mathrm{AC}_{n+1})$ satisfying

\[
  \mathrm{AC}_n + \mathcal{R}_n(\mathcal{X}) \iff \mathrm{AC}_{n+1}
\]

is equivalent to the cohomological vanishing condition that for every $0$-truncated object $Y \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$, the $(n+1)$-gerbe obstruction vanishes on a $0$-truncated projective cover of $Y$: specifically, the vanishing of Giraud's non-abelian gerbe cohomology $H^2_{\mathrm{gerbe}}(\mathcal{X}/Y, \Pi_1)$ for every group band $\Pi_1$ when $n = 0$, and the vanishing of Eilenberg--MacLane cohomology $H^{n+2}(\mathcal{X}/Y, \Pi_{n+1})$ for every abelian group band $\Pi_{n+1}$ when $n \ge 1$.

\end{conjecture}

In particular, the first two rungs of the Postnikov ladder specialize to classical geometric invariants:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Rung 0 ($\top \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}_0$):} Splitting $0$-truncated surjections of sets in $\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$, governed by 1-topos Booleanness, localicity, and vanishing of torsor cohomology $H^1(\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}/Y, G) = 0$.

  \item \textbf{Rung 1 ($\mathrm{AC}_0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}_1$):} Splitting $1$-groupoid stacks over sets $Y$, governed by the vanishing of non-abelian $1$-gerbe obstructions $H^2_{\mathrm{gerbe}}(\mathcal{X}/Y, G) = 0$ (Giraud's non-abelian cohomology).

  \item \textbf{Higher Rungs ($\mathrm{AC}_n \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}_{n+1}$ for $n \ge 1$):} Splitting $(n+1)$-types over $n$-types, governed purely by abelian Eilenberg--MacLane sheaf cohomology $H^{n+2}(\mathcal{X}/Y, A) = 0$ for internal abelian groups $A \in \mathbf{Ab}(\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}/Y)$.

\end{itemize}

\section{Hypercompleteness, Whitehead's Principle, and the $\infty$-Connected Residuum}

A subtle phenomenon emerges at the transfinite limit $n \to \infty$ of the Postnikov tower. Suppose an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$ satisfies $\mathrm{AC}_n$ for \emph{every} finite integer $n \ge 0$ ($\forall n < \infty,\, \mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_n$). Does $\mathcal{X}$ automatically satisfy full Infinity-Choice ($\mathrm{AC}_\infty$)?

In general, the answer is \emph{no}, and the categorical residuum separating bounded truncated choice ($\forall n < \infty,\, \mathrm{AC}_n$) from $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$ exposes two fundamental invariants of higher topos theory: \emph{Postnikov convergence (hypercompleteness)} and $\varprojlim^1$ phantom vanishing.

A morphism $f: X \to Z$ in an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$ is called $\infty$-connected if its $n$-truncation $\tau_{\le n}(f): \tau_{\le n}X \xrightarrow{\sim} \tau_{\le n}Z$ is an equivalence for every finite $n \ge 0$ (equivalently, all homotopy group sheaves $\pi_k(\mathrm{fib}(f))$ vanish). In the canonical $(\infty, 1)$-topos of spaces $\mathbf{Spaces} = \infty\text{-}\mathbf{Grpd}$, J. H. C. Whitehead's theorem states that every $\infty$-connected map is an equivalence. In an arbitrary Grothendieck $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$ (such as sheaves of spaces over a topological space of infinite covering dimension), however, non-trivial $\infty$-connected maps can exist.

\begin{definition}[Hypercompleteness and Postnikov Convergence]\label{def:hypercomplete}

Let $\mathcal{X}$ be an $(\infty, 1)$-topos.

\begin{itemize}

  \item $\mathcal{X}$ is \emph{hypercomplete} (satisfies the internal Whitehead Principle) if every $\infty$-connected morphism $f: X \to Z$ in $\mathcal{X}$ is an equivalence.

  \item Postnikov towers \emph{converge} in $\mathcal{X}$ if for every object $X \in \mathcal{X}$, the canonical map to the homotopy inverse limit of its Postnikov tower, $X \xrightarrow{\sim} \varprojlim_{n \in \omega} \tau_{\le n}X$, is an equivalence.

\end{itemize}

\end{definition}

Even when Postnikov towers converge in $\mathcal{X}$ ($X \simeq \varprojlim_n \tau_{\le n}X$), having a section $s_n: Y \to \tau_{\le n}X$ at each finite stage $n$ does not guarantee a compatible thread $(s_n)_{n \in \omega}$ in the homotopy inverse limit $\varprojlim_n \tau_{\le n}X$ unless sequential Dependent Choice ($\mathrm{DC}$) or Milnor $\varprojlim^1$ vanishing holds along the tower (and conversely, under $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$, $0$-truncated generation from Theorem~\ref{thm:global-inf-choice} ensures that Postnikov convergence over $0$-truncated targets coincides with hypercompleteness). Combining these ingredients yields the limit residuum:

\begin{theorem}[Limit Residuum for Infinity-Choice]\label{thm:limit-residuum}%
\uses{thm:global-inf-choice,def:hypercomplete,conj:postnikov-stepwise}%

In an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$ equipped with a Natural Numbers Object ($\mathrm{NNO}$), the categorical residuum bridging bounded truncated choice ($\forall n < \infty,\, \mathrm{AC}_n$) to full Infinity-Choice ($\mathrm{AC}_\infty$) is the conjunction of \emph{Postnikov convergence} (hypercompleteness under $0$-truncated generation) and sequential $\mathrm{DC}$ (killing $\varprojlim^1$ phantom obstructions along Postnikov towers):

\[
  \bigl(\forall n < \infty,\, \mathrm{AC}_n\bigr) + \bigl(\text{Postnikov Convergence } \wedge \text{ Sequential } \mathrm{DC}\bigr) \iff \mathrm{AC}_\infty
\]

\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$(\Longrightarrow)$ Assume $\mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_\infty$. Restricting $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$ to $n$-truncated effective epimorphisms over $0$-truncated targets immediately gives $\mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_n$ for every finite $n < \infty$, and restricting to $n = 0$ shows $\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_0$ (external choice on $0$-truncated sets). Over the internal $\mathrm{NNO}$ $\mathbb{N}$ of $\mathcal{X}$, splitting $\coprod_{n \in \mathbb{N}} A_{n+1} \twoheadrightarrow \coprod_{n \in \mathbb{N}} A_n$ in $\tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$ (together with external $\mathrm{AC}_0$ and $\mathrm{DC}$) extracts compatible threads across countable surjective towers, establishing sequential $\mathrm{DC}$. Moreover, by Theorem~\ref{thm:global-inf-choice}, $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$ guarantees that every object $W \in \mathcal{X}$ admits a $0$-truncated cover $S \twoheadrightarrow W$ with $S \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$. For any $\infty$-connected morphism $f : X \to Z$ in $\mathcal{X}$ (in particular the canonical Postnikov limit map $\theta_X : X \to \varprojlim_{n \in \omega} \tau_{\le n} X$), every pullback $z^* f : z^* X \to S$ along a map $z : S \to Z$ from any $S \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$ remains $\infty$-connected, hence $(-1)$-connected (an effective epimorphism onto $S$), and splits by $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$, proving $\pi_0$-surjectivity of $\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}}(S, f)$. Iterating this same pullback-splitting argument along the path-space pullbacks of the $\infty$-connected diagonals $\Delta_{z^* f}$ shows by induction on $k \ge 0$ that $\pi_k(\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}/S}(S, z^* X)) = 0$, so $\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}}(S, X) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}}(S, Z)$ is a weak equivalence of $\infty$-groupoids for every $S \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$. Under $0$-truncated generation from Theorem~\ref{thm:global-inf-choice}, this implies Postnikov convergence $X \xrightarrow{\sim} \varprojlim_{n \in \omega} \tau_{\le n} X$ (hypercompleteness).

$(\Longleftarrow)$ Conversely, assume $\mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_n$ for all $n < \infty$, Postnikov towers converge in $\mathcal{X}$, and sequential $\mathrm{DC}$ holds. Let $e : X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ be an arbitrary effective epimorphism onto a $0$-truncated object $Y \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$. In the slice $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}/Y$, factor $e$ through its relative Postnikov tower
\[
  \cdots \xrightarrow{p_{n+2}} \tau_{\le n+1}(X/Y) \xrightarrow{p_{n+1}} \tau_{\le n}(X/Y) \xrightarrow{p_n} \cdots \xrightarrow{p_0} \tau_{\le -1}(X/Y) \simeq Y,
\]
and set $M_n := \mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}/Y}(Y, \tau_{\le n}(X/Y))$ for each $n \ge 0$ (presented as a tower of Kan fibrations of mapping spaces). By Postnikov convergence in $\mathcal{X}$ (pulled back to the slice over $Y \in \tau_{\le 0}\mathcal{X}$), $X/Y \xrightarrow{\sim} \varprojlim_{n \in \omega^{\mathrm{op}}} \tau_{\le n}(X/Y)$, giving $\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}/Y}(Y, X) \xrightarrow{\sim} \varprojlim_{n \in \omega^{\mathrm{op}}} M_n$. First, for any stage-$n$ section $s_n : Y \to \tau_{\le n}(X/Y)$ in $M_n$, pulling back $\tau_{\le n+1}(X/Y) \twoheadrightarrow \tau_{\le n}(X/Y)$ along $s_n$ produces an $(n+1)$-truncated effective epimorphism $\pi_{n+1} : E_{n+1} := Y \times_{\tau_{\le n}(X/Y)} \tau_{\le n+1}(X/Y) \twoheadrightarrow Y$ in $\mathcal{X}/Y$, which splits by $\mathrm{AC}_{n+1}$; hence $(p_{n+1})_* : \pi_0(M_{n+1}) \twoheadrightarrow \pi_0(M_n)$ is surjective for every $n \ge 0$. Applying $\mathrm{AC}_0$ ($\pi_0(M_0) \neq \emptyset$) and sequential $\mathrm{DC}$ on the surjective tower $(\pi_0(M_n))_{n \ge 0}$ (lifting compatible stages via $\mathrm{DC}$ across the fibration tower) yields a compatible basepoint sequence $(s_n)_{n \ge 0}$ with $([s_n])_{n \ge 0} \in \varprojlim_n \pi_0(M_n)$. Second, for every $n \ge 1$, the homotopy fiber $\mathrm{fib}_{s_n}(M_{n+1} \to M_n) \simeq \mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}/Y}(Y, E_{n+1}) \simeq \mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}/Y}(Y, K(\Pi_{n+1}, n+1))$ has lower homotopy groups $\pi_k\bigl(\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}/Y}(Y, K(\Pi_{n+1}, n+1))\bigr) \cong H^{n+1-k}(\mathcal{X}/Y, \Pi_{n+1}) = 0$ for all $0 \le k \le n$ because $\mathrm{AC}_{n-k}$ splits every $(n-k)$-gerbe banded by $\Pi_{n+1}$. Thus every transition fiber for $n \ge 1$ is $n$-connected, so the long exact homotopy sequence yields $\pi_1(M_{n+1}, s_{n+1}) \xrightarrow{\cong} \pi_1(M_n, s_n)$ for all $n \ge 1$, ensuring the Mittag--Leffler condition and killing $\varprojlim^1_m \pi_1(M_m, s_m) = *$. By the Milnor exact sequence for the homotopy limit tower $(M_m)$,
\[
  * \longrightarrow \varprojlim^1_m \pi_1(M_m, s_m) \longrightarrow \pi_0\Bigl(\varprojlim_m M_m\Bigr) \longrightarrow \varprojlim_m \pi_0(M_m) \longrightarrow *,
\]
the compatible $\pi_0$-thread lifts to a global section $s \in \pi_0\,\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{X}/Y}(Y, X)$, proving $\mathcal{X} \models \mathrm{AC}_\infty$.
\end{proof}

\section{Methodological Bridge: Categorical Reverse Mathematics as a Discovery Engine in $(\infty,\infty)$-Category Theory}\label{subsec:crm-discovery-engine-inf-inf}

Passing along Axis~1 from $(\infty, 1)$-categories to directed $(\infty, \infty)$-categories while varying the ambient choice or topos base along Axis~2 turns Categorical Reverse Mathematics into an active \emph{discovery engine}: rather than only classifying pre-existing classical theorems, extracting the exact residuum $\mathcal{R}$ validating $A + \mathcal{R} \iff B$ systematically isolates hidden geometric hypotheses, prevents false equivalences, and engineers sharp separating countermodels across the $(\infty, \infty)$-landscape. Four concrete $(\infty, \infty)$-categorical arenas (Table~\ref{tab:crm-inf-inf-residuums}) demonstrate how this residuum calculus drove the breakthroughs verified across Chapters~\ref{ch:higher-crm}--\ref{ch:realizability-sites} and the \texttt{HomotopyCRM} Lean~4 suite.

\begin{center}\emph{[Table omitted in blueprint edition; see Royal Octavo monograph PDF.]}\label{tab:crm-inf-inf-residuums}\end{center}

\subsection*{(1) Dependent Choice $\mathrm{IDC}_\omega$ and Coinductive Stream Adequacy in \texttt{ICaTT} (Theorem~\ref{thm:coinductive-stream-choice})}

Evaluating $\mathbf{CaTT}$ and its coinductive-invertibility extension $\mathbf{ICaTT}$~\cite{BCM26,FinsterMimram17,ORW26} inside an arbitrary Grothendieck $1$-topos $\mathcal{E}$ requires \emph{Grothendieck--Maltsiniotis weak $\omega$-category / clan models} $\mathcal{C} \in \mathbf{Clan}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{CaTT}}, \mathcal{E})$ (distinct from strict $\omega$-categories, which collapse syntactic coherence towers). Inside $\mathcal{E}$, one compares the relational greatest fixed point $E_{\mathrm{rel}} = \nu \Phi \subseteq \bigsqcup_{k \ge 1} C_k$ of one-step reversibility against the internal $\mathbf{ICaTT}$ witness-stream image $\mathrm{im}(\mathrm{InvStr}) := \mathrm{im}(\pi_0 : \mathrm{InvStr}_k(\mathcal{C}) \to C_k)$, where $\mathrm{InvStr}_k(\mathcal{C}) := \varprojlim_{d \in \omega^{\mathrm{op}}} \mathrm{InvStage}^{(d)}_k(\mathcal{C})$. Residuum extraction proves that $\mathrm{IDC}_\omega$ ($\mathrm{DC}_\omega$) is the exact choice threshold bridging $E_{\mathrm{rel}}$ to $\mathrm{im}(\mathrm{InvStr})$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:coinductive-stream-choice}, Chapter~\ref{ch:realizability-sites}): under $\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{IDC}_\omega$ (Rung~$\ge 1$ of Kihara's spectrum), epimorphic frontier-filtered stages $\widetilde{\mathrm{Stage}}^{(d+1)}_k \twoheadrightarrow \widetilde{\mathrm{Stage}}^{(d)}_k$ guarantee $\mathrm{im}(\mathrm{InvStr}) = E_{\mathrm{rel}}$; conversely, at Rung~0 in Schanuel's Boolean atomic topos $\mathbf{Nom} = \mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Sym}(\mathbb{N}))$ ($\mathrm{LEM} + \neg\mathrm{DC}_\omega$), evaluating $\Phi$ and $\mathrm{InvStr}_1$ on the free Grothendieck--Maltsiniotis weak $\omega$-category $\mathrm{Free}_{\mathbf{CaTT}}(E[\mathbb{A}]) \in \mathbf{Clan}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{CaTT}}, \mathbf{Nom})$ generated by the fresh-atom computad $E[\mathbb{A}]$ (where finitary polynomial $\mathbf{CaTT}$ syntax preserves finite atom supports) leaves $u \in \Gamma(E_{\mathrm{rel}, 1})$ by Knaster--Tarski coinduction, whereas any candidate witness stream $\xi$ would select an infinite sequence of distinct fresh atoms $(a_1, a_2, \dots) \in \mathbb{A}^{\#(\omega)}$ broken over every finite support $F$ by a transposition $(a_m \; b) \cdot \xi \neq \xi$, emptying $\mathrm{InvStr}_1(\mathrm{Free}_{\mathbf{CaTT}}(E[\mathbb{A}]))_u = \emptyset$.

\subsection*{(2) Pure Postnikov Phantoms Without Homotopy Spheres (Proposition~\ref{prop:cat-le-omega-lfd} and Theorems~\ref{thm:ordinal-rank-completeness}, \ref{thm:classifier-scc-trapping}--\ref{thm:step-product-counterexample})}

Calibrating Ozornova--Rovelli--Walde's Questions~5.60 and~5.62 (v1 Question~5.58)~\cite{ORW26} via ordinal hom-ranks led Theorem~\ref{thm:ordinal-rank-completeness} (\texttt{HomotopyCRM/ OrdinalRank Completeness.lean}) to prove immediate $\alpha = 0$ stabilization $\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega} \subseteq \mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty,\infty)}$, isolating Conjecture~C1 ($\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega} \stackrel{?}{\subseteq} \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty,\infty)}$) as a pure Postnikov phantom problem. Applying Step~3 of the CRM protocol (obstruction extraction) first eliminated every spurious mechanism: $[1]$-classifier SCC trapping and grading rigidity (Theorem~\ref{thm:classifier-scc-trapping}) proved that compatible Postnikov towers cannot leave an equivalence class of $1$-cell reachability $\preceq_1$ except across an absorbing hierarchy, while automatic full faithfulness of $\eta_{\mathcal{C}} : \mathcal{C} \hookrightarrow RL(\mathcal{C})$, vanishing constant-tower $\varprojlim^1$, and $1$-acyclic completeness (Theorem~\ref{thm:ff-acyclic-completeness}) ruled out homotopy spheres, constant-tower $\varprojlim^1$ twists, and non-absorbing $1$-SCCs. That negative obstruction fingerprint pointed directly to the exact missing counterexample (Theorem~\ref{thm:step-product-counterexample}): the gaunt Noetherian rank-$\omega$ restricted-tail step-product $\mathcal{Q} \subseteq \prod_{k \ge 1} \mathcal{A}_k$ on finite-prefix indicator sequences $e^{(m)} = (1_1, \dots, 1_m, 0_{m+1}, \dots)$ over $\mathcal{A}_k = \mathcal{M}_{k+1} \in \mathrm{Cat}_{(0, k+1)}$, which refutes Conjecture~C1 via the pure Postnikov phantom $X_\bullet = (\ell_n(e^{(n-1)}))_{n \ge 1} \in RL(\mathcal{Q}) \setminus \mathcal{Q}$.

\subsection*{(3) Directed Delooping Tameness versus $\pi_{\le 0}$ Group-Completion and Smooth $4$-Manifold Stabilization}

Enforcing Guardrail~1---Giraud strict initiality ($\emptyset \times X \simeq \emptyset$) prevents any non-trivial ``stable $(\infty,1)$-topos,'' and $\Sigma^\infty_+(*) \simeq \mathbb{S}$ in $\mathrm{Pr}^L$ (never $\Sigma^\infty_+(U) \simeq \mathbb{S}$ for a general object $U$)---forces directed stabilization to pass to pointed $(\infty,\infty)$-categories $\mathrm{Cat}^R_* := (\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty,\infty)})_{*/}$, forming $\mathrm{CatSp}^R := \mathrm{Sp}(\mathrm{Cat}^R_*)$~\cite{Masuda26,Heine26}. Because directed looping $\Omega_{\mathrm{dir}}(B\mathcal{C}) \simeq \mathcal{C}$ commutes with $\pi_{\le n}$ for all $n \ge 1$ ($\Omega_{\mathrm{dir}}(\pi_{\le n}(X, x)) \xrightarrow{\sim} \pi_{\le n-1}(\Omega_{\mathrm{dir}}(X, x))$) while avoiding $n = 0$ group-completion ($\Omega(\pi_{\le 0} B\mathbb{N}) \simeq \mathbb{Z} \not\simeq \mathbb{N} = \pi_{\le 0}(\Omega_{\mathrm{dir}} B\mathbb{N})$, cf.\ Section~\ref{subsec:excision-catsp} and~\cite{Heine26,Geiss26}), CRM identifies Left Bousfield groupoidification $\pi_{\le 0}$ itself as the exact residuum generating classical stable topological complexity (Section~\ref{subsec:excision-catsp}): the categorical delooping $\mathbf{B}^\infty(\mathbf{FinSet}^{\simeq}) \in \mathrm{CatSp}^L$ remains $(k+1, k)$-truncated and Noetherian of rank $k + 2$ at every level $k$ (since only $\emptyset$ is $\amalg$-invertible), until levelwise $\pi_{\le 0}$ forces group-completion $\mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{Z}$ and Quillen's plus-construction $B\Sigma_\infty \to B\Sigma_\infty^+$ (Barratt--Priddy--Quillen) to birth the classical topological sphere spectrum $\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{top}}$ and all $\pi_*^s(\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{top}})$. By the exact same mechanism, $\mathbf{B}^\infty(M_4^\#) \in \mathrm{CatSp}^R$ under connected sum $\#$ preserves smooth $4$-manifold non-invertibility (avoiding Wall's stable diffeomorphism collapse triggered by $\pi_{\le 0}$), while square-zero absorbing monoids $M(X) := *_0 \sqcup X \sqcup *_2$ (where $(B^k M(X))^{\simeq} \simeq *$ for all $k \ge 1$, killing Rezk gluing and $\varprojlim^1$ twists) transport strictness across the lifted four-tier chain $\mathrm{CatSp}^L \subsetneq \mathrm{CatSp}^\omega \subsetneq \mathrm{CatSp}^{\mathrm{coind}} \subsetneq \mathrm{CatSp}^R$.

\subsection*{(4) Oriented Convexity and the $n = 2$ versus $n \ge 3$ Cubical Amalgamation Wall (Theorem~\ref{thm:directed-cubical-amalgamation})}

While formalizing directed Fraïssé Strong Amalgamation in \texttt{HomotopyCRM/ Directed Fraisse SAP.lean}, kernel verification of the residuum separating bare closed cellular embeddings from flow-acyclic binary pushouts revealed that path-interval vertex closure (\texttt{inter\_ mem}) alone admits a vacuous single-step loophole on $\{0, 1\} \hookrightarrow (0 \to 1)$, forcing the two-clause \texttt{IsFlowConvex} (\texttt{cell\_ mem} + \texttt{inter\_ mem}) condition and showing that 1-sided cobase-change (\texttt{IsFlowReachReflecting} + bare \texttt{IsClosedEmbedding}) suffices for \texttt{IsFlowAcyclic} (\texttt{sap\_ flowConvex}). Crucially, restricting \texttt{FlowStep} to equal-dimension codimension-$1$ cofaces ($\dim u = \dim v$) keeps Marcus Nicolas's pinched $2$-cube $P = \square^2 \cup_{\{10, 01\}} \square^1$---as well as $P^{\mathrm{op}}$ and $P^{\mathrm{co}}$---strictly \texttt{IsFlowAcyclic} even though $P^{\mathrm{op}}$ breaks strong Steiner partial duality via mixed-dimension chain coupling~\cite[Section~2.4, Footnote~5]{Masuda26}. Ascending from $n = 2$ to $n \ge 3$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:directed-cubical-amalgamation}, \texttt{HomotopyCRM/ Cubical Amalgamation.lean}) then exposes a sharp cubical phase transition: intersective diagrams in $\mathbf{Poset}_{\mathrm{og,fin}}^{\mathrm{closed}}$ satisfy disjoint $n$-amalgamation ($n$-$\mathrm{DAP}$) for all $n \ge 2$ via the quotient-free minimal-support amalgam \texttt{CubicalAmalgam}, whereas for every $n \ge 3$ the facet-supported directed $n$-cycle $F_n$ has \texttt{IsFlowAcyclic} proper faces and \texttt{IsFlowConvex} transition maps yet admits no \texttt{IsFlowAcyclic} closed-embedding cocone.

\section{Inductive versus Coinductive Equivalences in Directed $(\infty, \infty)$-Categories}\label{subsec:directed-inf-inf}

When we further drop $1$-invertibility along Axis~1 (Chapter~\ref{ch:base-fiber}, Section~\ref{sec:axis1-deformations}) to pass from $(\infty, 1)$-categories to $(\infty, \infty)$-categories, the Whitehead hypercompleteness gap acquires a directed counterpart---together with a foundational distinction between inductive initial-algebra fixed points and coinductive reflective localizations.

On the inductive side, Zach Goldthorpe~\cite{Goldthorpe23}, via an $\infty$-categorical generalization of Ad\'amek's free endofunctor algebra construction for weak Gepner--Haugseng enrichment $\mathrm{Enr}: \mathcal{V} \mapsto \mathcal{V}\mathrm{Cat}$~\cite{GepnerHaugseng15}, proved that over presentably symmetric monoidal $(\infty, 1)$-categories $\mathrm{SymMon}^{\mathrm{Pres}}_\infty$, $(\infty, \infty)$-categories equipped with \emph{inductively defined equivalences} (generated bottom-up from identity morphisms, modeled by the right-truncation limit $\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)} := \varprojlim_\kappa \mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty, n)}$, Theorem~3.3.5, with its Postnikov-complete / left-generated subcategory $\mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)} := \varprojlim_\pi \mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty, n)}$ embedding reflectively inside $\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)}$) form the initial algebra of $\mathrm{Enr}$, while over $\mathrm{SymMon}_\infty$, Ad\'amek iteration across the large ordinal $\Lambda$ stabilizes on Noetherian $(\infty, \infty)$-categories $\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{Noeth}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$ lacking infinite non-trivial parallel morphism towers ($\mathrm{Cell}_\infty(\mathcal{C}) \simeq \emptyset$). By contrast, an earlier dual claim of Goldthorpe~\cite{Goldthorpe23} asserting that $\mathrm{Cat}_\omega$ yields a terminal coalgebra of $\mathrm{Enr}$ on $\mathrm{SymMon}_\infty$ was withdrawn in revision per Simon Henry's erratum: the groupoid-reflection left adjoint $\pi_{\le 0}(\mathcal{C}) = |\mathcal{C}| \not\simeq \mathcal{C}^{\simeq}$ fails to agree with the maximal Kan groupoid core (whereas Theorem~3.2.6 of \texttt{arXiv:2307.00442v5} is the surviving weakly-contractible limit-preservation theorem supporting Theorem~3.3.5, and Simon Henry's counterexample excludes naive left-localization limits $\varprojlim_\pi \mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty, n)}$ from coinciding with $\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)}$).

Instead, inside the ambient right-truncation tower limit $\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)} := \varprojlim_\kappa \mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty, n)}$ taken along the dimensional core/truncation right adjoints $\kappa_{\le n}$, the verified landscape of $(\infty, \infty)$-categorical homotopy theories organizes into a strict four-tier chain of reflective localizations established by Ozornova--Rovelli--Walde~\cite{ORW26} and Gepner--Heine~\cite{GepnerHeine26} (illustrated alongside the step-product phantom in Figure~\ref{fig:inf-inf-ladder-phantom}):

\[
  \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)} \subsetneq \mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty, \infty)} \subsetneq \mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{coind}}_{(\infty, \infty)} \subsetneq \mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)}
\]

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Inductive / Postnikov-Complete Tier ($\mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$):} Left-generated $(\infty, \infty)$-categories ($\mathcal{C} \xrightarrow{\sim} RL(\mathcal{C}) \simeq \varprojlim_{k \in \omega^{\mathrm{op}}} i_k \pi_{\le k}(\mathcal{C})$) equipped with inductively generated equivalences built bottom-up from finite-dimensional truncations and identity morphisms.

  \item \textbf{Street--Verity $\omega$-Tier ($\mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty, \infty)}$):} Complicial / $\omega$-categorical models whose $\omega$-equivalences sit strictly between finite-dimensional Postnikov convergence and full coinductive saturation.

  \item \textbf{Coinductive Tier ($\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{coind}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$):} $(\infty, \infty)$-categories localized with respect to \emph{coinductive equivalences} (morphisms admitting two-sided inverses up to coinductive equivalence at every level).
