  \item \textbf{Right-Truncation Limit ($\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)} := \varprojlim_\kappa \mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty, n)}$):} The ambient projective limit along right-adjoint core truncations $\kappa_{\le n}$, on which the composite reflective localization endofunctor $RL : \mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)} \to \mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)}$ commutes with categorical suspension $\Sigma$ and set-indexed coproducts.

\end{itemize}

Within this four-tier reflective hierarchy, two verified placement results calibrate the exact scope of Noetherian tower termination relative to inductive and coinductive hypercompleteness:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Noetherian Coinductive Hypercompleteness ($\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{Noeth}}_{(\infty, \infty)} \subseteq \mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{coind}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$):} Vanishing of infinite non-trivial parallel cell towers ($\mathrm{Cell}_\infty(\mathcal{C}) \simeq \emptyset$) implies coinductive hypercompleteness, embedding $\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{Noeth}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$ inside $\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{coind}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$.

  \item \textbf{Strict Separation by $S^2$ ($S^2 \in \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)} \setminus \mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{Noeth}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$):} Viewing the $2$-sphere $S^2$ as an $\infty$-groupoid inside $\mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$, its infinitely many non-trivial higher homotopy groups $\pi_n(S^2)$ witness $\mathrm{Cell}_\infty(S^2) \not\simeq \emptyset$, strictly separating inductive $(\infty, \infty)$-categories from Noetherian ones.

\end{itemize}

In an arbitrary $(\infty, \infty)$-category $\mathcal{C}$, every inductive equivalence is coinductively invertible, but the converse fails outside hypercomplete regimes: for example, the higher cobordism $(\infty, \infty)$-category $\mathrm{Cob}_\infty$ has duals for morphisms in all dimensions---forcing every morphism to be a coinductive equivalence and collapsing $\mathrm{Cob}_\infty$ to an $\infty$-groupoid under coinductive saturation in $\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{coind}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$---while remaining a non-trivial directed $(\infty, \infty)$-category under inductive equivalences in $\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)}$ (and separating $\mathbf{B}^\infty \mathrm{Cob}_\infty \in \mathrm{CatSp}^R \setminus \mathrm{CatSp}^{\mathrm{coind}}$). Consequently, bridging principles valid for coinductive equivalences ($A$, in $\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{coind}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$) to strict inductive / Postnikov-convergent invertibility ($B$, in $\mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$) inside $\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)}$ isolates \emph{coinductive hypercompleteness} across the localization tower (with $\mathrm{Cell}_\infty(\mathcal{C}) \simeq \emptyset$ on $\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{Noeth}}_{(\infty, \infty)} \subseteq \mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{coind}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$ furnishing a sufficient Noetherian criterion, though not necessary in light of $S^2 \in \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)} \setminus \mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{Noeth}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$) as the exact directed Whitehead residuum.

\section{Ordinal-Rank Completeness, Negative Resolution of Conjecture~C1, \texttt{ICaTT} Clan Semantics, and Slice-Algebra Perfection}\label{subsec:ordinal-rank-icatt-perfection}

Finer calibrations sharpen the geometric mechanisms separating Tiers~1, 2, and~3 of the reflective localization tower, resolve \textbf{Conjecture~C1} negatively via an explicit gaunt Noetherian rank-$\omega$ step-product phantom, and anchor the exact boundary of Ad\'amek initial-algebra fixed points.

\begin{proposition}[Inductive Lali Split-Idempotent Ceiling and Cartesian-vs.-Gray Obstruction]\label{prop:lali-ceiling}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.prop11A_lali_stabilization_above_ceiling, HomotopyCRM.prop11A_ceiling_sharp_at_max_add_two}%
\leanok%
\uses{def:comparison-triple}%
Let $l : \mathcal{A} \rightleftarrows \mathcal{D} : r$ be a Gray-enriched (oriented~\cite[Definition~3.5.1]{GepnerHeine26}) left-adjoint-left-inverse (\emph{Lali}) adjunction with idempotent endofunctor $E := l \circ r : \mathcal{D} \to \mathcal{D}$, over objects $x, y \in \mathcal{D}$ possessing finite local hom cell-stabilization heights $K_x = \mathrm{height}(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(x, y)) < \omega$ and $K_y = \mathrm{height}(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(E x, E y)) < \omega$ (\texttt{HasHomCellHeight}).
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Finite-Rank Split-Idempotent Stabilization:} For every pair of parallel $(m-1)$-cell boundaries $(\Phi, \Psi)$ over $\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(x, y)$ (\texttt{HomBoundary}) in ambient dimension $m > \max(K_x, K_y) + 2$, the Lali idempotent $E$ induces equivalences on ambient $m$-cell animae
  \[
    \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(\Phi, \Psi) \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;} \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}}(E(\Phi), E(\Psi)) \simeq *,
  \]
  and this ceiling is sharp at $m = \max(K_x, K_y) + 2$ on the oriented join $\{a\} \star \Sigma^{K+1}(S^0)$ (owing to the $(+1) + (+1) = +2$ shift from global mapping suspension plus lax counit modification coherence). Both the stabilization bound and its exact $+2$ boundary are machine-checked in Lean~4 (\texttt{HomotopyCRM/ OrdinalRank Completeness.lean}, 1,774 lines, 4-Layer Gate verified) via \texttt{prop11A\_ lali\_ stabilization\_ above\_ ceiling} and \texttt{prop11A\_ ceiling\_ sharp\_ at\_ max\_ add\_ two} on \texttt{JoinSuspState.joinCone K}.
  \item \textbf{Cartesian-vs.-Gray Naturality Obstruction ($n \ge 2$):} On finite-stage walking $n$-equivalences $E^{(n)}$ ($n \ge 2$), Cartesian functor categories $\mathrm{Fun}^{\times}(E^{(n)}, E^{(n)})$ cannot contract $E^{(n)}$ ($\mathrm{id}_{E^{(2)}} \not\simeq \mathrm{const}_*$ in $\mathrm{Fun}^{\times}(E^{(2)}, E^{(2)})$, blocking $E^{(\omega)}$ from Cartesian contractibility) because Cartesian cylinder cells satisfy $\dim(\mathbf{D}^{n-1} \times \mathbf{D}^1) = \max(n-1, 1) = n - 1 < n$, whereas lax/oplax Gray functor categories $\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{lax}}(E^{(n)}, E^{(n)})$ supply the additive prism cell $\mathbf{D}^{n-1} \boxtimes \mathbf{D}^1$ of dimension $(n - 1) + 1 = n$~\cite{GepnerHeine26,Heine26,Geiss26}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

Why do both Henry--Loubaton's wide pushout $E^{(\omega)}$ (separating $\mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty, \infty)} \subsetneq \mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{coind}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$ in~\cite[Corollary~5.56]{ORW26}) and Ozornova--Rovelli--Walde's full subcategory $W \hookrightarrow V(\infty) := * \amalg_{\coprod_{n \in \mathbb{N}} \{1\}} \coprod_{n \in \mathbb{N}} E^{(n+1)}$ (separating $\mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)} \subsetneq \mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty, \infty)}$ in~\cite[\S 5]{ORW26}) evade Proposition~\ref{prop:lali-ceiling} ($\sup K_x = \infty$) and exit $\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{Noeth}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$? Two independent mechanisms force $\mathrm{Cell}_\infty \not\simeq \emptyset$:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{$\mathbb{E}_3$ Configuration-Space Spheres in Weak Cellular Pushouts:} Attaching weak cells at codimension $k \ge 3$ over loop boundaries activates Eckmann--Hilton / syllepsis braiding on $\Omega^3_x$, whose $2$-strand component has underlying anima $\mathrm{Conf}_2(\mathbb{R}^3)_{/\Sigma_2} \simeq \mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^2$ with $\pi_m(\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^2) \cong \pi_m(S^2) \neq 0$ for infinitely many $m \ge 2$.
  \item \textbf{Hub Excursions in $W \subseteq V(\infty)$:} Even on skeletal cell heights, each wedge summand $E^{(n+1)}$ identifies $0 \mapsto n \in \mathrm{Ob}(W)$ and $1 \mapsto *$, contributing $\eta_n : n \to *$ and candidate inverses $v_n : * \to n$. Already at object $1 \in \mathrm{Ob}(W)$, the hub excursion $f_1 := v_1 \circ \eta_1 : 1 \to * \to 1$ and iterated loops $f_{1, n} := v_1 \circ (\eta_n \circ v_n) \circ \eta_1 \in W(1, 1)$ embed unbounded $(n+1)$-cell towers into the single hom-object $W(1, 1)$, forcing $\mathrm{rank}(W(1, 1)) \ge \omega$ and $\mathrm{rank}(W) \ge \omega + 1$.
\end{itemize}

Define the recursive ordinal hom-rank by
\[
  \mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{D}) \;:=\; \sup_{x, y \in \mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{D})} \bigl(\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{D}(x, y)) + 1\bigr),
\]
and let $\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega}$ denote the class of $(\infty, \infty)$-categories whose hom-objects have finite ordinal rank ($\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{C}(x, y)) < \omega$ for all $x, y$, blocking both $\mathbb{R}\mathbb{P}^2$ homotopy towers and hub excursions). Calibrating Ozornova--Rovelli--Walde's~\cite[Questions~5.60 and~5.62 (v1 Question~5.58)]{ORW26} transfinite reflector $L_\omega : \mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)} \to \mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty, \infty)}$ built via $\mathcal{C}_{\alpha+1} := \mathcal{C}_\alpha[\mathrm{IsOmegaInv}(\mathcal{C}_\alpha)^{-1}]$ yields complete stabilization at $\alpha = 0$ on $\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega}$---machine-checked in \texttt{HomotopyCRM/ Ordinal Rank Completeness.lean} (1,774 lines, 4-Layer Gate verified) via universe-safe globular state coalgebras \texttt{Globular Inf System} and transfinite ordinal invertibility \texttt{Is Ordinal Inv}, together with \texttt{Omega Tower State} separating ranks $0 < 1 < 2 < \omega < \omega + 1$:

\begin{theorem}[Ordinal-Rank Completeness and Stabilization of ORW Question~5.58 at $\alpha = 0$]\label{thm:ordinal-rank-completeness}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.isOmegaInv_iff_isOrdinalInv_omega0, HomotopyCRM.theorem9A_ordinal_rank_completeness, HomotopyCRM.ranked_coindEquiv_iff_isEquiv, HomotopyCRM.theorem9A_omega_completeness, HomotopyCRM.orw_question558_stabilizes_at_zero, HomotopyCRM.apexArrow_isOmegaInv, HomotopyCRM.apexArrow_not_isEquiv, HomotopyCRM.apexArrow_not_isCoindEquiv}%
\leanok%
\uses{prop:lali-ceiling}%
For every ordinal $\theta$ and every ranked $(\infty, \infty)$-category $\mathcal{D}$ satisfying $\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{D}) \le \theta$, transfinite $\theta$-inductive invertibility coincides with genuine equivalence, $\mathrm{IsOrdinalInv}_\theta(\mathcal{D}) = \mathrm{IsEquiv}(\mathcal{D})$ (\texttt{theorem9A\_ ordinal\_ rank\_ completeness}, sharp at rank $\omega + 1$ via the additive non-surjective inclusion $\mathrm{Vect} \to \mathrm{Vect}^2$, $V \mapsto (V, 0)$, and \texttt{OmegaTowerState.apex}). Specializing to $\theta = \omega$, every $\mathcal{C} \in \mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega}$ satisfies $\mathrm{IsOmegaInv}(\mathcal{C}) = \mathrm{IsEquiv}(\mathcal{C})$ (\texttt{theorem9A\_ omega\_ completeness}), so v2 Questions~5.60 and~5.62 (v1 Question~5.58) stabilize immediately at $\alpha = 0$ (\texttt{orw\_ question558\_ stabilizes\_ at\_ zero}, giving $\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega} \subseteq \mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty, \infty)}$) and narrows \textbf{Conjecture~C1} strictly to \emph{Postnikov Phantom-Freeness}:
\begin{gather*}
  \text{\textbf{Conjecture~C1 (Phantom-Freeness):}} \qquad \mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega} \;\stackrel{?}{\subseteq}\; \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)} \\
  \text{[Resolved negatively in Theorem~\ref{thm:step-product-counterexample}].}
\end{gather*}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Local Finite-Dimensionality of $\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega}$ and Failure of $\omega$-Filtered Colimit Closure of $\mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$]\label{prop:cat-le-omega-lfd}
An $(\infty, \infty)$-category $\mathcal{C} \in \mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)}$ lies in $\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega}$ ($\mathrm{rk}_S(\mathcal{C}) \le \omega$) if and only if $\mathcal{C}$ is \emph{locally finite-dimensional}: for every pair of objects $x, y \in \mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C})$, there exists a finite integer $d(x, y) < \omega$ such that $\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(x, y) \in \mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty, d(x, y))}$. Moreover, while Ozornova--Rovelli--Walde's~\cite[\S 5]{ORW26} category $W = \varinjlim_d W(d)$ shows that $\mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$ fails $\omega$-filtered colimit closure inside $\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)}$, non-fully-faithful bonding maps $W(d) \to W(d+1)$ inject unbounded hub excursions $f_{1, n} \in W(1, 1)$, forcing $\mathrm{rk}_S(W) \ge \omega + 1$; consequently $W \notin \mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega}$ does not test Conjecture~C1.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By definition of recursive hom-rank, $\mathrm{rk}_S(\mathcal{C}) \le \omega$ holds if and only if $\mathrm{rk}_S(\mathcal{C}(x, y)) < \omega$ for all $x, y \in \mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C})$, which holds precisely when iterated hom-towers over $\mathcal{C}(x, y)$ have contractible cell animae above some finite depth $d(x, y) < \omega$, i.e., $\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(x, y) \in \mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty, d(x, y))}$. In \S 5 of~\cite{ORW26}, each finite stage $W(d) \subseteq V(d) \in \mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty, d+1)} \subseteq \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$ lies in $\mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$, yet $W = \varinjlim_d W(d) \in \mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty, \infty)} \setminus \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$. Because the iterated hub loops $f_{1, n} = v_1 \circ (\eta_n \circ v_n) \circ \eta_1$ populate $W(1, 1)$ at unbounded heights $n + 1$, $\mathrm{rk}_S(W(1, 1)) \ge \omega$ and $\mathrm{rk}_S(W) \ge \omega + 1$, excluding $W$ from $\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[{$[1]$}-Classifier SCC Trapping and Grading Rigidity]\label{thm:classifier-scc-trapping}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.theorem12B_classifier_scc_trapping, HomotopyCRM.theorem12B_tower_trapped_in_single_scc}%
\leanok%
\uses{thm:ordinal-rank-completeness}%
Let $\mathcal{C} \in \mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)}$, and define $1$-cell reachability $a \preceq_1 b$ on $\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C})$ by $\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(a, b) \neq \emptyset$.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Reachability SCC Trapping:} For every Postnikov-compatible tower $X_\bullet = (x_n)_{n \ge 1} \in RL(\mathcal{C})$ (satisfying $\ell_n(x_{n+1}) \simeq \ell_n(x_n)$ for all $n \ge 1$), all stage representatives $x_n$ are trapped inside a single strongly connected component (SCC) of $(\mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C}), \preceq_1)$.
  \item \textbf{Grading Rigidity:} On any infinite $1$-SCC $\mathcal{S}$ lacking strict absorbing/zero-like morphisms under composition, local finite hom-dimensions $d(a, b) < \omega$ cannot grow unboundedly compatibly with $1$-cell composition whiskering under $\pi_{\le M(a,b)}$ or coreflective $\kappa_{\le M(a,b)}$; hence any counterexample in $\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega} \setminus \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$ must decouple an infinite chain of objects via an absorbing hierarchy rather than an infinite non-absorbing $1$-SCC or homotopy spheres.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For each $x \in \mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C})$, the principal upper set $U_x := \{z \in \mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C}) \mid x \preceq_1 z\}$ induces a well-defined functor $\chi_x : \mathcal{C} \to [1] = \{0 < 1\}$ sending $U_x \mapsto 1$ and $\mathcal{C} \setminus U_x \mapsto 0$, because $[1] \in \mathrm{Cat}_{(0,1)}$ has contractible non-empty homs and $\mathrm{Hom}_{[1]}(1, 0) = \emptyset$. For every $n \ge 1$, the universal property of $\ell_n : \mathcal{C} \to \pi_{\le n}(\mathcal{C})$ factors $\chi_x \simeq \overline{\chi}_{x,n} \circ \ell_n$. Since $[1]$ is gaunt ($0 \not\simeq 1$),
\[
  \ell_n(x) \simeq \ell_n(y) \;\Longrightarrow\; \overline{\chi}_{x,n}(\ell_n y) = 1 \;\wedge\; \overline{\chi}_{y,n}(\ell_n x) = 1 \;\Longrightarrow\; x \preceq_1 y \;\wedge\; y \preceq_1 x.
\]
Setting $n = 1$ along $\ell_1(x_m) \simeq \ell_1(x_1)$ places every $x_m$ in the $1$-SCC of $x_1$, proving (1). For (2), whiskering a morphism in $\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(a, b)$ across invertible or non-absorbing paths in $\mathcal{S}$ transfers cell dimensions across objects unless higher composites collapse through an absorbing element.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Automatic Full Faithfulness, Vanishing Constant-Tower $\varprojlim^1$, and $1$-Acyclic Completeness on $\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega}$]\label{thm:ff-acyclic-completeness}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.theorem12C_antisymmetric_postnikov_completeness}%
\leanok%
\uses{thm:classifier-scc-trapping}%
For every locally finite-dimensional $(\infty, \infty)$-category $\mathcal{C} \in \mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega}$:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item For all $x, y \in \mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{C})$ and every $n \ge d(x, y) + 1$, $\mathrm{Hom}_{\pi_{\le n}(\mathcal{C})}(\ell_n x, \ell_n y) \simeq \pi_{\le n-1}(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(x, y)) \simeq \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(x, y)$, so the Postnikov unit $\eta_{\mathcal{C}} : \mathcal{C} \hookrightarrow RL(\mathcal{C}) = \varprojlim_n i_n \pi_{\le n}(\mathcal{C})$ is always fully faithful.
  \item Every Postnikov-compatible tower $X_\bullet \in RL(\mathcal{C})$ with an eventually constant representative $X_n \simeq \ell_n(x)$ for $n \gg 0$ has stabilizing equivalence bonding maps on $\mathrm{Isom}_{\pi_{\le n}\mathcal{C}}(\ell_n x, X_n)$ for $n \ge d(x, x) + 1$; hence all constant-tower $\varprojlim^1$ twists vanish ($X_\bullet \simeq \eta_{\mathcal{C}}(x)$).
  \item If $\preceq_1$ is $1$-acyclic (antisymmetric up to equivalence in $\mathcal{C}$: $x \preceq_1 y \land y \preceq_1 x \implies x \simeq y$), then $\mathcal{C} \in \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Since $\pi_{\le n-1} : \mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)} \to \mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty, n-1)}$ preserves finite products~\cite[Lemma~2.13 and Theorem~4.25]{ORW26} and Rezk completion $\pi_{\le n}(\mathcal{C}) \simeq L_{\mathrm{Rezk}}(\pi_{\le n-1}^{\mathrm{hom}}(\mathcal{C}))$ is Dwyer--Kan fully faithful on hom-objects~\cite[Theorem~5.6.2]{GepnerHaugseng15}, any $n \ge d(x, y) + 1$ gives $\pi_{\le n-1}(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(x, y)) \simeq \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(x, y)$, proving (1). Passing to maximal groupoid cores stabilizes $\mathrm{Isom}_{\pi_{\le n}\mathcal{C}}(\ell_n x, X_n) \simeq \mathrm{Isom}_{\mathcal{C}}(x, x)$ for $n \ge d(x, x) + 1$, giving an eventually constant Mittag--Leffler tower with $\varprojlim^1 = *$ and proving (2). Under $1$-acyclicity, Theorem~\ref{thm:classifier-scc-trapping}(1) forces $x_n \simeq x_1$ for all $n \ge 1$, so (2) yields $X_\bullet \simeq \eta_{\mathcal{C}}(x_1)$ and $\mathcal{C} \in \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$, proving (3).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Gaunt Noetherian Rank-$\omega$ Step-Product Counterexample $\mathcal{Q}$ Disproving Conjecture~C1]\label{thm:step-product-counterexample}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.isHereditarilyGaunt_monoid, HomotopyCRM.absorbingMonoid_collapse_le, HomotopyCRM.stepProductQ_omega_completeness, HomotopyCRM.isHereditarilyGaunt_qFull, HomotopyCRM.homQ_card_eq, HomotopyCRM.qFull_not_nTruncated, HomotopyCRM.theorem12D_stepProductQ_phantom_not_realized, HomotopyCRM.conjecture_c1_disproof_summary}%
\leanok%
\uses{thm:ordinal-rank-completeness,thm:classifier-scc-trapping,thm:ff-acyclic-completeness}%
There exists a gaunt, locally finite-dimensional, Noetherian $(\infty, \infty)$-category $\mathcal{Q}$ of global rank $\mathrm{rk}_S(\mathcal{Q}) = \omega$ satisfying $\alpha = 0$ stabilization in $\mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty, \infty)}$ yet admitting a pure Postnikov phantom $X_\bullet \in RL(\mathcal{Q}) \setminus \mathcal{Q}$, witnessing
\[
  \mathcal{Q} \;\in\; \bigl(\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega} \cap \mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{Noeth}}_{(\infty, \infty)} \cap \mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty, \infty)}\bigr) \setminus \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}
\]
and resolving Conjecture~C1 negatively.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Construct inductively a sequence of finite gaunt commutative absorbing monoids $(\mathcal{M}_k, m_k, 1_k, e_k) \in \mathrm{Cat}_{(0, k)}$: start at $k = 1$ with $\mathcal{M}_1 := [1] = \{e_1 < 1_1\}$, product $m_1 = \wedge$, unit $1_1$, and absorbing element $e_1$. Given $\mathcal{M}_k$, let $\mathcal{M}_{k+1} \in \mathrm{Cat}_{(0, k+1)}$ have object set $\{e_{k+1}, 1_{k+1}\}$ with $\mathrm{Hom}(1_{k+1}, 1_{k+1}) := \mathcal{M}_k$ and $\mathrm{Hom}(e_{k+1}, e_{k+1}) = \mathrm{Hom}(e_{k+1}, 1_{k+1}) = \mathrm{Hom}(1_{k+1}, e_{k+1}) := *$ (unique $1$-cells $f_k : e_{k+1} \to 1_{k+1}$ and $g_k : 1_{k+1} \to e_{k+1}$), setting $g_k \circ f_k = \mathrm{id}_{e_{k+1}}$ and $f_k \circ g_k = e_k$; strict absorption of $e_k$ under $m_k$ ensures associativity and induces a commutative absorbing monoid structure $(m_{k+1}, 1_{k+1}, e_{k+1})$ on $\mathcal{M}_{k+1}$. Set $\mathcal{A}_k := \mathcal{M}_{k+1} \in \mathrm{Cat}_{(0, k+1)}$ with $0_k := e_{k+1}$, so that in every coordinate factor $\mathcal{A}_k$,
\[
  \begin{aligned}
    \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}_k}(0_k, 0_k) &\simeq \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}_k}(0_k, 1_k) \simeq \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}_k}(1_k, 0_k) \simeq *, \\
    \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}_k}(1_k, 1_k) &= \mathcal{M}_k \not\simeq *.
  \end{aligned}
\]
Each $\mathcal{A}_k$ is finite gaunt ($0_k \not\simeq 1_k$), and induction on $k$ gives $\pi_{\le n}(\mathcal{A}_k) \simeq *$ for all $0 \le n \le k$: for $1 \le n \le k$, $\pi_{\le n-1}(\mathcal{M}_k) \simeq *$ collapses $\pi_{\le n-1}^{\mathrm{hom}}(\mathcal{A}_k)$ to the walking isomorphism $J$, whose Rezk completion $\pi_{\le n}(\mathcal{A}_k)$ is $*$, while $\pi_{\le 0}(\mathcal{A}_k) \simeq \pi_{\le 0}(\pi_{\le 1}(\mathcal{A}_k)) \simeq *$.

Define $\mathcal{Q} \subseteq \prod_{k \ge 1} \mathcal{A}_k$ as the full subcategory spanned by the finite-prefix indicator sequences $e^{(m)} := (1_1, \dots, 1_m, 0_{m+1}, 0_{m+2}, \dots)$ ($m \in \mathbb{N}$). Combining tail contractibility $\prod_{k > \max(m, r)} \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}_k}(0_k, 0_k) \simeq *$ with off-diagonal absorbing contraction on the indices $\min(m, r) < k \le \max(m, r)$ (where $\{e^{(m)}_k, e^{(r)}_k\} = \{0_k, 1_k\}$ forces $\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}_k}(e^{(m)}_k, e^{(r)}_k) \simeq *$, formalized as \texttt{qHomAt\_ eq\_ pt\_ of\_ le}) leaves only the diagonal coordinates $1 \le k \le \min(m, r)$ where $e^{(m)}_k = e^{(r)}_k = 1_k$:
\[
  \begin{aligned}
    \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{Q}}(e^{(m)}, e^{(r)}) &\;\simeq\; \prod_{k=1}^{\max(m, r)} \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}_k}(e^{(m)}_k, e^{(r)}_k) \\
    &\;\simeq\; \prod_{k=1}^{\min(m, r)} \mathcal{M}_k \;\in\; \mathrm{Cat}_{(0, \min(m, r))},
  \end{aligned}
\]
which has exact finite hom-rank $\min(m, r)$. Hence $\mathcal{Q}$ is gaunt, locally finite-dimensional of global rank $\mathrm{rk}_S(\mathcal{Q}) = \omega$, Noetherian, and lies in $\mathrm{Cat}_{\le \omega} \cap \mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{Noeth}}_{(\infty, \infty)} \cap \mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty, \infty)}$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:ordinal-rank-completeness}. Yet for each $n \ge 1$, the slice inclusion $\sigma_n = (c_{1_1}, \dots, c_{1_{n-1}}, \mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{A}_n}, c_{0_{n+1}}, \dots) : \mathcal{A}_n \hookrightarrow \mathcal{Q}$ maps $0_n \mapsto e^{(n-1)}$ and $1_n \mapsto e^{(n)}$, pushing $\pi_{\le n}(\mathcal{A}_n) \simeq *$ to an equivalence $\gamma_n : \ell_n(e^{(n)}) \xrightarrow{\sim} \ell_n(e^{(n-1)})$ in $\pi_{\le n}(\mathcal{Q})$. Thus $y_n := \ell_n(e^{(n-1)})$ with transition $\pi_{\le n}(y_{n+1}) \xrightarrow{\sim} \ell_n(e^{(n)}) \xrightarrow{\gamma_n} y_n$ defines a compatible tower $X_\bullet \in RL(\mathcal{Q})$. For every $e^{(m)} \in \mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{Q})$, the $(m+1)$-st projection $\mathrm{pr}_{m+1} : \mathcal{Q} \to \mathcal{A}_{m+1} \in \mathrm{Cat}_{(0, m+2)}$ factors universally through $\overline{\mathrm{pr}}_{m+1} : \pi_{\le m+2}(\mathcal{Q}) \to \mathcal{A}_{m+1}$, separating $\overline{\mathrm{pr}}_{m+1}((X_\bullet)_{m+2}) = 1_{m+1} \not\simeq 0_{m+1} = \overline{\mathrm{pr}}_{m+1}(\ell_{m+2}(e^{(m)}))$ in gaunt $\mathcal{A}_{m+1}$. Hence $X_\bullet \not\simeq \eta_{\mathcal{Q}}(e^{(m)})$ for all $m \in \mathbb{N}$, proving $\mathcal{Q} \notin \mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty, \infty)}$. Every component of this construction and separation is machine-checked in Lean~4 (\texttt{HomotopyCRM/ StepProduct Phantom.lean}, 3,505 lines, 22 \texttt{\#guard\_msgs in \#print axioms} checks in \texttt{KernelAxiomGate}, including \texttt{stepProductQ\_ rank\_ hom}, \texttt{homQ\_ card\_ eq}, and \texttt{theorem12D\_ stepProductQ\_ phantom\_ not\_ realized}).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The Step-Product Phantom as the $(\infty,\infty)$-Categorical Twin of $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}$]\label{rem:step-product-e0-twin}
Comparing $\mathcal{Q} \subset \prod_{k \ge 1} \mathcal{A}_k$ with the smooth-$4$-manifold modulus $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0} = \prod_{\mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2 / \bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2 \simeq 2^{\mathbb{N}} \sslash E_0$ of Chapter~\ref{ch:base-fiber} (Section~\ref{sec:exotic-r4-e0}, Theorem~\ref{thm:double-solvent-r4}) reveals an exact structural parallel across opposite ends of Categorical Reverse Mathematics. In both settings, individual objects carry only finite prefixes ($e^{(m)} \in \mathrm{Ob}(\mathcal{Q})$ vs.\ finite-support elements $\bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2$), while passing to asymptotic invariants---finite Postnikov truncations $\pi_{\le n}$ for $\mathcal{Q}$, or compact interior isotopies and finite-stage Polish quotients $\pi_0^{\mathrm{Pol}}$ for exotic $\mathbb{R}^4$'s---collapses finite prefixes into a single coherent phantom object ($X_\bullet \in RL(\mathcal{Q}) \setminus \mathcal{Q}$ vs.\ the non-splitting condensed class $[q] \in \mathrm{Ext}^1_{\mathrm{CondAb}}(\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}, \bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2)$). Whereas $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{ZFC}}$ obliterates the geometric structure of $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}$ via a Vitali Hamel section, $\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)}$ retains $X_\bullet$ inside $RL(\mathcal{Q}) \setminus \mathcal{Q}$ as a pure Postnikov residuum (Figure~\ref{fig:inf-inf-ladder-phantom}).
\end{remark}

\begin{center}\emph{[Figure omitted in blueprint edition; see Royal Octavo monograph PDF.]}\label{fig:inf-inf-ladder-phantom}\end{center}

On the type-theoretic and algebraic fixed-point fronts, two complementary calibrations anchor Tier~3 ($\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{coind}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$) and relative undercategory perfection:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Grothendieck--Maltsiniotis Weak-$\omega$ Clan Calibration of Infinitely Coherent Type Theory (\texttt{ICaTT}):} Benjamin, Champin, and Markakis~\cite{BCM26} formulate Finster--Mimram's weak $\omega$-categorical type theory $\mathbf{CaTT}$~\cite{FinsterMimram17} and its coinductive-invertibility extension $\mathbf{ICaTT}$ via syntactic clans $\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{CaTT}} \hookrightarrow \mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{ICaTT}}$ whose models in $\mathbf{Clan}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{CaTT}}, \mathbf{Set})$ and over a Grothendieck $1$-topos $\mathbf{Clan}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{CaTT}}, \mathcal{E})$ are \emph{Grothendieck--Maltsiniotis weak $\omega$-category / clan models}~\cite{BCM26,ORW26} (distinct from strict $\omega$-categories). Extending the 3-variable walking-arrow context $(d_0^- : \star) \rhd (d_0^+ : \star) \rhd (d_1 : d_0^- \to d_0^+)$ by a single coinductive witness stream $e_1 : \mathrm{Inv}(d_1)$ under suspension-substitution yields the finite walking-equivalence context $\Gamma_E \equiv \mathcal{E}_1$, whose clan semantics evaluates strictly to Ozornova--Rovelli--Walde's walking coinductive equivalence computad ($\mathcal{J}^*[\![\mathcal{E}_1]\!] \cong \mathcal{E}_1^{\mathrm{OR}}$, Theorem~6.5). Bounded-$\eta$ plus $\beta$-rewriting proves $\mathbf{ICaTT}$ syntactically conservative over $\mathbf{CaTT}$ (Proposition~4.6, Theorem~6.2), while localness with respect to $\Sigma^{n-1}\mathcal{E}_1^{\mathrm{OR}} \to \mathbf{D}^{n-1}$ cuts out \emph{exact Tier~3} ($\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{coind}}_{(\infty, \infty)}$, failing to reach Tier~2 since $E^{(\omega)}$ is $\mathcal{E}_1$-local); internally over Grothendieck $1$-topoi $\mathcal{E}$, Theorem~\ref{thm:coinductive-stream-choice} isolates $\mathrm{IDC}_\omega$ as the exact choice residuum for witness-stream adequacy on Grothendieck--Maltsiniotis weak $\omega$-category / clan models $\mathbf{Clan}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{CaTT}}, \mathcal{E})$.

  \item \textbf{Kernel-Verified Relative Slice-Algebra Perfection (\texttt{HomotopyCRM/ DeltaRing Perfection.lean}):} Goldthorpe's relative undercategory fixed-point construction~\cite[Proposition~2.3.13]{Goldthorpe23}---machine-checked in Lean~4 (\texttt{HomotopyCRM/ DeltaRing Perfection.lean}, 1,398 lines, 4-Layer Gate verified) via \texttt{slice Endofunctor F c} on \texttt{Under c.V}, \texttt{lambek\_ isIso}, \texttt{FixedPoint CoalgebraUnder}, and universal fixed-point coalgebra equivalence (\texttt{initFixedPoint CoalgebraUnder}, \texttt{theoremA\_ homEquiv}, \texttt{theoremA\_ isInitial\_ fixedPointCoalgebraUnder})---realizes the sequential perfection colimit $A_{\mathrm{perf}} = \varinjlim(A \xrightarrow{\phi} A \xrightarrow{\phi} \cdots)$ of a Joyal $\delta$-ring~\cite{Joyal85} in \texttt{DeltaRing2Cat} as the initial slice algebra in \texttt{Under A} (\texttt{perfection SliceAlg}, \texttt{theoremB\_ perfectionSliceAlg\_ isInitial}). Crucially, \emph{shift chirality} sets the Lambek structural isomorphism to the forward colimit shift $\psi_\infty = s$ while forcing the induced Frobenius endomorphism on $A_{\mathrm{perf}}$ to equal its inverse $\phi_{A_{\mathrm{perf}}} = s^{-1}$ (\texttt{theoremB\_ natEndo\_ app\_ eq\_ shift\_ inv}). Moreover, on $p$-torsion commutative rings, Joyal's integral polynomial cofactor $C_p(x, y) = \frac{x^p + y^p - (x+y)^p}{p} = -\sum_{k=1}^{p-1} \frac{1}{p}\binom{p}{k} x^k y^{p-k} \in \mathbb{Z}[x, y]$ ($C_2(x, y) = -xy$) is strictly required: on the $2$-torsion witness $R_2 = \mathbb{Z}[\eta]/(2\eta, \eta^2)$ (decomposing as $\mathbb{Z} \oplus \mathbb{F}_2$ as an additive abelian group only, whereas $R_2 \not\cong \mathbb{Z} \times \mathbb{F}_2$ as rings since $\eta^2 = 0$), the augmentation Frobenius lift $\phi(a + b\eta) = a$ ($\phi(\eta) = 0$) annihilates $2$-torsion yet admits two distinct valid $\delta$-structures $\delta_0(a + b\eta) = \frac{a - a^2}{2} + ab\,\eta$ ($\delta_0(\eta) = 0$) and $\delta_1(a + b\eta) = \frac{a - a^2}{2} + (a + 1)b\,\eta$ ($\delta_1(\eta) = \eta$), alongside the torsion-free dual-integer witness \texttt{DualInt} ($\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2)$, with 1-step perfection $\mathbb{Z}$). As a strict guardrail, this slice initial-algebra model constructs \emph{relative perfection $A_{\mathrm{perf}}$ only}, and never models general non-perfect prisms $(A, I)$ or the absolute prismatic site $(R)_{\Prism}$~\cite{BhattScholze22}, where forcing a Lambek initial-algebra isomorphism inverts $\phi$ and destroys the Nygaard filtration (\texttt{Checks. dualInt\_ nygaard\_ separation}).
\end{itemize}

\section{Univalence, Higher Inductive Types, and Non-AC Homotopy Lattices}

Just as Chapter~\ref{ch:non-ac-lattices} extended 1-categorical reverse mathematics beyond $\mathrm{AC}$ to analysis, algebra, and differential geometry, Higher Categorical Reverse Mathematics calibrates the non-choice theorem lattices of \emph{Synthetic Homotopy Theory} and \emph{Higher Differential Geometry} inside an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$.

\subsection{The Universe Lattice: Weak Classifiers to Voevodsky Univalence}

In an $(\infty, 1)$-category $\mathcal{X}$ with finite limits, a universe (or object classifier) for a class of $\kappa$-small morphisms is an object $\mathcal{U}$ equipped with a universal small fibration $\widetilde{\mathcal{U}} \to \mathcal{U}$ such that every $\kappa$-small morphism $X \to Y$ is a pullback of $\widetilde{\mathcal{U}} \to \mathcal{U}$. In a weak higher pretopos, the classifying map $Y \to \mathcal{U}$ need only be weakly unique (surjective on equivalences).

Elevating weak classification ($A$, sufficient for basic type dependency) to Voevodsky's \emph{Univalence Axiom} ($B$, asserting that the canonical map $\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{U}}(X, Y) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathrm{Equiv}(X, Y)$ is an equivalence for all $X, Y: \mathcal{U}$, enabling structure identity principles and descent) isolates Colimit Universality and Descent (Giraud's $\infty$-Axioms) as the exact structural residuum on $\mathcal{X}$.

\subsection{The Homotopical Recursion Lattice: $W$-Types to Higher Inductive Types (HITs)}

In Chapter~\ref{ch:non-ac-lattices} (\S\ref{sec:predicativity-wtypes}), we formulated the principle that in a 1-topos or $\Pi$-pretopos, the categorical residuum bridging arithmetical comprehension ($\mathrm{ACA}_0$) to transfinite bar recursion ($\mathrm{ATR}_0$) is the existence of initial algebras for polynomial endofunctors (Martin-Löf $W$-types).

In an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$, ordinary $W$-types generate $0$-truncated well-founded trees, which cannot construct higher spheres $S^n$, suspensions $\Sigma X$, Eilenberg--MacLane spaces $K(G, n)$, or homotopy pushouts $A \amalg^h_C B$. Taking $A$ as $0$-truncated $W$-type recursion and $B$ as Synthetic Homotopy Theory (such as the calculation $\pi_1(S^1) \simeq \mathbb{Z}$ or the Seifert--van Kampen theorem), the categorical residuum $(A \Rightarrow_{(\infty,1)\text{-}\mathbf{Cat}} B)$ is the existence of \emph{Higher Inductive Types (HITs)}---specifically, universal homotopy pushouts and initial algebras for polynomial endofunctors enriched with path constructors.

\subsection{The Excision Lattice: Blakers--Massey, Freudenthal Suspension, and Directed Stabilization in $\mathrm{CatSp}$}\label{subsec:excision-catsp}

In classical algebraic topology, the Freudenthal Suspension Theorem ($\pi_{k+n}(S^n)$ stabilizes for $n > k+1$) and the Blakers--Massey Homotopy Excision Theorem (measuring how far a homotopy pushout square is from being a homotopy pullback square) are proved using singular chains or CW-approximation.

In Higher $\mathrm{CRM}$, these theorems hold constructively inside any $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$ without invoking $\mathrm{LEM}$ or $\mathrm{AC}$. When we weaken the ambient universe $\mathcal{X}$ to a general lex $(\infty, 1)$-category with pushouts and ask for the categorical residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{X})$ that bridges basic homotopy pushouts ($A$) to the \emph{Blakers--Massey Excision and Freudenthal Suspension Theorems} ($B$), Anel--Biedermann--Finster--Joyal~\cite{ABFJ20} proved that the exact residuum is \emph{universality of colimits and modality descent} (the $(\infty, 1)$-pretopos exactness axioms).

When passing to stable and directed stable homotopy, a fundamental Giraud exactness obstruction governs the ambient meta-domain: because Giraud universality of colimits makes the initial object $\emptyset$ strict ($\emptyset \times X \simeq \emptyset$) in every $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X}$, adjoining a zero object ($\emptyset \simeq 1$) collapses $\mathcal{X} \simeq *$, so no non-trivial ``stable $(\infty, 1)$-topos'' exists, and symmetric monoidal stabilization $\Sigma^\infty_+ : \mathcal{S} \to \mathrm{Sp}$ in $\mathrm{Pr}^L$ sends the terminal space $*$ (never an object classifier $\mathcal{U}$ or general object $U$) to the sphere spectrum $\Sigma^\infty_+(*) \simeq \mathbb{S}$. Likewise, in the directed setting, stabilizing pointed $(\infty, \infty)$-categories $\mathrm{Cat}^R_* := (\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty, \infty)})_{*/}$ along reduced directed suspension and directed looping $\Sigma_{\mathrm{red}} \dashv \Omega_{\mathrm{dir}}$ forms the presentable $(\infty, 1)$-category of \emph{categorical spectra} $\mathrm{CatSp} := \mathrm{Sp}(\mathrm{Cat}^R_*)$ across the lifted four-tier chain $\mathrm{CatSp}^L \subsetneq \mathrm{CatSp}^\omega \subsetneq \mathrm{CatSp}^{\mathrm{coind}} \subsetneq \mathrm{CatSp}^R$~\cite{Masuda26,Heine26,ORW26}:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Masuda v2 Steiner Partial-Duality Correction and 3-Periodic Lax/Bifiber Triad:} In~\cite[Section~2.4, Footnote~5]{Masuda26} (crediting Marcus Nicolas), Masuda withdraws v1's Proposition~2.4.17 regarding odd/even partial duals $(-)^{\mathrm{op}}, (-)^{\mathrm{co}}$ on strong Steiner complexes---refuted by the pinched $2$-cube $\square^2 \cup_{\{10, 01\}} \square^1$---retaining monoidal preservation of $\infty\mathbf{Gaunt}_{\mathrm{Ste}}$ under the total dual $(-)^{\circ} = (-)^{\mathrm{coop}}$ and establishing a \emph{3-periodic lax/bifiber triad} in $\mathrm{CatSp}$ (Theorem~5.3.1 and Remark~5.3.4): any morphism $f : \Omega_{\mathrm{dir}} X \to Y$ induces $\overrightarrow{\mathrm{cof}}(f) \xrightarrow{\sim} Z \xrightarrow{\sim} \overrightarrow{\mathrm{fib}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{dir}} f)$ with $Y \to Z \to \Sigma_{\mathrm{dir}} X$ a non-lax \emph{bifiber sequence}, cycling with strict period~$3$ through lax cofiber sequences ($\overrightarrow{\mathrm{cof}}$), bifiber sequences, and lax fiber sequences ($\overrightarrow{\mathrm{fib}}$).
  \item \textbf{Gei\ss's Oriented Freudenthal Suspension Theorem:} Extending Freudenthal stability to directed $\omega$-categories without group-completion collapse, Gei\ss~\cite[Theorem~5.1 and Corollary~5.14]{Geiss26} proves that for every pointed $n$-connected $\omega$-category $\mathcal{C}$, the directed unit $\mathcal{C} \to \Omega_{\mathrm{dir}}\Sigma_{\mathrm{red}}\mathcal{C}$ is $2n$-connected---established via a $2$-shifted $T^2$ comparison and the $(n + m + 2)$-connected Cartesian pushout-product bound (Theorem~4.13) on the directed James construction $\Omega_{\mathrm{dir}}\Sigma_{\mathrm{red}}\mathcal{C} \simeq \mathcal{J}(\mathcal{C}) = \varinjlim_k J_k(\mathcal{C})$---stabilizing $\pi_{r+k}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{red}}^k \mathcal{C})$ for all $k \ge \max(0, r - 2n)$ without inverting directed $1$-cells ($\tau_0(\Sigma^\infty_{\mathrm{dir}} S^0) \cong \mathbb{N}$ vs.\ $\pi_0(\mathbb{S}) \cong \mathbb{Z}$).
\end{itemize}

\subsection{The Differential Cohesive $(\infty, 1)$-Lattice: Higher Gauge Theory}

In Chapter~\ref{ch:non-ac-lattices} (\S\ref{sec:cohesive-lattice}), we saw that Lawvere's cohesive adjoint quadruple $\CohesiveQuad$ over a 1-topos $\mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ bridges local infinitesimal calculus to global de Rham cohomology. Passing to an $(\infty, 1)$-topos $\mathcal{X} \to \infty\text{-}\mathbf{Grpd}$ equipped with Urs Schreiber's \emph{differential cohesion}~\cite{Schreiber13} ($\CohesiveQuad$ combined with an infinitesimal shape modality $\mathrm{Im} \dashv \mathrm{Inf}$) unlocks the categorical reverse mathematics of \emph{higher gauge theory and Chern--Weil theory}:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Local $n$-Connections to Global Principal $\infty$-Bundles:} Taking $A$ as local Lie $\infty$-algebra valued differential forms on coordinate charts and $B$ as global principal $\infty$-bundles with connection (such as $B^2 U(1)$ bundle gerbes in string and M-theory), the residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{X})$ is cohesive descent and $\Pi$-product preservation.

  \item \textbf{Flat Connections to Differential Cohomology Hexagon:} Bridging local curvature $2$-forms ($A$) to the global Simons--Sullivan differential cohomology hexagon ($B$) isolates the exactness of the cohesive fracture square between $\Pi$ (homotopy shape) and $\mathrm{Disc}$ (discrete underlying geometry).

\end{itemize}

\section{Summary of Higher Categorical Residuums in $(\infty, 1)$- and $(\infty, \infty)$-Contexts}

\begin{center}\emph{[Summary ledger table omitted in blueprint edition; see Royal Octavo monograph PDF.]}\end{center}

\chapter{Site-Level Combinatorics, Realizability Topoi, and Computational Reverse Mathematics}

\label{ch:realizability-sites}

\section{From Sheaf Topoi to Realizability Universes}\label{sec:kleene-eff}

Having calibrated the summit of higher categorical Reverse Mathematics in $(\infty, 1)$-topoi and weak $(\infty, \infty)$-categories in Chapter~\ref{ch:higher-crm}, we now investigate the combinatorial and computational engines that generate them. Base $1$-topoi furnish the foundational universes inside which weak $\omega$-categories, relational coinductive streams, and discrete or profinite symmetry invariants are internalized. Chapter~\ref{ch:realizability-sites} unites three distinct combinatorial pillars:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Computability and Realizability Topoi (\S\ref{sec:kleene-eff}--\S\ref{sec:ltt-topologies}):} Calibrating constructive reverse mathematics ($\mathrm{WKL}_0, \mathrm{FAN}$) over Partial Combinatory Algebras via Lawvere--Tierney modal operators in $\mathbf{LTT}(\Eff)$.
  \item \textbf{Site Combinatorics and Fra\"iss\'e Permutation Topoi (\S\ref{sec:site-combinatorics}--\S\ref{subsec:kihara-choice-nominal}):} Discovering the Fra\"iss\'e atomic-topology escape from Left Bousfield groupoidification, classifying Kihara's five-rung atomic choice spectrum, and computing nominal parity Galois cohomology.
  \item \textbf{Directed Combinatorial Geometry and Higher Coinductive Streams (\S\ref{subsec:cubical-amalgamation}--\S\ref{subsec:coinductive-streams-choice}):} Machine-checking binary Strong Amalgamation ($\mathrm{SAP}$) along flow-convex embeddings, proving the all-$n \ge 3$ cubical amalgamation phase transition obstructed by facet cycle diagrams $F_n$, and calibrating internal coinductive invertibility streams ($\mathbf{ICaTT}$) against internal Dependent Choice ($\mathrm{IDC}_\omega$).
\end{enumerate}

In the preceding chapters, our primary models of variable mathematical universes were Grothendieck sheaf topoi $\mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}(\mathbb{C}, J)$ over topological spaces, locales, and algebraic sites, as well as their higher $(\infty, 1)$-topos generalizations. Yet as noted in Chapter~\ref{ch:foundations}, Categorical Reverse Mathematics ($\mathrm{CRM}$) is designed to transfer functorially across \emph{all} structured elementary topoi, including a second major domain of topos theory: \emph{realizability topoi} arising from computability theory.

Whereas a Grothendieck sheaf topos measures \emph{spatial and topological variation} (whether locally defined sections glue coherently across open coverings), a realizability topos measures \emph{algorithmic and computational witnessability} (whether existential and implicational statements are witnessed by uniform partial recursive programs).

To construct a realizability universe, one begins with a \emph{Partial Combinatory Algebra (PCA)} $\mathcal{A} = (A, \cdot)$---a set $A$ equipped with a partial binary application operation $(a, b) \mapsto a \cdot b$ possessing combinators $\mathsf{k}, \mathsf{s} \in A$ that satisfy functional completeness (the $\lambda$-abstraction theorem):

\[
  \mathsf{k} \cdot a \cdot b = a, \qquad \mathsf{s} \cdot a \cdot b \downarrow \quad \text{and} \quad \mathsf{s} \cdot a \cdot b \cdot c \simeq (a \cdot c) \cdot (b \cdot c),
\]
where $\downarrow$ indicates definedness (termination of the partial application) and $\simeq$ denotes Kleene strong equality (meaning that if either side is defined, then both sides are defined and equal).

Three canonical PCAs anchor computational reverse mathematics:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Kleene's First Algebra ($\mathcal{K}_1$):} The underlying set is $\mathbb{N}$, and $e \cdot n \simeq \varphi_e(n)$ is the result of applying the $e$-th Turing machine (partial recursive function) to input $n \in \mathbb{N}$.

  \item \textbf{Oracle / Relative Kleene Algebras ($\mathcal{K}_1^O$):} The underlying set is $\mathbb{N}$, with application $e \cdot_O n \simeq \varphi_e^O(n)$ given by Turing machines equipped with an external oracle $O \subseteq \mathbb{N}$ (such as the halting jump $\emptyset'$ or hyperarithmetical oracles).

  \item \textbf{Kleene's Second Algebra ($\mathcal{K}_2$):} The underlying set is Baire space $\mathbb{N}^\mathbb{N}$, where $\alpha \cdot \beta$ is continuous tracking application (governed by Brouwerian continuity and fan functionals).

\end{itemize}

Via the Hyland--Johnstone--Pitts~\cite{HJP80} \emph{tripos-to-topos construction}, any PCA $\mathcal{A}$ over a base topos $\mathbf{Set}$ generates an elementary \emph{realizability topos} $\mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{A})$ whose objects $(X, =_X)$ are sets $X$ equipped with $\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})$-valued partial equivalence relations $=_X: X \times X \to \mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})$ assigning to each pair $(x, x')$ the set of realizers witnessing $x = x'$.

When $\mathcal{A} = \mathcal{K}_1$, $\mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{K}_1)$ is Martin Hyland's Effective Topos ($\Eff$)~\cite{Hyland82,vanOosten08}. From the perspective of Categorical Reverse Mathematics, $\Eff$ exhibits a striking combination of structural invariants that cannot occur in any spatial Grothendieck topos:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Two-Valuedness Without Booleanness:} The terminal object $1 \in \Eff$ is externally projective and indecomposable ($\Eff$ is two-valued), yet $\Eff$ is strongly non-Boolean ($\Omega \not\simeq 1+1$) because a proposition $\phi$ is true in $\Eff$ if and only if it possesses a computable realizer.

  \item \textbf{Non-Grothendieck Exactness:} $\Eff$ is an elementary topos with a Natural Numbers Object ($\mathrm{NNO}$), but it has no points (no geometric morphisms $\mathbf{Set} \to \Eff$) and is not cocomplete over $\mathbf{Set}$.

  \item \textbf{Internal Church's Thesis ($\mathrm{CT}$) and Markov's Principle ($\mathrm{MP}$):} Internally in $\Eff$, \emph{every} function $f: \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{N}$ is computable ($\Eff \models \mathrm{CT}$), and double-negation elimination holds for decidable existential predicates over $\mathbb{N}$ ($\Eff \models \mathrm{MP}: \forall n\,(\phi(n) \vee \neg\phi(n)) \wedge \neg\neg\exists n\,\phi(n) \to \exists n\,\phi(n)$).

  \item \textbf{Countable and Dependent Choice Without Full Choice:} Because a realizer for $\forall n \in \mathbb{N}\,\exists x \in X\, R(n, x)$ is already a computable function extracting a witness for each $n$, $\Eff$ validates Countable Choice ($\mathrm{CC}_{\mathbb{N}}$) and Dependent Choice ($\mathrm{DC}_{\mathbb{N}}$) over $\mathbb{N}$ (and more generally over any modest/projective object), while refuting full $\mathrm{AC}$ (since $\mathrm{AC} \implies \mathrm{LEM}$ by Diaconescu's theorem, Chapter~\ref{ch:choice-atlas}, Theorem~\ref{thm:diaconescu}).

\end{itemize}

\section{The Computability Residuum Atlas: Kleene Trees, Weak König's Lemma, and Lawvere--Tierney Oracles}\label{sec:ltt-topologies}

Because classical Friedman--Simpson reverse mathematics is built over recursive comprehension ($\mathrm{RCA}_0$), calibrating the classical ``Big Five'' inside realizability topoi $\mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{A})$ reveals the exact categorical bridge between \emph{computability theory} and \emph{topos geometry}.

\subsection{The Failure of Weak König's Lemma ($\mathrm{WKL}$) and the Kleene Tree Obstruction}

In classical reverse mathematics over $\mathrm{RCA}_0$, the first major reversal tier above computable mathematics is Weak König's Lemma ($\mathrm{WKL}_0$): every infinite binary tree $T \subseteq 2^{<\mathbb{N}}$ possesses an infinite path $\alpha \in 2^{\mathbb{N}}$. In recursion theory, $\mathrm{WKL}_0$ fails in the minimal $\omega$-model $\mathrm{REC}$ of computable sets because of \emph{Kleene's recursive tree} $T_{\mathrm{Kleene}} \subseteq 2^{<\mathbb{N}}$---an infinite, computable binary tree separating two disjoint recursively inseparable enumerable sets, which possesses no computable infinite branch.

Inside the Effective Topos $\Eff = \mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{K}_1)$, internal $\mathrm{WKL}$ fails for the exact same reason: $T_{\mathrm{Kleene}}$ is an internal infinite binary tree in $\Eff$, but because $\Eff \models \mathrm{CT}$, any internal infinite branch $\alpha: \mathbb{N} \to 2$ would have to be a computable function in $\mathbf{Set}$, which does not exist. Similarly, the contrapositive of $\mathrm{WKL}$---Brouwer's Fan Theorem ($\mathrm{FAN}$), asserting that every bar on $2^{\mathbb{N}}$ is uniform (equivalent to the internal compactness of the Cantor space object $2^{\mathbb{N}}$)---\emph{also} fails in $\Eff$, whereas $\mathrm{FAN}$ holds in Kleene's function-realizability topos $\mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{K}_2)$ and in the sheaf topos $\mathbf{Sh}(\mathbb{N}^\mathbb{N})$.

What is the categorical residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ that bridges constructive recursive arithmetic ($A = \mathrm{HA}^\omega + \mathrm{DC}_{\mathbb{N}}$) to $\mathrm{WKL}$ or $\mathrm{FAN}$ inside a realizability universe $\mathcal{E} = \mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{A})$?

\begin{theorem}[The Realizability Residuums for $\mathrm{FAN}$ and $\mathrm{WKL}$]\label{thm:realizability-fan-wkl}%
\uses{def:comparison-triple,def:residuum}%

Let $\mathcal{E} = \mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{A})$ be the realizability topos over a Partial Combinatory Algebra $\mathcal{A}$.

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{The Compactness / Fan Residuum ($\mathrm{RCA}_0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{FAN}$):} Elevating basic recursive arithmetic ($A$) to internal Cantor compactness / the Fan Theorem ($B = \mathrm{FAN}$, sufficient for uniform continuity and the Extreme Value Theorem on $[0,1]$) isolates the \emph{Baire-continuity / fan-functional structure} of the PCA $\mathcal{A}$ (passing from discrete Turing application $\mathcal{K}_1$ to continuous Kleene application $\mathcal{K}_2$, or restricting to the localic reflection of $2^{\mathbb{N}}$).

  \item \textbf{The Lifschitz / Low-Oracle Residuum ($\mathrm{RCA}_0 \Rightarrow \mathrm{WKL}_0$):} Elevating recursive arithmetic ($A$) to actual branch selection in infinite binary trees ($B = \mathrm{WKL}$) without collapsing to full classical logic isolates \emph{Lifschitz bounded-non-determinism realizability} (where realizers encode finite non-empty candidate sets $e \subseteq \mathbb{N}$ closed under König tree pruning, or where the PCA $\mathcal{A}$ is closed under Jockusch--Soare diagonally non-computable / $\mathrm{PA}$-degree oracles).

\end{itemize}

\end{theorem}

\subsection{Lawvere--Tierney Topologies and Weihrauch Degrees}\label{subsec:ltt-weihrauch}

In modern computable analysis, the finer structure of reverse mathematics is calibrated by \emph{Weihrauch reducibility} ($f \le_W g$): a computational problem (multi-valued map) $f: \subseteq X \rightrightarrows Y$ is Weihrauch reducible to $g: \subseteq Z \rightrightarrows W$ if there exist computable pre- and post-processors $\Phi, \Psi$ such that $f(x)$ is solved by a single oracle call to $g(\Phi(x))$.

In Categorical Reverse Mathematics, Sori Lee and Jaap van Oosten~\cite{LeeVanOosten13,vanOosten08} characterized the lattice of basic local operators ($j: \Omega \to \Omega$) on the Effective Topos, while Peter Lietz and Thomas Streicher~\cite{LietzStreicher12} exhibited realizability separations refuting semi-classical principles such as $\mathrm{BD}$-$\mathbb{N}$; together they demonstrated that the hierarchy of computational principles inside a realizability topos $\mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{A})$ is governed by the lattice of \emph{Lawvere--Tierney topologies (local operators)} $j: \Omega \to \Omega$. Every Lawvere--Tierney topology $j$ on $\mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{A})$ defines a full reflective \emph{sheaf subtopos} $\mathbf{Sh}_j(\mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{A})) \hookrightarrow \mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{A})$ whose internal logic forces all $j$-dense propositions to become true.

\begin{theorem}[The Lawvere--Tierney Subtopos Residuum Principle]\label{thm:ltt-residuum}%
\uses{thm:realizability-fan-wkl,thm:omega-dictionary}%

Let $\mathcal{E}$ be an elementary topos (such as $\Eff = \mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{K}_1)$), and let $A \implies B$ be a deductive reversal between internal mathematical principles where $\mathcal{E} \models A$, $\mathcal{E} \not\models B$, and $B$ is a \emph{$j$-hereditary} principle (closed upward along passing to smaller sheaf subtopoi $\mathbf{Sh}_{j'}(\mathcal{E}) \hookrightarrow \mathbf{Sh}_j(\mathcal{E})$, such as a geometric sequent or modal forcing condition). Within the lattice of subtopoi $\mathbf{SubTopos}(\mathcal{E}) \simeq \mathbf{LTT}(\mathcal{E})^{\mathrm{op}}$, the categorical residuum $(A \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{SubTopos}(\mathcal{E})} B)$ is the \emph{minimal Lawvere--Tierney modal operator} $j_{(A \Rightarrow B)}: \Omega \to \Omega$ generated by forcing $B$, such that for any subtopos $\mathcal{E}_j = \mathbf{Sh}_j(\mathcal{E}) \hookrightarrow \mathcal{E}$:

\[
  \mathcal{E}_j \models B \iff j \ge j_{(A \Rightarrow B)} \quad \text{in } \mathbf{LTT}(\mathcal{E}).
\]
(By contrast, anti-classical principles that are \emph{not} $j$-hereditary, such as Church's Thesis $\mathrm{CT}$, hold at $j = \mathrm{id}_\Omega$ in $\Eff$ yet fail in the strictly smaller subtopoi $\mathbf{Sh}_{j_{\mathrm{Lif}}}(\Eff)$ and $\mathbf{Sh}_{\neg\neg}(\Eff) \simeq \mathbf{Set}$.)

\end{theorem}

In accordance with the Admissibility Criterion of Chapter~\ref{ch:foundations} (\S\ref{sec:admissibility}), while $j_{(A \Rightarrow B)}: \Omega \to \Omega$ is abstractly the relative pseudocomplement in $\mathbf{LTT}(\Eff)$, genuinely solving the $\mathrm{CRM}$ problem requires characterizing $j_{(A \Rightarrow B)}$ as an explicit computational or oracle modality on the underlying PCA (such as the Lifschitz operator $j_{\mathrm{Lif}}$ or a Turing-jump operator $j_d$). Specifically, ascending the Lawvere--Tierney lattice inside $\Eff$ calibrates the exact computational cost of classical reversals:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Identity Topology ($j = \mathrm{id}_\Omega$):} Validates $\mathrm{CT}$, $\mathrm{MP}$, $\mathrm{DC}_{\mathbb{N}}$, and the Cauchy real line $\mathbb{R}_C \simeq \mathbb{R}_D$ (because $\mathrm{CC}_{\mathbb{N}}$ holds in $\Eff$), while refuting $\mathrm{WKL}$, $\mathrm{LLPO}$, and $\mathrm{LEM}$.

  \item \textbf{Lifschitz / $\mathrm{LLPO}$ Topology ($j_{\mathrm{Lif}}$):} Forces the Lesser Limited Principle of Omniscience ($\mathrm{LLPO}$) and Weak König's Lemma ($\mathrm{WKL}$) for bounded-multiplicity trees, refuting $\mathrm{CT}$ while remaining non-Boolean.

  \item \textbf{Turing-Jump Topologies ($j_O$ for Turing degrees $d = \mathrm{deg}(O)$):} Each Turing degree $d$ induces a Lawvere--Tierney topology $j_d: \Omega \to \Omega$ whose subtopos $\mathbf{Sh}_{j_d}(\Eff)$ is equivalent to the relative realizability topos $\mathbf{RT}(\mathcal{K}_1^O)$; iterating the Turing jump $d \mapsto d'$ along $\mathbb{N}$ climbs from $\mathrm{RCA}_0$ to arithmetical comprehension $\mathrm{ACA}_0$.

  \item \textbf{Double-Negation Topology ($j_{\neg\neg} = \neg\neg: \Omega \to \Omega$):} The maximum dense Lawvere--Tierney topology on $\Eff$, whose sheaf subtopos $\mathbf{Sh}_{\neg\neg}(\Eff) \simeq \mathbf{Set}$ collapses the entire computational hierarchy back into classical Boolean set theory.

\end{itemize}

\section{The Site-Level Combinatorial Dictionary for Grothendieck Topoi}\label{sec:site-combinatorics}

When we return from realizability topoi to Grothendieck sheaf topoi $\mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}(\mathbb{C}, J)$ over a small category $\mathbb{C}$ equipped with a Grothendieck topology $J$, a natural question arises: how do abstract categorical residuums $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$---such as Stone/De Morgan structure on $\Omega$, Booleanness, or internal choice---translate into concrete combinatorial properties on the morphisms and covering sieves of the site $(\mathbb{C}, J)$?

Drawing on Peter Johnstone's \emph{Sketches of an Elephant}~\cite{Johnstone02} and Olivia Caramello's theory of site-level invariants~\cite{Caramello18}, Categorical Reverse Mathematics establishes a direct \emph{Site-Combinatorial Residuum Dictionary}:

\begin{theorem}[Combinatorial Characterization of Logical and Choice Residuums on Sites]\label{thm:site-dictionary}%
\uses{thm:omega-dictionary,thm:bdf}%

Let $\mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}(\mathbb{C}, J)$ be the Grothendieck topos of sheaves on a site $(\mathbb{C}, J)$. Then the primary structural residuums $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ pull back to the following combinatorial properties on $(\mathbb{C}, J)$:

\begin{enumerate}

  \item \textbf{The De Morgan ($\mathrm{WLEM}$) Site Residuum (The $J$-Amalgamation / Ore Condition):} The subobject classifier $\Omega_{\mathcal{E}}$ is an internal De Morgan (Stone) lattice ($\mathcal{E} \models \neg\phi \vee \neg\neg\phi$) if and only if for every object $U \in \mathbb{C}$ and every pair of $J$-closed sieves $R_1, R_2$ on $U$ whose intersection $R_1 \cap R_2$ contains only $J$-empty morphisms, there exists a $J$-covering sieve $S \in J(U)$ such that for every $f: V \to U$ in $S$, either $f^*(R_1)$ is $J$-empty or $f^*(R_2)$ is $J$-empty. In a contravariant presheaf topos $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}$ ($J$ trivial), this holds if and only if $\mathbb{C}$ satisfies the \emph{Right Ore condition on cospans}: every cospan $V_1 \xrightarrow{f_1} U \xleftarrow{f_2} V_2$ in $\mathbb{C}$ can be completed to a commutative square $V_1 \xleftarrow{g_1} W \xrightarrow{g_2} V_2$ with $f_1 g_1 = f_2 g_2$ (note that completing spans $V_1 \leftarrow U \rightarrow V_2$ is the dual condition for covariant presheaves $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{C}}$; see Johnstone, \emph{Sketches of an Elephant}, \S D4.6, D4.6.3~\cite{Johnstone02}).

  \item \textbf{The Gödel--Dummett Linearity Site Residuum (Directedness / Tree Condition):} $\mathcal{E} \models (\phi \to \psi) \vee (\psi \to \phi)$ if and only if for every pair of sieves $R_1, R_2$ on $U$, there is a $J$-cover $S \in J(U)$ on which either $f^*(R_1) \subseteq f^*(R_2)$ or $f^*(R_2) \subseteq f^*(R_1)$. For a poset site $\mathcal{P}$ with the trivial topology, this holds if and only if $\mathcal{P}$ is a \emph{forest of trees} (every principal down-set/cone is linearly ordered).

  \item \textbf{The Booleanness ($\mathrm{LEM}$) Site Residuum (Dense-Covering Collapse / Groupoid Condition):} $\mathcal{E}$ is Boolean ($\Omega_{\mathcal{E}} \simeq 1+1$) if and only if every $J$-dense sieve on any object $U \in \mathbb{C}$ is already a $J$-covering sieve in $J(U)$ (meaning $J = J_{\neg\neg}$ coincides with the double-negation topology on $\mathbb{C}$). For a presheaf topos $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}$ with the trivial topology, $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}$ is Boolean if and only if the category $\mathbb{C}$ is a \emph{groupoid} (every morphism $f: V \to U$ in $\mathbb{C}$ is an isomorphism).

  \item \textbf{The Global Choice ($\mathrm{AC}$) Site Residuum (Split-Cover Refinement):} Over $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{AC}}$, $\mathbf{Sh}(\mathbb{C}, J) \models \mathrm{AC}$ if and only if $(\mathbb{C}, J)$ is Morita-equivalent to the canonical topology on a complete Boolean algebra $B$, so that every $J$-covering sieve refines to a family of mutually disjoint subterminal projections that split globally.

\end{enumerate}

\end{theorem}

\section{Group-Action and Permutation Topoi ($\mathbf{Set}^G$, Schanuel's Topos, and Fraïssé Automorphism Groups)}\label{sec:permutation-topoi}

Theorem~\ref{thm:site-dictionary}(3) identifies an important family of Boolean universes: whenever $\mathcal{G}$ is a group (viewed as a one-object groupoid), the presheaf topos $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{G}}$ of $\mathcal{G}$-sets is automatically a \emph{Boolean topos} ($\Omega \simeq 1+1$). More generally, if $\mathcal{G}$ is a \emph{topological group} equipped with a neighborhood base of open subgroups $\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{G})$, the category $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$ of discrete sets equipped with a \emph{continuous} $\mathcal{G}$-action (where every element $x \in X$ has an open stabilizer subgroup $\mathrm{Stab}_{\mathcal{G}}(x) \in \mathcal{U}(\mathcal{G})$) forms an \emph{atomic, two-valued, Boolean Grothendieck topos}.

In foundational logic, continuous group-action topoi $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$ are the exact categorical incarnation of \emph{Fraenkel--Mostowski--Specker permutation models} of set theory with atoms ($\mathrm{ZFA} + \neg\mathrm{AC}$) and \emph{Fraïssé homogeneous model theory}, exhibiting a sharp dichotomy between discrete groups possessing a minimal open subgroup $\{1\}$ and non-discrete oligomorphic Polish groups possessing no minimal open subgroup (Kihara~\cite[Theorems~6.2 and~6.19]{Kihara26}):

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Discrete Group Topoi ($\mathbf{Set}^G$):} For any non-trivial discrete group $G \neq \{1\}$, the trivial subgroup $\{1\} \subsetneq G$ \emph{is} open, so the principal torsor $G = G/\{1\}$ is a minimal-stabilizer atom and an internally projective generator ($G \times A \twoheadrightarrow A_{\mathrm{triv}} \cong A$). Consequently, over $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{AC}}$, $\mathbf{Set}^G$ is Boolean and satisfies both the Internal Axiom of Choice ($\mathrm{IAC}$) and $\mathrm{COSHEP}$ (Kihara~\cite{Kihara26}, Theorem~6.2). However, $\mathbf{Set}^G$ still fails External Choice ($\mathrm{EAC}$ / $\mathrm{AC}$) and localicity because the terminal object $1$ is \emph{not} externally projective: the unique map $G \twoheadrightarrow 1$ from the free transitive $G$-set $G$ onto the trivial $G$-set $1$ is an epimorphism with empty global sections $\Gamma(G) = G^G = \emptyset$ (Kihara~\cite{Kihara26}, Theorem~6.19).

  \item \textbf{Schanuel's Topos and Non-Discrete Fraïssé Permutation Topoi ($\mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{M}))$):} Let $\mathcal{M}$ be a countable ultrahomogeneous relational structure (a \emph{Fraïssé limit}, such as an infinite countable set $I$ with equality, dense linear order $(\mathbb{Q}, <)$, or the Rado graph), and let $\mathcal{G} = \mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{M})$ be its Polish topological automorphism group equipped with the pointwise convergence topology (where open subgroups are pointwise stabilizers $\mathcal{G}_{(F)}$ of finite substructures $F \subseteq_{\mathrm{fin}} \mathcal{M}$). Then $\mathcal{E} = \mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{M}))$ (known as \emph{Schanuel's nominal topos} $\mathbf{Nom}$ when $\mathcal{M}$ is a pure infinite set) is a two-valued ($\mathrm{Sub}(1) = \{0, 1\}$), Boolean, atomic Grothendieck topos. For \emph{every} topological group $\mathcal{G}$ possessing a proper open subgroup $U \subsetneq \mathcal{G}$, the homogeneous coset surjection $\mathcal{G}/U \twoheadrightarrow 1$ is an epimorphism in $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$ with empty global sections $\Gamma(\mathcal{G}/U) = (\mathcal{G}/U)^{\mathcal{G}} = \emptyset$ (\texttt{isEmpty\_ globalSections\_ quotient\_ of\_ lt\_ top} and \texttt{Checks. layer2\_ sep\_ guardrail5\_ coset\_ globalSections} in \texttt{HomotopyCRM/ NominalSimplicity.lean}), so $1$ is \emph{not} externally projective and localicity fails unless $\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{G}) = \{\mathcal{G}\}$ ($\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G}) \simeq \mathbf{Set}$). Moreover, in non-discrete oligomorphic Fraïssé permutation topoi $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{M}))$ without minimal open subgroups (where $\{1\}$ is not open, \texttt{Checks.not\_isOpenSubgroup\_bot}, since $\mathcal{G}_{(F \cup \{a\})} \subsetneq \mathcal{G}_{(F)}$), no projective atom exists, so external projectivity of $1$ ($\mathrm{EAC}$), localicity, $\mathrm{COSHEP}$, $\mathrm{IAC}$, and $\mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{Set}}$ (for unstable $\mathcal{M}$) or $\mathrm{AC}$ all fail, with external projectivity of $1$, localicity, $\mathrm{COSHEP}$, $\mathrm{IAC}$, and $\mathrm{AC}$ collapsing together to $\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{G}) = \{\mathcal{G}\}$ ($\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G}) \simeq \mathbf{Set}$).

\end{itemize}

Why do external projectivity of $1$ and Choice fail in $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{M}))$ even though the topos is Boolean and two-valued? Because whenever a proper open subgroup $U \subsetneq \mathcal{G}$ exists (such as $\mathcal{G}_{(F)}$ for any non-empty finite support $F \subseteq_{\mathrm{fin}} \mathcal{M}$), the inhabited homogeneous orbit $\mathcal{G}/U \twoheadrightarrow 1$ admits no $\mathcal{G}$-invariant global section ($\Gamma(\mathcal{G}/U) = (\mathcal{G}/U)^{\mathcal{G}} = \emptyset$), and non-trivial finite-support orbits $\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}_{(F)}$ possess strictly smaller open stabilizers $\mathcal{G}_{(F')} \subsetneq \mathcal{G}_{(F)}$ with no canonical invariant selector---preventing $1$ from being externally projective whenever $U \subsetneq \mathcal{G}$ exists, and preventing any inhabited object of $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{M}))$ from being internally projective in the absence of a minimal open subgroup.

Calibrating the hierarchy of weak choice principles inside permutation topoi $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$ yields the \emph{Symmetry-Breaking / Support Residuum Theorem}:

\begin{theorem}[The Permutation-Topos Residuum Atlas in $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$]\label{thm:permutation-topos}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.pointwiseStabilizer_lt_top_of_nonempty, HomotopyCRM.isEmpty_globalSections_quotient_of_lt_top, HomotopyCRM.open_normal_eq_top, HomotopyCRM.open_finiteIndex_eq_top, HomotopyCRM.instModuleZMod2EvenFinset, HomotopyCRM.instAddTorsorEvenFinsetTOdd, HomotopyCRM.isEmpty_t_odd_sections_empty, HomotopyCRM.nonempty_t_odd_sections_singleton, HomotopyCRM.atomSection_vsub_card_and_ne_zero}%
\leanok%
\uses{thm:site-dictionary,thm:coshep-svc}%

Let $\mathcal{G} = \mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{M})$ be the topological automorphism group of a Fraïssé structure $\mathcal{M}$ over $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{AC}}$, and let $\mathcal{E} = \mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$ be the atomic Boolean Grothendieck topos of continuous $\mathcal{G}$-sets:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Small Violations of Choice ($\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{SVC}$):} Every Fraïssé permutation topos $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$ unconditionally satisfies Blass's Small Violations of Choice ($\mathrm{SVC}$), because every continuous $\mathcal{G}$-set is a subquotient of $C \times S$ where $C$ is a trivial (choice) $\mathcal{G}$-set and $S = \coprod_{F \subseteq_{\mathrm{fin}} \mathcal{M}} \mathcal{G}/\mathcal{G}_{(F)}$ is the universal object of finite supports.

  \item \textbf{From $\mathrm{SVC}$ to Enough Projectives and External Choice ($\mathrm{SVC} \Rightarrow \mathrm{COSHEP}$ and $\mathrm{AC}$):} By Theorem~\ref{thm:coshep-svc} and Kihara~\cite[Theorems~6.2 and~6.19]{Kihara26}, in any atomic, two-valued permutation topos $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$ (where $\mathrm{Sub}(1) = \{0, 1\}$), external projectivity of $1$ ($\mathrm{EAC}$ / $\mathrm{AC}$) and localicity hold if and only if $\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{G}) = \{\mathcal{G}\}$ ($\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G}) \simeq \mathbf{Set}$), whereas $\mathrm{COSHEP}$ and $\mathrm{IAC}$ hold if and only if $\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{G})$ possesses a minimal open subgroup (achieved by $\{1\} \in \mathcal{U}(G)$ in discrete $\mathbf{Set}^G$, but failing across all non-discrete Fraïssé groups $\mathcal{G} = \mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{M})$ without minimal open stabilizers, where $\mathrm{EAC}$, localicity, $\mathrm{COSHEP}$, $\mathrm{IAC}$, and $\mathrm{AC}$ all collapse to $\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{G}) = \{\mathcal{G}\}$).

  \item \textbf{Finite $k$-Choice ($\mathrm{FAC}_k$) and Ramsey / Amalgamation Symmetries:} For each integer $k \ge 2$, $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$ satisfies Finite $k$-Choice ($\mathrm{FAC}_k$) on constant finite fibers if and only if no open stabilizer subgroup $U \in \mathcal{U}(\mathcal{G})$ admits a continuous transitive action on a $k$-element set---isolating the absence of finite index-$k$ symmetry obstructions $H^1_{\mathrm{cont}}(U, S_k) = 0$ in the Fraïssé automorphism group $\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{M})$.

\end{itemize}

\end{theorem}

\section{The Fraïssé Atomic-Topology Escape from Presheaf Groupoidification}\label{subsec:fraisse-atomic-escape}

Combining the Site-Combinatorial Dictionary (Theorem~\ref{thm:site-dictionary}) with the Fraïssé permutation topoi of Theorem~\ref{thm:permutation-topos} resolves a foundational tension between directed combinatorial embeddings and classical Boolean logic: how can a category $\mathcal{K}$ of finite structures and strict, non-invertible embeddings $A \hookrightarrow B$ generate a Boolean classifying topos without forcing its embeddings to become isomorphisms?

\begin{theorem}[The Atomic-Topology Escape from Left Bousfield Collapse for Fraïssé Classes]\label{thm:fraisse-atomic-escape}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.sap_closed_oriented_poset, HomotopyCRM.steiner_cycle_obstruction, HomotopyCRM.IsFlowReachReflecting.toIsClosedEmbedding, HomotopyCRM.IsFlowConvex.toIsFlowReachReflecting, HomotopyCRM.sap_flowReachReflecting, HomotopyCRM.sap_flowConvex}%
\leanok%
\uses{thm:permutation-topos}%
Let $\mathcal{K}$ be a countable Fraïssé class of finite relational structures and strict embeddings satisfying the Joint Embedding Property ($\mathrm{JEP}$) and the Amalgamation Property ($\mathrm{AP}$), with countable ultrahomogeneous Fraïssé limit $\mathcal{M}$, and write $\mathbb{C} = \mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{op}}$ so that covariant diagrams $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{K}}$ coincide with contravariant presheaves $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}$.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{The Presheaf Groupoidification Obstruction:} By Theorem~\ref{thm:site-dictionary}(3), the bare presheaf topos $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{K}} = \mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{C}^{\mathrm{op}}}$ (with the trivial topology) is Boolean ($\Omega \simeq 1+1$) if and only if $\mathcal{K}$ is a groupoid; forcing classical logic onto $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{K}}$ without a non-trivial Grothendieck topology---or applying groupoidification $\pi_{\le 0}(\mathcal{K})$---inverts all embeddings $A \hookrightarrow B$ into isomorphisms.
  \item \textbf{Amalgamation as the Right Ore Residuum for $\mathrm{WLEM}$:} Under $\mathbb{C} = \mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{op}}$, a cospan $B_1 \to A \leftarrow B_2$ in $\mathbb{C}$ is a span of embeddings $B_1 \hookleftarrow A \hookrightarrow B_2$ in $\mathcal{K}$, so the Fraïssé Amalgamation Property ($\mathrm{AP}$) on $\mathcal{K}$ is equivalent to the Right Ore condition on cospans in $\mathbb{C}$, validating De Morgan's Law ($\mathrm{WLEM}$) in $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{K}}$ by Theorem~\ref{thm:site-dictionary}(1).
  \item \textbf{Atomic Sheafification Preserves Directed Embeddings:} Because $\mathbb{C} = \mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{op}}$ satisfies the Right Ore condition, every non-empty sieve on $\mathbb{C}$ is $\neg\neg$-dense, so $J_{\neg\neg}$ coincides with the atomic Grothendieck topology $J_{\mathrm{at}}$ (generated by single morphisms). Passing to the sheaf subtopos $\mathbf{Sh}(\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{op}}, J_{\mathrm{at}}) \simeq \mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{M}))$~\cite{Caramello18} achieves full Booleanness ($\mathrm{LEM}$) and two-valuedness (via $\mathrm{JEP}$) without inverting the embeddings of $\mathcal{K}$, trading morphism invertibility for the non-localic stabilizer obstructions of Theorem~\ref{thm:permutation-topos}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\section{Kihara's Five-Rung Atomic Choice Spectrum and Nominal Parity Cohomology}\label{subsec:kihara-choice-nominal}

Calibrating internal and external choice principles across Boolean atomic Grothendieck topoi $\mathcal{E} = \mathbf{Sh}(\mathcal{K}_\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}, J_{\mathrm{at}})$ associated to subcanonical amalgamation ($\mathrm{AP}$) classes $\mathcal{K}_\lambda$ parameterized by an infinite regular cardinal $\lambda$ satisfying $\lambda^{<\lambda} = \lambda$ (with $\lambda = \aleph_0$ recovering countable Fra\"iss\'e classes of finite structures), Takayuki Kihara~\cite[Sections~3, 5--8, and Table~1]{Kihara26} establishes a \emph{strict five-rung Choice Spectrum} (charted with its Lean-verified obstructions in Figure~\ref{fig:kihara-choice-spectrum}):
\[
  \mathrm{LEM} \;\subsetneq\; \mathrm{DC}_\omega + \mathrm{AC}_{\le \lambda} \;\subsetneq\; \mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{Set}} \;\subsetneq\; \mathrm{IAC} + \mathrm{COSHEP} \;\subsetneq\; \mathrm{EAC}.
\]
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Rung~0 ($\mathrm{LEM}$ holds; $\mathrm{DC}_\omega + \mathrm{AC}_{\le \lambda}$ fails):} Schanuel's nominal topos $\mathbf{Nom} = \mathbf{Sh}(\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{fin}}^{\mathrm{op}}, J_{\mathrm{at}}) \simeq \mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Sym}(\mathbb{N}))$ validates $\mathrm{LEM}$, $\mathrm{SVC}$, and constant-fiber $\mathrm{FAC}_k$, yet refutes both $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$ and $\mathrm{DC}_\omega$ ($\mathrm{IDC}_\omega$) because $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{fin}}$ has unbounded finite cardinalities (Theorem~7.9); similarly, $\mathbf{Sh}(\mathrm{WO}_\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}, J_{\mathrm{at}})$ validates $\mathrm{LEM} + \mathrm{AC}_\lambda$ (Proposition~7.8) while refuting $\mathrm{DC}_\omega$ via the shift $s(n) = n + 1 : \omega \hookrightarrow \omega$ (Proposition~7.5) and refuting $\mathrm{AC}_{\lambda^+}$ (Proposition~7.7).
  \item \textbf{Rung~1 ($\mathrm{DC}_\omega + \mathrm{AC}_{\le \lambda}$ holds; $\mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{Set}}$ fails):} Unstable classes admitting $\omega$-chain and $\le \lambda$-fan cocones at $\lambda^{<\lambda} = \lambda$---linear orders $\mathrm{LO}_\lambda$, groups $\mathrm{Grp}_\lambda$, Boolean algebras $\mathrm{BA}_\lambda$, and graphs $\mathrm{Graph}_\lambda$---validate $\mathrm{DC}_\omega$ and $\mathrm{AC}_{\le \lambda}$ (Theorems~3.3--3.4), yet refute $\mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{Set}} \equiv \forall \kappa, \mathrm{AC}_\kappa$: under $\lambda$-$\mathrm{TV}$ ($\lambda$-two-valuedness, meaning every non-trivial slice over a $\mathcal{K}_\lambda$-object splits into at most $\lambda$ pieces, ensuring $\mathcal{K}_\lambda$ generates without hyper-split obstructions), $\mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{Set}} \iff \mathrm{AC}_{\lambda^+} \iff$ \emph{Galois $\lambda$-stability} $s(\mathcal{K}; \lambda) = \lambda$, where $s(\mathcal{K}; \lambda)$ denotes the cardinality of the space of Galois $1$-types over a base structure of size $\lambda$ (Theorems~5.6 and~5.10; see also Theorem~6.34 for Polish groups).
  \item \textbf{Rung~2 ($\mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{Set}} + \mathrm{DC}_\omega$ holds; $\mathrm{IAC} + \mathrm{COSHEP}$ fails):} Unbounded Galois-stable ($\omega$-stable) classes---$\mathrm{Set}_\lambda$, $\mathrm{Vec}_{F, \lambda}$, $\mathrm{AbGrp}_\lambda$, and $\mathrm{Mod}(\mathrm{ACF}_0)_\lambda$ ($\lambda \ge \aleph_0$)---validate $\mathrm{DC}_\omega + \mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{Set}}$ (Theorem~5.6, Corollary~7.1), yet lack maximal structures, refuting $\mathrm{COSHEP} \iff \mathrm{AtPAx} \iff \mathrm{IAC} \iff$ \emph{Maximal Extension Property}, where $\mathrm{AtPAx}$ is the Atomic Presentation / Projectivity Axiom asserting that every object is covered by a projective atom (Proposition~6.6 and Theorems~6.2, 6.7, and~6.8).
  \item \textbf{Rung~3 ($\mathrm{IAC} + \mathrm{COSHEP}$ holds; $\mathrm{EAC}$ fails):} Bounded $\mathrm{AP}$ classes with non-rigid maximal structures---$\mathrm{Set}_{\le n}$ ($n \ge 2$, yielding $\mathbf{Set}^{S_n}$) and finite discrete $\mathbf{Set}^G$ ($G \neq \{1\}$)---satisfy Maximal Extension ($\mathrm{IAC} + \mathrm{COSHEP}$) while failing rigidity ($\mathrm{EAC}$ fails by Theorem~6.19).
  \item \textbf{Rung~4 ($\mathrm{EAC}$ and localicity hold):} Maximal Extension plus rigidity of maximal structures ($\mathrm{Aut}(M_i) = \{1\}$, e.g., $\mathrm{Set}_{\le 1}$), collapsing connected $\mathcal{E}$ to $\mathbf{Set}$ ($\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{G}) = \{\mathcal{G}\}$, Theorem~6.19).
\end{itemize}

\begin{center}\emph{[Figure omitted in blueprint edition; see Royal Octavo monograph PDF.]}\label{fig:kihara-choice-spectrum}\end{center}

At Rung~0, the sheaf-cohomological separation between constant and non-constant coefficients in Schanuel's nominal topos $\mathbf{Nom} \simeq \mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Sym}(\mathbb{N}))$ has been machine-checked in Lean~4 (\texttt{HomotopyCRM/ Nominal Simplicity.lean}, 1,275 lines, 4-Layer Gate verified): because every open normal or open finite-index subgroup of $\mathrm{Sym}(\mathbb{N})$ is improper (\texttt{open\_ normal\_ eq\_ top} via explicit block-swap conjugators, \texttt{open\_ finiteIndex\_ eq\_ top}), constant-coefficient Galois cohomology vanishes identically, $H^1(\mathbf{Nom}; \Delta(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})) = \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{cts}}(\mathrm{Sym}(\mathbb{N}), \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}) = 0$. By contrast, equipping finite subsets of $\mathbb{N}$ with symmetric difference $\triangle$ forms the $\mathbb{F}_2$-module sheaf $\mathrm{Finset}_\Delta(\mathbb{N}) = \mathbb{F}_2[\mathbb{A}]$ (\texttt{natCast\_ card\_ symmDiff\_ zmod2}) and yields the short exact sequence of abelian group sheaves in $\mathbf{Nom}$:
\[
  0 \longrightarrow \mathrm{EvenFinset} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Finset}_\Delta(\mathbb{N}) \xrightarrow{\;|\cdot|\bmod 2\;} \Delta(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}) \longrightarrow 0,
\]
locally split over $1$-atom covers $\mathbf{y}(\{a\}) \twoheadrightarrow 1$ by \texttt{atomSection a} $= \{a\}$ (\texttt{nonempty\_ t\_ odd\_ sections\_ singleton}) with odd-cardinality torsor fiber $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}} := (|\cdot|\bmod 2)^{-1}(1)$ (\texttt{T\_ odd}, \texttt{Checks. tOdd AddTorsor Check}). Since no non-empty finite subset of $\mathbb{N}$ is $\mathrm{Sym}(\mathbb{N})$-invariant, $H^0(\mathbf{Nom}; \mathrm{EvenFinset}) = H^0(\mathbf{Nom}; \mathrm{Finset}_\Delta(\mathbb{N})) = \{\emptyset\} = 0$ while $H^0(\mathbf{Nom}; \Delta(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})) = \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}$, so the connecting homomorphism $\delta : \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z} \hookrightarrow H^1(\mathbf{Nom}; \mathrm{EvenFinset})$ injects $1 \mapsto [\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}}] = \delta(1) \neq 0$ (\texttt{isEmpty\_ t\_ odd\_ globalSections}, witnessed by the Čech $1$-cocycle $\{a\} \triangle \{b\} = \{a, b\} \in \mathrm{EvenFinset}$ for $a \neq b$ in \texttt{atomSection\_ vsub\_ card\_ and\_ ne\_ zero} and \texttt{Checks. derived\_ cech\_ cocycle\_ atom Section}).

\section{Cubical $n$-Amalgamation and $2 \times 2$ Orthogonality}\label{subsec:cubical-amalgamation}

While Kihara's Theorem~5.6 equates $\mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{Set}}$ with 1-dimensional Galois fan stability, higher $n$-amalgamation ($n \ge 3$) measures higher-dimensional descent across punctured $n$-cubes $\mathcal{P}^-(n) := \{S \subseteq [n] : S \subsetneq [n]\}$:

\begin{proposition}[Internal Matching Criterion for $n$-Amalgamation]\label{prop:matching-amalgamation}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.PuncturedCube.card_eq, HomotopyCRM.cubical_ndap_closed_oriented_poset, HomotopyCRM.hasCubicalNDAP_iff_isIntersective}%
\leanok%
\uses{thm:fraisse-atomic-escape}%
In $\mathcal{E} = \mathbf{Sh}(\mathcal{K}_\lambda^{\mathrm{op}}, J_{\mathrm{at}})$, a punctured $n$-cube $D : \mathcal{P}^-(n) \to \mathcal{K}_\lambda$ ($n \ge 2$) admits an $n$-amalgamation cocone in $\mathcal{K}_\lambda$ if and only if the canonical projection from the internal matching limit $\mathrm{Match}_n(D) := \varprojlim_{S \in \mathcal{P}^-(n)^{\mathrm{op}}} \mathbf{y}_{D(S)} \to \mathbf{y}_{D(\emptyset)}$ is an epimorphism in $\mathcal{E}$. Moreover, $D$ admits Disjoint $n$-Amalgamation ($n$-$\mathrm{DAP}$) if and only if $\mathrm{Match}_n^{\mathrm{disj}}(D) \twoheadrightarrow \mathbf{y}_{D(\emptyset)}$ is epimorphic, where $\mathrm{Match}_n^{\mathrm{disj}}(D) \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Match}_n(D)$ is the disjoint matching subobject carving out tuples $(j_S)_{S \subsetneq [n]}$ whose images satisfy exact face intersection $j_S(D(S)) \cap  j_T(D(T)) =  j_{S \cap T}(D(S \cap T))$ (matching \texttt{D. Is Intersective} on $\mathbf{Poset}_{\mathrm{og,fin}}^{\mathrm{closed}}$).
\end{proposition}

Consequently, Galois stability ($\mathrm{AC}_{\mathrm{Set}}$) and higher $n$-amalgamation ($n \ge 3$) form an orthogonal $2 \times 2$ classification:

\begin{center}\emph{[Summary ledger table omitted in blueprint edition; see Royal Octavo monograph PDF.]}\end{center}

In directed combinatorial geometry (\texttt{HomotopyCRM/ Directed Fraisse SAP.lean}, 2,125 lines, 4-Layer Gate verified), binary ($n = 2$) Strong Amalgamation via \texttt{Set Pushout Square} holds both on finite oriented graded posets along closed cellular embeddings (\texttt{Oriented Graded Poset}, \texttt{Is Closed Embedding}) and on flow-acyclic complexes (\texttt{IsFlow Acyclic}, using unbiased equal-dimension steps $\dim(u) = \dim(v)$ in \texttt{FlowStep. dim\_ eq} that keep Marcus Nicolas's pinched $2$-cube $P = \square^2 \cup_{\{10, 01\}} \square^1$, $P^{\mathrm{op}}$, and $P^{\mathrm{co}}$ all strictly flow-acyclic, even though $P^{\mathrm{op}}$ breaks strong Steiner partial duality via mixed-dimension chain coupling~\cite{Masuda26}) along two-clause flow-convex embeddings (\texttt{IsFlow Convex}: \texttt{cell\_ mem} + \texttt{inter\_ mem}, strictly separated across three embedding tiers in \texttt{Checks. layer2\_ sep\_ closed\_ not\_ flow Reach Reflecting} and \texttt{Checks. layer2\_ sep\_ flow Reach Reflecting\_ not\_ flow Convex} and closed under 1-sided cobase-change in \texttt{Amalgamated Poset Square. isFlow Reach Reflecting\_ j₁}, \texttt{Amalgamated Poset Square. isFlow Convex\_ j₁}, and \texttt{sap\_ flow Convex} to block \texttt{steiner\_ cycle\_ obstruction}).
